# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Changed

- **The Hooks tab groups Glimmervoid's own hooks by agent.** Under Glimmervoid's own hooks, Claude Code, Codex, Kimi Code and Grok each have their own collapsible group with a hook count. The Codex group names the installed Codex version, since its hook set depends on that version, and Grok is marked best effort. The list is read from the hooks Glimmervoid actually writes for each agent, so it now also shows hooks the old hand-kept list missed, such as the second AskUserQuestion hook for spawned sessions, and each row says what Glimmervoid uses that hook for.
- **Agents learn about whiteboards when you ask for a diagram.** When a Claude Code, Codex or Kimi Code prompt asks for a graph, diagram, chart, flow, map, architecture, layout, sketch or drawing, the agent gets one line with that prompt pointing it at `glimmervoid whiteboard new`, so the diagram lands on a board you can see and edit instead of in the chat. The line appears at most once per conversation, never after the session has used a whiteboard, and never for a session already connected to a board.
- **Agents choose the board layout that fits.** The whiteboard help agents read no longer opens its example with columns or calls free placement a last resort. It lists a column and lane grid, placing a box beside another, and pixel positions as equal choices, so a flow or a state graph is no longer forced into a column grid.
- **Your day tips say what they measured.** The morning tip and the Usage view insights now state the fact and the number instead of a terse slogan. "Sharpest 09:00 to 12:00. Hard work first." now reads "You replied to agents fastest 09:00 to 12:00, 1.8x your usual pace." The waiting, no-break and many-sessions tips read the same way, for example "Replies took 2.5x longer once more than 3 sessions waited on you."
- **Alert sounds play again while you work in the dashboard.** With the dashboard focused, an alert from any session other than the one you are looking at plays your alert sound, once per burst. An alert from the session you are looking at (centered in Focus, open on the phone Terminal screen, or on a board) stays silent and counts as seen, so no desktop notification or Telegram ping follows for it later. With the dashboard in the background, alerts work as before.
- **Boards give the canvas the whole view.** The row of tabs and buttons above a board is gone; its controls now float over the canvas. The drawing tools, note, frame and unit sit in a bar at the bottom center as icons with their shortcut letter, and fit sits bottom left with new undo and redo buttons. The menu button at the top left switches boards and holds new board, board settings (caption, code session and legend) and delete board. Click the board title beside it to rename the board: Enter saves, Escape cancels. With nothing selected, a board shows no properties panel; select an element to edit it as before.
- **The docked session's board status floats on the board.** With a session docked beside a board, the line saying it has read every change (or how many edits are queued for it) and the send to agent button now sit bottom left on the canvas, just above fit, undo and redo, instead of in a strip above the docked card. Send to agent is a small arrow icon pointing at the docked chat.

### Fixed

- **The Shown count no longer covers the session menu button.** When a session has shown files you have not opened, the count beside the Shown tab used to sit on top of the card's menu button. The header now leaves room for it.

## [0.49.0] - 2026-10-31

### Added

- **Zoom into shown files on the card.** In the Shown viewer, pinch on a phone, or use Ctrl or Cmd with the scroll wheel or a trackpad pinch on desktop, to zoom an image, video or HTML page up to 8x around your fingers or pointer. A zoomed page stays clickable and scrollable. A page whose own markup blocks the zoom script, such as one with its own script-blocking security policy, shows no zoom controls and is otherwise unchanged. The rest of the dashboard stays at its normal size. Drag or scroll to move around a zoomed file, and double-tap or double-click an image to switch between fit and 2x; a video keeps its own double-click for fullscreen. Zoom out, Fit and Zoom in buttons sit next to the caption with the current zoom percent beside them. Each file opens fitted, and switching files resets the zoom.
- **A new mark, and status icons that move.** Glimmervoid's mark is now four corner brackets around a small diamond light. It replaces the play triangle in the favicon, the site nav and the loading screen. The favicon keeps the mark when a session needs you and turns its light amber, or red for a failure. The loading screen shows the mark scanning while the dashboard connects, then the mark spins once like a vault dial, the brackets clamp in, the light comes on and the mark bursts away to show the dashboard; it no longer shows a flying animal. Session status icons for the live states are now diamonds: Working is a small solid diamond that sends out one ping for each burst of terminal output and stays lit for the whole turn, Monitoring is an eye that glances left and right, Starting is a ring of brackets that spins up and blurs at top speed, a session that has ended shows a closed outline, and one not started yet shows the unlit brackets. Needs input, Complete, Failed and Idle keep their question mark, check, X and pause bars. When a session changes state its icon now animates to the next one in under half a second, with the color blending between the two, and nothing around the icon moves. With reduced motion on, icons change instantly and the loading screen skips its animation.
- **Desktop tabs show the same icons as the phone.** Every view tab in the desktop header now leads with the line icon the phone bar and More menu use, so Focus, Boards, Settings and the rest look the same on every device. The Glimmervoid name in the header now carries the new diamond mark instead of the play triangle.
- **Boards has its own button on the phone.** The phone bottom bar now reads Focus, Terminal, Boards, More, so a board is one tap away instead of inside More, and More no longer lists it. Every bottom bar button and More menu item now draws a line icon instead of a text symbol, and the Focus icon is now a viewfinder framing a glimmer star.
- **Claude Code, Codex and Kimi Code agents learn Glimmervoid commands when they apply.** A Claude Code session now gets one line next to a tool result naming the Glimmervoid command that fits: `show` after a Write, Edit or shell call names an image, video or HTML file, `spawn` after the agent starts an in-process subagent, `push` after a `git push` fails, `settings` when a call touches `~/.glimmervoid/config.json`, and `review` after `gh pr review`. Each line appears at most once per conversation, never for a command the session already ran, and never for one its limits refuse. Glimmervoid now also receives Claude's Write, Edit, Agent and failed Bash tool calls through its local hook, which adds about 2 ms to each. A Codex session gets the `show` line after a shell command names such a file; Codex's shell hook now runs in the background, so it no longer holds up each shell call, and the line reaches Codex with its next model request. A Kimi Code session, whose tool hooks cannot carry text back, gets the `spawn` line with its next prompt after it starts an in-process subagent.
- **Walk a code review from the board.** A board with a code session shows a Not on the board panel listing the files that session changed that no ref on the board covers; click one to open it in the session's review sidebar. Boxes and units whose refs touch a file the review tab calls worth a look carry an amber marker, and a unit whose status disagrees with the code session changes is outlined amber. Select an element with refs and use open in review in the properties panel to jump to that file in the review sidebar.
- **Plan units carry a status checked against the code.** A unit is planned, building or landed; set it with `glimmervoid whiteboard plan status <board> <unit> <status>` or from the Status field in the properties panel, and `plan show` and the board print it. Glimmervoid checks the mark against the code session's changes: a planned unit whose ref files already changed, or a landed unit whose ref files are unchanged or still uncommitted, comes back as a warning on the agent's put and in `get --layout`. `get --layout` also lists the changed files no ref on the board covers, and the referenced files the review tab calls worth a look.
- **Board elements can point at code.** A box or unit takes up to 8 `refs` (`path`, `path#L12` or `path#L12-L30`), and any line or note takes one `ref`. Refs resolve against the board's code session worktree, which is the session that created the board, or else the first session that connects; switch it from the Code session field in the properties panel. When an agent puts a ref that names a missing file, runs past the end of the file, or leaves the worktree, the put still lands and returns a warning; `glimmervoid whiteboard get --layout` and `render` repeat those warnings. A file outside the worktree is never read. An operator note with a ref names it in the agent's next prompt. Edit refs, line refs and note refs from the properties panel.
- **Shown files open on the session card.** Each card header now has Terminal and Shown tabs. Shown is dimmed until an agent shows a file, and a badge on its corner counts the files that arrived since you last opened it, so the header never shifts when it clears; the same count shows next to the session in the roster. The Shown tab flips the card to a viewer of the newest file, with previous and next arrows, the Left and Right keys, a caption with "N of M", Open full size, and a strip of every shown file oldest to newest. Clicking a chip in the terminal opens the viewer on that file. A new file never switches the card away from the terminal. On a phone only the Shown icon sits in the top bar; tap TERMINAL in the bottom bar to go back to the terminal, and Remove session moves from the X into the ... menu. The Shown button, its dot and the full-screen viewer are gone.
- **Boxes on a board say what a change does to them.** A box can be marked `added` (green dashed outline), `removed` (red dashed, faded, title struck through) or `relabeled`, which shows its old title struck through above the new one; a relabeled box needs its old title in `was`. The legend lists each mark in use. Set them from an agent's put or the Change field in the properties panel. This replaces the `ghost` flag, which boards no longer take.
- **Shown files get small thumbnails.** When an agent shows an image or a video, Glimmervoid now makes a small WebP thumbnail (a video's first frame for videos) and the chip in the terminal loads that instead of the full file, which could be up to 200 MB. A file the thumbnailer cannot read shows its kind label instead. Glimmervoid now depends on `sharp` and `ffmpeg-static` to make them.
- **Merge tells you when it could not push.** When Merge lands a session on main but origin refuses the push, or origin's main moved on in the meantime, a warning now says the work is on your local main and not on origin yet, and what to do next. Before, the Review tab showed the same empty "No changes yet" as a merge that reached origin.
- **Wire boxes by dragging from a dot**: hovering a box on a board shows a dot in the middle of each side. Drag from a dot onto another box to wire the two, with a dashed line following the cursor and the box under it lit up. Dropping on empty space or back on the same box adds nothing, and two boxes already wired that way are not wired again. The **wire** button still wires two selected boxes. Phones show no dots.
- **A properties panel edits what you select on a board.** Select one box, wire, frame or panel and a panel in the top right of the board shows its fields: a box's title, shape, column, lane, decision outputs and styled lines; a wire's label, tone, dashed and return styles, sides and decision output; a frame's title and loop mark; a panel's title and lines. With nothing selected it edits the board's title, caption and legend. Each change saves on its own and can be undone.
- **Draw on a board with tools, and undo your edits.** The board tool bar has select, box, decision, panel and wire tools, also on the keys V, R, D, P and A while the board has focus. Pick box, decision or panel, then click the board to place one where you clicked and type its title; the tool goes back to select after each placement. The wire tool drags from any part of a box to another box. Cmd+Z (Ctrl+Z) undoes your own last board edit from anywhere in the board view, including right after a change in the properties panel or tool bar, and Shift+Cmd+Z (Shift+Ctrl+Z) redoes it, up to 100 steps per board, in order however fast you press; a text field keeps its own text undo. A note brought back by undo keeps who wrote it and who resolved it; if an agent has since changed what you edited and the undo no longer fits, it says so and drops that step. The box button no longer opens a dialog, and the wire button no longer wires two selected boxes: use the wire tool.
- **Box lines have styles, boards get side panels, and the legend explains wires.** A box line can be `code`, a bullet `item`, a `numbered` step (counting through each run) or a `kv` pair with a bold key. A new `panel` element holds side notes such as prior art in a column of its own to the right of the board, and wires route around it. The legend under the board now lists each wire style in use, such as failure paths, optional and return wires, and a board op with `legend` renames an entry or adds one.
- **Boards can draw a decision with named exits.** A box with `shape: "decision"` and a list of `outputs` draws a pointed header with one labeled row per output, and a wire that names `fromOutput` leaves from that row, so each branch of a choice starts at its own exit instead of all wires bunching on one side. A put refuses a wire from an output the decision does not have, and outputs on a plain box.
- **Board wires find their own way around boxes.** Wires on a board now route around every box wherever it sits, not only through the gaps between columns, so a box placed beside another or dragged off the grid still gets clean right-angle wires. A wire can name the side it leaves or enters by (`fromSide`, `toSide`), a `back` wire draws as a dashed return edge that loops around the outside of the flow, and a frame marked `loop` shows a repeat badge. A put now also warns when boxes overlap, a box covers a frame title, a side has more wires than fit, a wire bends more than six times, or a board has more wires than one routing pass can place, in which case the rest are drawn as straight dog-legs.
- **Boards are a grid of columns and lane rows, and boxes can sit off the grid.** Each lane on a board is now its own labeled row band, so a box lands in a column and lane cell instead of every lane sharing one stack. A box can also sit beside another box (`near`, on any side) or at a point, with no column at all. A columns op can carry boxes along with a `move` map when it renames or drops a column, instead of refusing until each box was moved by hand.
- **Agents can see the boards they draw.** `glimmervoid whiteboard get <board> --layout` prints where every box, frame, note and wire landed, with the layout warnings, and `glimmervoid whiteboard render <board> --out board.svg` writes the board to an SVG file drawn the same way the dashboard draws it. `glimmervoid whiteboard put <board> --file ops.json` reads the ops from a file, so a large put no longer has to fit on the command line.
- **Board wires follow a dragged box**: dragging a box used to leave its wires behind until you let go. They now re-route around the board as the box moves, and where they sit when you let go is where they stay. When an agent or another tab changes the board mid-drag, the drag carries on over the new board.
- **Plans on whiteboards**: a session can lay out units of work on any board, in dependency order, with `glimmervoid whiteboard plan add`, `plan after` and `plan show`. A unit is any size, from one ticket to a whole pull request. It can come after other units, and it can link to another board that holds its detail. `plan show` numbers the units in order, and units that can run in parallel share a number, as 2a and 2b. A dependency cycle is refused with the loop it would make. Deleting a linked board unlinks its units and tells the sessions connected to them. The board's free drawing is untouched.
- **Plan band on the board canvas**: a board with units shows them in a band above its free drawing, one column per step, with an arrow for each dependency. The **unit** button adds a unit from the dashboard, optionally linked to another board, and clicking **open linked board** on a unit opens that board.
- **`glimmervoid why`**: point it at a line, as `glimmervoid why src/app.ts:42`, or at a commit sha, and it names the session that made that commit: its name, agent, task, when it committed, and the prompt you gave it just before. Glimmervoid now records each commit a session with its own worktree makes, including the Coherence journal commit made on Merge. The record stays in your Glimmervoid folder and nothing is written into the repository. A commit rebased cleanly or promoted onto main still resolves to its session. A line whose commit no session made says so. If there are no records for the repository at all, for example after moving the checkout or with a different `--config`, it says that instead and shows where it looked. Commits made before this version are not recorded.

- **Session worktrees start with dependencies on macOS.** When a session starts in a worktree without `node_modules`, new or resumed, and the main checkout's `node_modules` matches the worktree's `package-lock.json` or `pnpm-lock.yaml`, Glimmervoid clones it into the worktree before the agent starts, so tools like Coherence work from the first prompt without an install. The clone is copy-on-write on APFS: it takes seconds and almost no disk, and an install inside the worktree never touches the main checkout. Workspace packages and multi-repo workspace members get their own `node_modules` too, except a package whose install cannot be matched to its own lockfile. Nothing is cloned when the install is out of date, it links outside the repository (`npm link` or a `file:` dependency), the checkout is not on APFS, or the worktree is on another volume, and the dashboard header shows a worktree warning saying why. On Linux and Windows, worktrees start without `node_modules`, as before. Turn it off with `worktreeCloneDependencies: false` in config.json.

### Changed

- **Linked pull requests are readable in the session menu.** Link pull request in the session menu is now Pull requests, and its dialog lists each linked pull request on its own row with its status icon, `owner/repo#number`, title and stage, such as Checks failing, instead of a bare `#number` chip. Tap a row to show it in Reviews; the x at its end still unlinks it.
- **Restart sits near the bottom of the session menu.** In the ... menu on a session, Restart now sits second from the bottom in amber, just above Remove session, which is now red, so neither is the first thing under your thumb.
- **Radar on the phone reads as separate cards.** Each error is now its own bordered card with a chevron on the right, so you can tell where one ends and the next begins and that it can be tapped. Tapping one turns its chevron down and opens its details inside the same card, outlined in the accent color, with the full title shown once instead of twice.
- **Phone settings line up across every section.** Switch descriptions and status notes now use the full row width, number fields end at the same edge whatever their unit, and the Telegram test and Delete my data buttons run full width like the rest.
- **Coherence's Structure reading stops failing on this repo.** The pinned `@posthog/coherence` moves from 1.8.0 to 1.12.0. On 1.8.0 one TypeScript server error sank the whole reading, so a session's start and every stop after an edit cold-loaded a 1.6 GB TypeScript server for about a minute and kept nothing. On 1.12.0 the file the server cannot answer is named and the rest of the reading is kept, so an unchanged tree skips the reading and a one-file edit takes about 8 s. It adds `web-tree-sitter` as a development dependency.
- **Phone settings toggles line up and rows stop jumping.** Each switch now sits beside its setting's title instead of on its own line below the description, and the line below it that holds the restore-default button and the save status keeps its space, so a row no longer grows or shrinks after you change it.
- **Session menu items drop their trailing dots.** Restart, Edit repositories, Link pull request and Connect board no longer end in `...`, on desktop and phone.

- **Telemetry counts command hints.** Two new events, `command_hint_shown` and `command_hint_followed`, carry only which Glimmervoid command an agent was pointed at and which kind of agent, never the tool call, so the maintainers can tell which one-time hints actually help. `GLIMMERVOID_TELEMETRY=0` and Settings > Privacy turn them off with the rest.

- **`glimmervoid --help` says when to use each session command.** Every line under Inside a Glimmervoid session now ends with when the command fits, such as `show` after making a screenshot or HTML page you should see, so an agent reading help picks the right command instead of only learning what each one does.

- **The Glimmervoid start line now arrives with the first prompt for every agent.** Claude Code and Codex used to receive it as a launch argument (`--append-system-prompt`, `developer_instructions`), which Glimmervoid skipped whenever your own launch arguments or Codex `config.toml` set one, so those sessions never learned Glimmervoid was there. Every Claude Code, Codex and Kimi Code session now gets it with its first prompt, and again after `/clear`, whatever you set.

- **The open session is quieter on the phone Board**: the row of the session you last opened kept a purple outline that looked like it needed you. On phones it now keeps only its raised background and bold name. The desktop Focus rail is unchanged.
- **Settings is calmer and lines up.** Every control sits in one column on the right, and on/off settings are switches. The page title says whether its settings apply to this browser or this machine, and a row only carries a tag when it differs or when the change waits for new sessions or a restart. Reset shows only on a setting you have changed, and the `#` link only when you hover the row. Every description is now one short sentence, and `docs/configuration.md` follows it. Experimental sections and settings carry an EXPERIMENTAL tag in the menu and on the page, and an experimental page opens with a caution line. Tab order is a compact list: drag a row, or use its up and down arrows. A failed update now says in plain words where it stopped, and that your current version is still running when it is, with the build log behind Show details.
- **Focus rail rows name their linked boards and pull requests.** A session's connected boards show by title and its pull requests by number on their own line under the task, instead of a row of identical "Board" chips mixed in with status, and status like PARKED keeps its own line. The session you have focused lists its links one per line, with each pull request's title and stage and each board's queued edits, up to five, with the rest on hover. Phone Board rows work the same way, with the list on the session you last opened.
- **Usage follows Claude's move to a weekly-only limit.** Anthropic is removing the 5-hour limit from Ultra (formerly Max 20x) in stages. The Usage tab now shows a Claude 5-hour meter only while Claude Code itself still reports one, and no longer estimates one from local transcripts: the "This 5-hour window, Claude" card on the overview is gone, the Overhead 5-hour row and the Usage tab's warning colour follow only the reported window, and the reported weekly window keeps its meter. When one agent hits its limit, the suggestion of another agent to switch to now weighs each agent's tightest reported window, 5-hour or weekly.
- **The Boards view header is one row.** Board tabs, the board's version, connected sessions and the toolbar now share a single line above the canvas instead of three, so the board gets more room. The open board's name shows on its selected tab rather than as a separate heading.

### Fixed

- **A shell session looks like a shell.** In the Focus rail and on the phone Board, a shell session now shows a terminal icon in a muted color, and SHELL in its hover text, in place of the yellow pause icon that made it look like an idle agent session.
- **A removed session no longer lingers on the phone.** A session removed while the phone was asleep or offline now disappears from the dashboard as soon as it reconnects, instead of staying listed until a page refresh.
- **The session menu fits on a phone.** The session actions menu ran off the left edge of a phone screen. Its items now show their names only, without the grey line under each, on desktop too.
- **Rendering a board no longer hides changes from the agent.** `glimmervoid whiteboard render` and `glimmervoid whiteboard get --layout` used to mark the board read without printing what others changed, so an operator edit made just before a render never reached the agent's next `get` or next prompt. Both now leave the board unread.
- **The Review tab's branch line stays current.** It used to read "Base branch: no upstream" when a session's worktree was still being set up as you opened it, and it kept its old ahead and behind counts after a Merge. It now checks again once the worktree is ready and after every Merge.
- **Dropping a file over 15 MB on a session says so.** The upload used to fail with "HTTP 502" when the dashboard reached Glimmervoid through a proxy or port forward, because the server cut the connection before replying. It now replies "file is larger than 15mb" straight away, and `glimmervoid show` over its limit gets its own message instead of a reset. Each refused upload also leaves an `[upload]` line in the server log naming the session, the reason and the size.

## [0.48.3] - 2026-10-30

### Added

- **Dashboard updates for npm installs**: if you installed Glimmervoid with `npm install -g` on macOS or Linux, Settings > Updates now downloads a new version in the background on your channel (Release or Experimental), the same way it already does for a git clone, and the next Restart or stop switches to it. You no longer need a terminal for this. Before switching, the downloaded version must print the right version number and pass `glimmervoid doctor`, and the previous version is kept until the new one is up. If the new version quits on Restart before it starts serving, Glimmervoid puts the previous version back, starts it again, and marks the update failed. This rollback does not happen when systemd runs Glimmervoid, since systemd starts the new version itself. Glimmervoid installs into the folder it is running from, even when your npm is set to install somewhere else, using the npm that comes with the Node it runs on. If it cannot write to that folder or to the installed `glimmervoid` folder inside it (for example, you installed with `sudo`), Settings > Updates says so and shows the command to run. If the download fails, the run is marked failed with npm's output and the running version keeps working. Windows and `npx` runs still show their install command.

### Changed

- **`glimmervoid doctor` exits 1 when node-pty fails to load**, so scripts and the dashboard updater can tell a broken install from a working one.
- **Board wires run at right angles** through channels between columns, not as curves. Wires of one style into the same side of a box merge into one trunk with a single arrowhead. A wire that skips a column or runs backward goes through a gap between boxes, or over the top of them, and never through a box. Labels sit where they touch no line, box or note. When a layout still has a crossing, a wire through a box or a label with no clear spot, the agent's `glimmervoid whiteboard put` reply lists it as a warning, so the agent can reorder its boxes.

## [0.48.2] - 2026-10-30

### Added

- **Set up Community from the Community tab**: with the Community tab on but no PostHog personal API key saved, the tab now shows a setup card instead of the bare message "community.enabled but posthog.apiKey is missing". It walks you through four steps: paste a PostHog personal API key, which for Community needs only the `query:read` scope on that one project (PostHog error monitoring shares the key and needs error tracking read and write), confirm the PostHog project that holds the community questions (2 for PostHog's own), name the research skill the agent follows, and list the Discord channels to watch, with a note on the Discord bot events those channels need. The skill and Discord steps can be skipped. Each step saves straight to your settings and the community lane starts as soon as the key is saved, with no restart. Finish setup shows the question queue.

### Fixed

- **"Also being changed in" works both ways with spawned sessions**: a session started with `glimmervoid spawn` saw its overlap with your session, but your session never saw the overlap with it. Both Review panels now show the collision and its conflict risk.
- **The Review panel stops repeating itself**: with changes not committed yet, the headline says so and the line under Merge no longer restates it. A base branch with no upstream no longer gets its own "no upstream" line; the text is on the headline tooltip and the refresh button beside it.
- **The Reviews desk is easier to scan and drive from the keyboard**: Approve, Comment and Request changes now stay pinned to the bottom of the review pane, and only the review scrolls above them. Each finding has one Post checkbox in place of the Post and Drop pair. A dropped finding is dimmed with its file struck through, and its severity is spelled out next to the diamonds. `N` and `P` move between findings, `Enter` or `Space` opens the one you are on, and `X` still posts or drops it. Open in session and About this PR now sit in the right column, and Earlier review sits below the findings. The review queue packs its rows tighter, drops the "Agent:" prefix, and shows the org tag only for authors outside your org. The button that collapsed the queue is gone: drag the divider to resize it.
- **+ Session stays in the header after a setup card**: opening the Reviews tab while it showed its setup card hid the + Session button on every tab until setup finished. The button now hides only while the first-run checklist on the Focus view or phone shows.
- **Community research skill names are checked**: a skill typed as `/research` or with spaces saved without complaint, but the community lane silently ran without it. Settings now rejects such a name, and the Community setup card drops a leading `/`.
- **Team review gives every source change a full review**: a pull request that changed fewer than 200 lines of source was triaged as small and its reviewer was told so, which invited a shallow pass. A 61-line change that loosened a command allowlist was approved with no comments, though it opened a hole. Now only a pull request that changes nothing but Markdown documentation and tests counts as small. The Small PR model and effort settings now apply only to those pull requests and are renamed to match. Every review now also has to say, for each check the pull request loosens, what that check now lets through, and for each changed shared value, who else reads it. On a first review, a claim in the pull request description that the code contradicts now becomes a finding on the draft instead of a note you only see in the assessment.
- **`glimmervoid whiteboard delete <board>`**: a session deletes a board it created. A board another session made, or one made before this release, stays put, and the operator deletes it from the dashboard.
- **`pair`, `settings`, `agent setup` and `doctor` parse their flags too**: each answers `--help` with its own usage, such as `glimmervoid pair --help` listing `--list`, `--revoke` and `--name`, and `glimmervoid settings get --help` showing that subcommand. An option a command does not have, such as `glimmervoid pair --bogus`, is refused instead of minting a pairing link. The refusal for a word that starts with a dash now says to put it after `--`; quoting it never helped, since the shell drops the quotes.
- **In-session commands parse their flags**: `glimmervoid whiteboard new --help` created a board titled "--help" instead of printing help. Every in-session command, such as `whiteboard`, `spawn`, `send` and `show`, now answers `--help` or `-h` with its own usage, and `whiteboard --help` lists its subcommands. An option the command does not have, such as `whiteboard new --foo`, is refused with its usage and nothing is sent. Text that has to start with a dash goes after `--`, as in `glimmervoid whiteboard new -- --help`. A command given words it does not take, such as `glimmervoid board extra`, is refused too, and an unknown option before a command, such as `glimmervoid --bogus`, no longer starts the server.
- **Kimi Code K3.1 Pro and K3 are priced**: the Usage tab no longer reports "No price" for `kimi-code/k3.1-pro` and `kimi-code/k3` and counts their sessions as free. It now estimates them at Moonshot list price. `kimi-code/kimi-for-coding` (K2.8 Preview) has no published price, so it still shows as unpriced.
- **Board chips look like chips, and a docked session sits beside its board**: a session connected to a whiteboard showed `board: <title>` in its sidebar row as plain text that ran off the row. It now shows a board icon and the word Board, next to its pull request chips. Hovering it shows the board's title and how many edits the session has not read yet. The status key lists the board icon. Opening a board from that chip used to squeeze the session into a strip under the board, with its terminal cut off at the size it had on the Focus view. The session now sits on the left with the board to its right, both use the full height of the view, and the terminal resizes to fit.
- **Boards lay out without overlaps**: a note no longer covers boxes in the next column, because the gap after a column widens to fit its notes. A frame no longer cuts the box above it or climbs into the column header. Long column titles wrap inside their column instead of running into the next header. Fit now centers the board left to right instead of pinning it to the left edge.

## [0.48.1] - 2026-10-30

### Added

- **Set up Reviews from the Reviews tab**: with Team review on but no GitHub teams chosen, the Reviews tab, on desktop and phone, now shows a setup card instead of an empty queue that read "all clear". It checks that the GitHub CLI is signed in, showing your login or the `gh auth login` command with Check again. It then lists your GitHub teams as checkboxes, with a box to type `org/team-slug` for any team it cannot list. Save teams starts Team review and My PRs straight away.
- **See who is in your GitHub org**: on the Reviews tab, each pull request's author now carries a tag in the queue and in the review header: the org name, such as PostHog, when the author is a member of the org that owns the repository, or external when they are not. Private org members count as members. The issue detail on the Issues tab shows the same tag after "opened by".
- **Pick a review skill from the Reviews tab**: until you choose which skill Team review runs, a one-line notice above the review queue offers your reviewer agent's skills, or No skill, with Save skill.
- **Usage tab opens on your pace**: the Usage tab now leads with one sentence saying whether any plan limit runs out before it resets, then each agent's 5-hour and weekly limit as a bar with a tick for how much of the window has gone, a hatched projection of where it ends at this pace, and the reset as a clock time such as "resets 08:09" instead of "resets in 161h 30m". Spend, Models, Sessions, History, Overhead and Budgets open as their own screens, beside the overview on desktop and behind Back on a phone. The Report screen switches between Today, Last 7 days and Last 30 days, compares the total with the period before, draws daily bars against the average, and ranks models and sessions by cost; tap a model for its input, output and cache tokens.

### Changed

- **Shorter Will post to GitHub box**: a bare approval now reads "At 111bb69." and the note field is labeled Note with an Optional placeholder.
- **Issues tab follows each issue from triage to close**: a rail across the top shows how many issues sit in Triage, Shape, Running, Review and Close, and picking a step narrows the list to it. The list now puts what needs you first: issues whose session waits on you, then failed sessions, merged pull requests whose issue is still open, pull requests in review, working sessions, issues assigned to you, and everything else, longest wait first. Issues assigned to you skip Triage. Each issue's detail shows where it stands and the one action that step needs, such as **Open in Reviews** for a pull request under review, or **Close issue** once its pull request has merged, which closes it on GitHub as completed without a comment. **Reopen** stays on the closed issue until you pick another one. The scope tabs, sort menu and Filters drawer are gone: type `is:mine`, `is:team`, `is:project`, `is:close` or any step, `repo:`, `label:`, `has:session` or `has:pr` in the search box instead. J and K move through the list, O opens the issue's session, and Escape goes back to the list, as on the Reviews tab.
- **Radar is one triage queue**: the Radar tab now lists live issues and unarchived investigations together in three tiers. NOW holds fixes waiting for your approval and calls only you can make, NEXT holds running investigations, failed runs and new or worsening issues nobody has looked at, and LATER holds quiet issues and explained ones waiting to be archived. An issue and its investigation share one row, headed by what the investigation found rather than the exception name, with a four-step bar showing how far it got (seen, investigating, verdict, fix staged). Select a row to see its detail pane with every action, the most useful one first, and a strip of the last 14 days of occurrences with the days it was investigated marked. Press J and K or the arrow keys to move, Enter for the first action and E to archive. **Archive transient** clears every transient investigation in LATER at once. Quiet projects, poll failures and stale projects move to one line at the bottom. On a phone the detail opens under the row you tap.

- **Check by hand folds long file lists**: on the Reviews tab, a Check by hand item that names more than 6 files now shows one line such as "84 files in 6 folders" instead of a wall of paths. Open it to see the files grouped by folder, with the folders they all share trimmed off the front.

### Removed

- **Usage largest-block meter**: the Usage tab no longer shows the estimated "largest block" meter, which competed with the official plan limit and read as one. Sort controls on the model and session tables and the token mix bar are gone with the tables.
- **Your day after-22:00 tip**: Your day no longer shows the "time after 22:00" tip, whose wording read as a clock time rather than how long you worked late.

### Fixed

- **Team reviews start again**: since 0.48.1-next.303 every team review failed three times within seconds and wrote no report, because Claude read the review prompt as one more blocked-tool rule and started with no prompt. Claude sessions now always receive their prompt as the prompt, so team review and the Community lane run again. PRs whose review failed need re-queueing.
- **Unpushed base no longer reads as diverged**: a session started while your local base branch held commits not yet pushed to origin now warns that the base is ahead of origin (unpushed commits) instead of saying it has diverged. A base that really forked from origin still says diverged.
- **Usage ranges are calendar days**: Today, Last 7 days and Last 30 days now cover whole local days, today plus the days before it, so a period's total matches the sum of its daily bars instead of counting a rolling window that started mid-morning.
- **Phone dialogs stay above the keyboard**: on a phone, dialogs such as New board now open at the top of the screen instead of the middle, so the on-screen keyboard no longer covers the text box and its buttons.
- **Trace shows task notifications as notifications**: a background task finishing no longer appears in Trace as a prompt you typed, now that Claude Code records those turns in a newer format.
- **Your day times match your day**: Your day no longer counts prompts you did not type, such as Claude Code SDK sessions running on a timer, background task notifications and peer messages, so stretches end when you take a break and your reply hours, sharpest hours and the break tip come from your own prompts. The longest-stretch tip now says to break every 90 minutes instead of naming a clock time taken from yesterday, and a sharpest window that crosses midnight now counts the hours after midnight it names.

## [0.48.0] - 2026-10-29

### Added

- **Whiteboards you and your sessions edit together**: the new Boards tab holds boards of boxes in columns, wires between them, frames around groups and sticky notes, drawn in Comic Mono. Create a board, add and drag boxes, wire two of them, pin a note or frame a group, and every change shows up live in every open dashboard. A session works the same board with `glimmervoid whiteboard` (`new`, `list`, `connect`, `get`, `put`, `resolve`), and `get` tells it in one line per element what someone else changed since it last looked. Boards live in `~/.glimmervoid/whiteboards/` and survive restarts; deleting one tells every connected session it is gone. Connect a session to a board from its card menu with **Connect board...**; the session's row then carries a `board:` chip, and clicking it opens the board with that session's card docked beneath it, so you can watch and steer the agent while the board changes. A connected session hears about other people's edits without asking: the chip and the dock show how many are queued, your next prompt to that session carries a one-line summary of each, and **send to agent** in the dock tells an idle agent to read the board. On a phone the board pans and pinches, and tapping a box pins a note on it.
- **Lanes read PostHog through your own login**: when your Claude Code, Codex or Kimi Code has the PostHog MCP server connected (`https://mcp.posthog.com/mcp`), the lanes Glimmervoid runs on that CLI, such as Reviews and Community, can look things up and run read queries in PostHog with that login, with no key to set up. Every call passes a read-only check first: creating, changing or deleting anything, switching project or organization, and any other PostHog tool are refused before they reach PostHog. Your own sessions are unchanged. With no PostHog server connected, lanes run as before. A Codex or Kimi Code lane given PostHog loads none of your other MCP servers for that CLI, and Codex needs 0.165.0 or later.
- **Sessions read a review in full**: `glimmervoid reviews <pr>` (a pull request url or `owner/repo#number`) gives a session the whole Reviews tab draft as JSON: verdict, summary, body, assessment, every inline comment with its severity and diff excerpt, GitHub threads and the earlier review. A review still queued or running reports its state instead, and a pull request not in the Reviews tab says to queue one with `glimmervoid review <pr>`. Ask a chat to run it instead of pasting the review in. `glimmervoid reviews` with no pull request lists the tab as before.
- **Your day on the Usage tab**: once you have three days of Claude Code or Codex sessions, the Usage tab opens with Your day: how many sessions you prompted yesterday and for how long, plus up to three one-line tips drawn from how yesterday compared with your last two weeks, such as how many waiting sessions you handle before your replies slow down, the hours you reply fastest, your longest stretch without a 15 minute break, time after 22:00, and bouncing between five or more sessions in half an hour. Only sessions you prompted count, so a session left open all week adds nothing, and lanes and team reviews are left out. The top tip also flies in with an animal on your first dashboard visit after 04:00, and when more sessions are waiting than you usually juggle while you are on a longer stretch than usual, an animal suggests a break, at most once every three hours; Nah keeps that one quiet until tomorrow. Turn it off with Your day in Usage settings.

### Changed

- **Worth a look groups files by what they share**: files also being changed in the same other session now sit under one "Also being changed in" heading, and files that usually change with the same missing file sit under one "Usually changes with" heading, instead of one line per file repeating the same sentence. The list shows five groups before Show all, and clicking a file still opens it below. Each "Also being changed in" group now says how likely a merge conflict is: Conflict risk High when the two sessions edit the same lines of a file or lines within three of each other, or both create it; Low when their edits are further apart; Medium when the edits cannot be compared, such as a binary file. Hover a file for the lines behind it. Only the shared files are compared, and only when another session shares one, so a change with no overlap costs nothing extra.
- **A turn you watch finish shows its checkmark first**: when a session finishes while you are typing in its terminal, its card now shows Complete for a moment and then goes to Idle, instead of jumping straight to Idle so you never see that it finished.
- **Codex sessions get status hooks and Sane YOLO without the trust bypass**: Codex runs hooks from your Codex home only once they are trusted, so Glimmervoid now starts every local Codex session from a generated Codex home under `~/.glimmervoid/agent-homes/codex`: your own `config.toml` plus Glimmervoid's hooks, trusted for exactly those hooks, with everything else linked back to your real Codex home, which Glimmervoid never writes. Status and Sane YOLO now work on a default Codex card without `codexBypassHookTrust`. Hooks a repository ships stay untrusted. A `config.toml` that defines hooks as a `[hooks]` table refuses the spawn with the reason, and a Codex that cannot list its hooks runs without Sane YOLO, with one log line saying so. Trust you grant from inside a Glimmervoid Codex session is kept in the generated home and dropped when the last one ends.
- **A session you add is focused right away**: adding a session from the Add session dialog, the first-run checklist, or a project group's + on the focus rail or phone board now opens the new session and puts the cursor in its terminal, as Cmd+Option+0 already did. Sessions added from another tab, the `glimmervoid` CLI or a lane still leave your focus where it is.
- **Unreadable folders moved to Sane YOLO**: the Unreadable folders setting is now `saneYoloUnreadablePaths`, and Sane YOLO refuses an agent's reads of those folders in every session its agent supports, alongside the built-in credential list. An existing `guardrails.unreadablePaths` list moves over on the next start, with one log line saying so; entries that are neither absolute nor start with `~/` are dropped.
- **Sane YOLO now guards every session and blocks credential reads**: the Sane YOLO hook now runs in every session and lane its agent supports, not only in sessions that skip permission prompts, so a session that still asks you before a command gets `git reset --hard` and similar commands refused with a reason instead of a prompt. It now also catches reads of secrets that name the file: SSH keys, `.env` files, `~/.aws`, cloud and Kubernetes configs, the GitHub CLI login, `.netrc`, `.npmrc`, `.git-credentials` and every coding CLI's own login, through a shell command or a file tool such as Read, Glob or Grep. It is a check on the agent's commands, not a sandbox: a wildcard or a path the command builds at run time can still reach a secret. Turn Sane YOLO off in Settings, or with `saneYolo: false`, to drop both, except in a project whose own Sane YOLO setting is on.
- **Tab tips only suggest what is off**: the animal tip now points only at a feature you have not turned on, so Reviews and Usage, which are on unless you switched them off, no longer get one. A tip waiting for you is dropped once you turn its feature on, and Show me always opens that feature's switch in Settings. Experimental features such as Community and Issues never get a tip.
- **Community research no longer fetches web pages**: the Community lane keeps web search but can no longer open a page with WebFetch, so customer data it reads in PostHog has no easy way out. The `community.mcpTools` setting is gone: PostHog now reaches the lane through your own login (see Added), and other MCP tools are no longer offered.
- **`glimmervoid limits` prints JSON**: inside a session, `glimmervoid limits` now prints its JSON reply like every other session command, instead of text lines. `glimmervoid --help` still lists the session's limits as text. Every session command's reply is now checked against its contract before it is sent; one that breaks it comes back as `{"ok":false}` with a 500 and a `[agent-api]` line in the server log.
- **Tab tips count only Glimmervoid sessions**: the tip that points you at a tab now counts the prompts and Bash commands of the sessions Glimmervoid runs, read from the hooks those sessions already send, so no new hook slows them down. It no longer reads every Claude Code, Codex and Kimi Code transcript on this machine or your shell history, so work you do outside Glimmervoid no longer counts toward a tip. Kimi Code sessions, and Codex versions without the Bash hook, count prompts only. Your saved `feature-hints.json` carries over unchanged.

### Removed

- **Sessions no longer run inside guardrails**: Claude Code and Kimi Code sessions you start now launch directly, without the OS sandbox that blocked writes to credentials and Glimmervoid's own state. Sane YOLO is what catches risky commands and credential reads now, in every session its agent supports. The GUARDRAILS badge and its release and restore toggle on the session card, the Run without guardrails checkbox in the Add session dialog, and the Unguarded repositories and Blocked folders settings are gone; the settings section is now Sane YOLO and unattended. `glimmervoid limits` no longer reports `guarded` or dead ends, and a session's boundary is `none` or `workspace`. A leftover `guardrails` block, or `unguarded` on a project, in `config.json` is ignored with one log line at startup. A session can now spawn a child of any agent.
- **Most lanes no longer run inside guardrails**: Community, Radar, Keep mergeable, Workflows, Benchmarks and the task title and judge lanes now launch without the OS sandbox and without the Claude Code settings sandbox benchmark subjects carried. Every lane instead refuses to start unless the Sane YOLO hook is in place, starts with SSH agent, Git prompt and GitHub token variables emptied, and a Codex lane always runs in Codex's read-only sandbox. These lanes now also run on Windows. A Kimi Code lane given PostHog gets its own throwaway Kimi Code home holding only the PostHog server and the read-only check.
- **Reviews no longer run inside guardrails**: a team review now launches without the OS sandbox, under the same rules as the other lanes: Sane YOLO is required and catches credential and `.env` reads, the SSH agent and GitHub token variables start empty, and reviews now run on Windows. The Review sandbox readout and the Extra allowed domains and Open network egress settings are gone, and a leftover `teamReview.sandbox.extraDomains` or `openNetwork` in `config.json` is ignored. Load my MCP servers stays.
- **The guardrails engine is gone**: the install no longer pulls in `@anthropic-ai/sandbox-runtime`, and on Linux it no longer needs `bubblewrap`, `socat` or `ripgrep`. `glimmervoid doctor` drops its Guardrails section and the install drops its guardrails notice. Coherence readings now run directly instead of inside the session's sandbox. On the first start, Glimmervoid deletes the leftover `~/.glimmervoid/guardrails-homes` and `guardrails-policies` folders, after copying back any agent login a sandboxed session refreshed and removing the empty placeholder files a crashed sandboxed session left in a worktree; while a Glimmervoid still on an older version runs on this machine, it keeps them until a later start. Per-session temp folders stay where they were and are still cleared when their session ends.
- **Visions is gone**: the Visions tab, the editor relay, the `glimmervoid visions` commands and the machine context ingest that fed them are removed, along with their settings. A `visions` or `ingest` block left in `config.json` is ignored, so nothing needs editing there. Remove the editor pieces Visions installed by hand: uninstall the Glimmervoid Visions extension from VS Code (and Cursor or any other VS Code family editor), delete `glimmervoid-visions.lua` from your Neovim config, drop the `[language-server.glimmervoid-visions]` block from Helix's `languages.toml`, and remove the `glimmervoid-visions` server entry from Kate's `kate/lspclient/settings.json`, Sublime Text's `LSP.sublime-settings` and your Emacs init file. The install also no longer pulls in `@parcel/watcher`.

### Fixed

- **Clearing a Kimi Code session resets its card**: `/clear` in Kimi Code now drops the old conversation's task title, pending question kind and compaction state from the card, as `/clear` already does in Claude Code and Codex.
- **Codex plans wait for your approval**: when Codex in plan mode proposes a plan, its card now shows Plan ready and asks for your input, as Claude Code and Kimi Code do, instead of reporting the turn as finished. Approving the plan runs it as a new turn. Glimmervoid reads this from the Codex session log on your machine, so a Codex session running on a remote host still reports the plan as a finished turn.
- **Community finds Discord channels with an emoji in their name**: a Discord channel whose name starts with an emoji or other symbol, such as the PostHog server's bug and feature channels, now matches its plain name in Settings > Community (experimental), so `report-a-bug` picks up messages again. Before, no message from such a channel ever reached the Community tab.
- **Removing a session that needs input asks first**: closing a session whose agent is waiting for your answer (Needs input) now shows the Remove dialog, as closing a working session does, instead of stopping the agent mid-task with no warning.
- **Restart works after a Node upgrade**: when Homebrew (or another package manager) upgraded Node while Glimmervoid was running, the dashboard Restart button stopped the server and never brought it back, with nothing in the log. The replacement server now starts through the `node` on your PATH that the server booted from, such as `/opt/homebrew/bin/node`, so it follows the upgrade. If even that Node is gone, the server log says so with a `[lifecycle]` line and the process exits 1.

## [0.47.0] - 2026-10-29

### Fixed

- **Telegram waits for input left sitting**: a session alert no longer reaches Telegram the moment it happens when no dashboard is open. Telegram now hears only about alerts you leave unanswered for the phone escalation delay (`phoneEscalationMs`, 5 minutes by default), whether or not a dashboard is open. Escalations that land within the grouping window arrive as one message.
- **Spawned sessions stop chiming**: the dashboard chime now plays only for alerts the server decides to send, so sessions a lead spawns and the machine shell stay silent, as their notifications already were. No sound plays while the dashboard window is focused, and a burst of alerts from several sessions shows one desktop notification that collects them, closing once you look at any of those sessions.
- **Alerts that land together sound once**: alerts from any sessions within `notifyDebounceMs` (3 seconds by default) of the first one now fold into one alert instead of one per session. `notifyDebounceMs` used to drop a repeat of the same alert from the same session; it is now this grouping window, and existing values keep working.
- **Codex commands show in the activity stream again**: current Codex versions run shell commands through an `exec` tool call, which the activity stream skipped, so a Codex session's commands never appeared there. Each command that call runs now shows as its own `exec_command` line, and one the call builds at run time shows as `exec_command` without its text. Codex `exec_command` and `shell` function calls now show the command they ran, too. Your prompts to an agent are still never published to the stream.
- **Debug state and Trace moved into the session menu**: with debug mode on, a session's Debug state and Trace now sit in its `...` Session actions menu instead of as two extra icons in the card header, on desktop and on your phone. The phone Terminal top bar gets the room back for the title, session name and safety badges.
- **Safety badges show on your phone**: the phone Terminal screen now shows the session's YOLO or Sane YOLO badge and its Guardrails badge next to its status. Tap a badge to read what it means; tap Guardrails to release or restore them, as on desktop.
- **The review desk fills a wide screen and shows its keys**: on a wide Reviews pane the pull request column now stretches to the edge instead of stopping partway and leaving an empty band, the right-hand panel grows up to 420px, and the Your note box starts about four lines tall. Approve, Request changes and Comment now show their keys (A, R, C), Open in session shows O, and the Findings heading shows N and P to move between findings and X to post or drop the current one.
- **Answering an agent's question clears Needs input right away**: after you answer a Claude Code question (AskUserQuestion) or a Codex question (request_user_input, Codex 0.163.0 or newer), the card now leaves Needs input the moment the answer is submitted. Before, it waited for the terminal title to start spinning again, which could take half a minute or never happen while the agent ran a command, leaving the card on `?` until you dismissed it. Sessions pick this up on their next start.
- **Card safety icons now agree on color**: the Sane YOLO badge is now plain like the Guardrails on badge, since both mean a safety net is active. Orange now marks only a session without one: plain YOLO, or guardrails released.
- **A restored dashboard tab no longer shows an empty board**: when your browser reopens a tab from its cache (say after a restart) holding a dashboard older than the running server, the dashboard now notices on connect and reloads itself onto the current version, instead of showing no sessions until you refresh. A tab already open on an older dashboard needs one manual refresh to pick this up.
- **Adding a session no longer fails with "already exists"**: when the name you add a session under is already taken by a running session, including one the dashboard is not showing, Glimmervoid now adds it as `name (2)`, `name (3)` and so on. The same goes for sessions opened from a GitHub issue, a pull request review or a Radar issue. Renaming a session to a taken name is still refused. A refused add now leaves a line in the server log with the name, folder and reason.
- **Dashboard errors are no longer lost**: every error the dashboard shows in its red header line is now also written to the server log, as `[dashboard] header error: <source>: <message>`. An error raised while the dashboard is disconnected is held in the page and written once it reconnects, so connection trouble no longer leaves gaps. Log lines the dashboard sends the server for other reasons are held the same way. Closing the tab before it reconnects still drops what it held. A page sends at most 32 distinct lines, then one line saying the limit was reached. With telemetry on, each distinct header error is also sent to PostHog once per page load as `header_error_shown`, carrying the part of the dashboard that raised it and the message text, cut to 512 characters, with your home folder shown as `~`. This changes the telemetry promise: header error text can name a session, folder or branch, and Settings and the configuration reference now say so. Turn telemetry off to keep it local.

### Added

- **A Community tab drafts answers to community questions**: turn on Settings > Community (experimental) > Show the Community tab and Glimmervoid watches new questions on posthog.com/questions, plus the Discord channels you list under Discord channels, using your Radar PostHog key. Discord messages are first sorted by a quick check with a small model, so only messages that ask a question reach the tab. Each new question runs your Research skill in a locked-down lane that can read public sources and run read-only `gh` searches, then shows its confidence, what it checked, its gaps and a draft reply. Glimmervoid never posts anything: copy the draft, open the thread, start a session on the question, re-run the research or set the question aside. Questions and drafts survive a restart, and only questions asked after you turn the tab on are researched. A finished High confidence draft sends a browser notification, and a Telegram one when Telegram notifications are on and no dashboard is open; every other finish only marks the tab.

- **A plain terminal on the machine Glimmervoid runs on**: Add session now offers Shell next to your agents. It opens your login shell (`$SHELL`, else `/bin/sh`; `COMSPEC` on Windows) on the Glimmervoid machine, so when Glimmervoid runs on a remote host such as a Coder devbox you get a terminal there from the dashboard or a paired phone, with no SSH. A Shell session runs right in the folder you give it, or your home folder when Path is blank, and never makes a worktree. It always runs without guardrails or Sane YOLO, since you type every command yourself. Its card never shows Working or Needs Input and never notifies you, including when the shell exits. After a Glimmervoid restart the card stays in the list but does not start on its own; starting it again opens a fresh shell. Shell does not count as an agent in the first-run checklist: with no agent installed, Agent CLI stays missing and offers Open shell, so you can install one from a terminal on that machine. A `customAgents` entry with the id `shell` now collides with the built-in Shell, so rename it before upgrading or Glimmervoid refuses to load `config.json`.
- **Team reviews run on your own setup**: Settings > Team review now lets you pick the Review skill from the user and plugin skills your review agent already loads, or type one by name; turning team review on asks you to pick one, and No skill is a valid choice. Review agent picks Claude Code, Codex CLI or Kimi Code, and Review model and Review effort suggest what that agent's own config offers (Kimi Code has no effort setting). Small PR agent, model and effort set the same for small pull requests, or follow the review agent. Replies to the review's threads are judged by the same agent. Reviews no longer pin Opus for full reviews and Sonnet for small ones: with the model and effort left empty, the agent uses its own defaults, so set Review model to `opus` and Small PR model to `sonnet` to keep the old choice. An existing config with no skill set reviews with no skill.
- **See and loosen the review sandbox**: a Review sandbox readout in Settings shows what the review agent can reach: its network allowlist, whether MCP servers load, which home and checkout folders it cannot read, that the GitHub token is removed, and which tools it may use. The defaults are unchanged. Extra allowed domains adds domains to the allowlist, Open network egress drops the allowlist entirely, and Load my MCP servers gives the review agent your own MCP servers; each warns that the review runs code from the pull request under review. Codex CLI and Kimi Code always load the MCP servers their own config names, so they review, and judge replies, only with Load my MCP servers on. They also have no tool allow or deny list, so the sandbox is the only limit on what they run, including when judging replies, where Claude Code gets only Read and Write.
- **A tip when a tab could do the work for you**: when you or your agents keep doing work a dashboard tab already handles, an animal flies in at the bottom right with a one-time tip. Reviewing three or more pull requests in a week points at Reviews; the same goes for GitHub issues (Issues), token and rate limit checks (Usage), production error hunts (Radar), pointing agents at editor files (Visions) and comparing agents by hand (Bench). Signals come from the commands and prompts in every Claude Code, Codex and Kimi Code session on this machine, including ones you started outside Glimmervoid, and from your own shell history. Only new lines are read, even with the ingest lane off, and sessions Glimmervoid's own lanes start in the background never count. Commands a Claude Code subagent runs count toward its session too, and they show in the activity stream under that session. Show me opens the tab, or its switch in Settings when the feature is off; Nah silences that tip for good. A tab you opened in the last 14 days never gets one. Glimmervoid keeps the counts in `feature-hints.json` beside `config.json`, and with telemetry on it sends PostHog when a tip is shown, opened or dismissed, keyed by feature. The animal shows even with flying animals off, and fades in without flying when your system asks for reduced motion.
- **Kimi Code sessions**: Glimmervoid can now run Kimi Code (the `kimi` CLI) as a session agent, alongside Claude Code and Codex. There is nothing to set up, and Glimmervoid never edits your `~/.kimi-code/config.toml` (or `$KIMI_CODE_HOME/config.toml`): each kimi session starts on a home Glimmervoid builds in `~/.glimmervoid/agent-homes/kimi`, whose config is yours plus Glimmervoid's hooks and whose other files and folders point back at your kimi home, so your login, trusted folders and sessions are shared with plain kimi. Model changes you make inside a session are kept until the last kimi session exits, and that config copy, which can hold your API keys, is deleted then. `glimmervoid doctor` shows the home under `kimi home`. A kimi card takes kimi's own session title (your first prompt, then the title kimi generates after the first turn, or one you set with `/title`) at full length, and shows working, needs input (permission prompts and AskUserQuestion) and complete, and a subagent's own finish no longer marks the card complete early. Kimi asks whether to trust a folder the first time you open it, and Glimmervoid never answers that for you. In a folder kimi already trusts, Glimmervoid types the session's first prompt in for you; a new session worktree is not trusted yet, so there you answer the trust question in the terminal and type the first prompt yourself. Sane YOLO works on kimi through CC Safety Net. Kimi sessions run inside guardrails by default, like Claude Code sessions, with the same opt-outs (mark the session or its repository unguarded): a guarded kimi session can use its own kimi home, login and the Glimmervoid-built home, while guarded Claude Code sessions still cannot read kimi's home folder or login files. Kimi token use shows on the Usage page (at $0, since Glimmervoid has no price for kimi's models yet). When you are signed in to kimi with `/login`, the Usage page's Plan limits also show your kimi 5 hour and 7 day windows: Glimmervoid reads your kimi login without ever refreshing or changing it and asks kimi for them every 15 minutes while a kimi session runs, and again when you open the Usage page; if kimi refuses (an expired login, or an API key instead of `/login`), the last windows stay up with their age. Kimi replies and tool calls also feed the activity stream. If you ran the old `glimmervoid agent setup kimi` during development, the block it left in your config.toml is ignored and you can delete it.
- **Shown HTML pages play right in the viewer**: when a session runs `glimmervoid show` on an HTML page, the viewer now shows the page itself, live and clickable, filling the box on desktop and on a phone, instead of a card with an Open page button. Open full size still opens it in its own tab. The page stays walled off from the dashboard: it can run its own scripts and forms, but cannot read the dashboard, pop up alerts, or use the camera, microphone or location.
- **The website's pixel animals take off**: on glimmervoid.dev, tapping one of the pixel animal tiles sends that animal flying across the screen, as often as you like. Nothing happens when your system asks for reduced motion.

### Removed

- **rtk output compression is gone**: Glimmervoid no longer installs rtk, no longer rewrites agent Bash commands through it, and drops the rtk setting, the rtk compression tile in the Usage view, and the rtk line in `glimmervoid doctor`. Testing showed the compressed output cost agents more than it saved: Claude Code reran most commands raw, and Codex gave wrong answers from cut-down `head` and `tsc` output. An old `rtk` or `usage.rtkSavings` key in `config.json` is ignored, so there is nothing to change. Glimmervoid no longer uses `~/.glimmervoid/bin/rtk` or the rtk data folder (`~/Library/Application Support/rtk` on macOS, `rtk` under `$XDG_DATA_HOME` or `~/.local/share` on Linux), and you can delete both by hand. Sane YOLO still looks through an `rtk` prefix, so a leftover rtk binary cannot slip a blocked command such as `rtk terraform destroy` past it. Sessions running when you upgrade keep rtk until they restart.
- **Worth a look no longer flags untested files**: the review panel stops listing "No test reaches it", and the change map no longer works that fact out. "Usually changes with", other sessions changing a file, and package links into another repository still show.
- **The Conversation view is gone**: the experimental Conversation face, its "Conversation view" card menu item and its Settings toggle no longer exist, so a session card shows its terminal or its plan. Claude Code sessions no longer get the hooks that held every permission prompt for the dashboard, and a local Codex session no longer starts its own app-server. A `conversationView` key left in `config.json` is ignored. A child session's questions still reach its parent as before, and Codex in a Coder workspace runs as before.

### Changed

- **Quieter empty Changes panel**: a session with no changes yet shows just "No changes yet" instead of a sentence about what will appear there, and the panel header no longer repeats the session name next to Review.
- **One type scale across the dashboard**: dashboard text now uses four sizes (12, 14, 16 and 20px) instead of six, each tied to a role: labels and metadata at 12px, body text at 14px, titles and phone form fields at 16px, and the header title and big stat numbers at 20px. The smallest 10px and 11px text grows to 12px, so dense rows read more easily but truncate a little sooner. Lowercase text drops its letter spacing so CommitMono's smart kerning evens out the gaps; only uppercase labels and headings stay tracked.
- **Visions is now experimental**: Visions and its machine context ingest only appear on the Experimental update channel, as Issues, Community and Coherence do. On a release build the Visions settings are hidden and Visions stays off, even if you turned it on before.
- **Feature tips never point to experimental features**: the owl tip card no longer suggests Issues or Visions, or any other feature that is still experimental, on any build.
- **Reviews sorts by what to do next, without tabs**: the All, Reviewing and Mine tabs are gone. Your pull requests and your teammates' now share one queue ordered by priority, and each row in Your turn leads with what to do: Fix, Review, Reply, Update or Merge. Fix comes first and covers a keep-mergeable repair that failed and an agent review that errored. A keep-mergeable repair still running sits under Agent working. Failing checks and merge conflicts on your own pull requests now sit under Update, below reviews and replies. Within each group, older pull requests come first. The attention banner and the next-attention key follow the same order.
- **Stacked pull requests show where they sit**: in Reviews, a pull request of yours that builds on another now shows a small stack badge such as `2/3` (its level in the stack, then the stack's height) instead of the longer "Stacked on" label. Pull requests built on the same base share a level. Hover the badge to see which pull request it builds on. Opening one also shows a Stack section under its heading that lists every pull request in its stack, indented by level, with each one's state; click another to open it.
- **Worth a look sits under the changed files**: the review panel now lists the changed files first and the Worth a look list after them, so the files are the first thing you see.
- **Reviews session pills rest while hidden**: the session pills on your own pull requests no longer repaint while the Reviews view is in the background, and catch up the moment you open it. While it is open, a status change rewrites only the pill text that actually changed.
- **Status lights cost far less to update**: when a session's status changes, the dashboard now repaints only that session's row and card, once per frame, instead of every row in the rail, and the server sends one status message per session per moment instead of up to four. With 30 sessions, one status change now touches the page about 27 times instead of about 494, and takes about 3.2 ms of script instead of 4.2. The tab title, the favicon and the alert chime still update immediately, even while the tab is in the background; the rail catches up when you come back to it. The elapsed-time clocks now tick only for sessions that are running, waiting or starting.

- **Desktop no longer downloads the phone layout**: the phone screens now download only when the dashboard is in the phone layout, at load or when you switch to it, so a desktop load moves about 4 KB less before the board appears. On a phone the screens appear once that download finishes.
- **Rarely used extras download on first use**: the merge celebration, the first-run checklist, the Restart dialog, alert sounds and the flying animals now download the first time they appear instead of before the board, so a cold load moves about 10 KB less before the board appears. Each one appears a moment later the first time on a slow link.
- **The doc editor and shown-file viewer download on first open**: the doc and shown-file buttons still appear as soon as an agent surfaces a file, and the editor or viewer downloads the first time you open it, so a cold load moves about 2 KB less before the board appears.

- **The open session comes back sooner after a reload**: the dashboard now connects to the terminal of the session you had open as soon as the page starts, instead of waiting for the list of sessions first. On a simulated 3G link a reload shows that session after about 2.1 seconds instead of 3.1. If that session is gone, the dashboard connects as before.

- **Reloading the dashboard no longer rechecks its fonts**: the CommitMono font files now carry a content hash in their name, so the browser keeps them cached until an update changes them instead of asking the server about each one on every reload.

- **Each secondary view brings its own styles**: the styles for Settings, Usage, Reviews, My PRs, Radar, Visions, Hooks, Trace, Benchmarks and the plan view now download with that view instead of with the board, so the board's stylesheet shrinks from 39 KB to 30 KB compressed. On a simulated 3G link the board still appears after about 8.3 seconds, because the fonts take the freed bandwidth.

## [0.46.1] - 2026-10-27

### Fixed

- **Settings save again on a new install**: in 0.46.0, a config file with only a few settings, as on a new install, refused every settings save, with a warning that config.json looked wiped. Saves now go through, and experimental features stay off on release builds as before.

## [0.46.0] - 2026-10-27

### Added

- **A shorter first-run checklist**: on a new install, folders in your home that hold git repos (such as `~/src`) are saved as repository roots without a click, so the checklist goes straight to starting a session; the roots step opens only when none are found, with a single path field. The missing-agent step is one line with a Copy button beside the install command, and the help about restarting Glimmervoid or adding customAgents appears only after a Rescan still finds no agent. The intro, footer and hint lines are gone, desktop notifications sit under Optional (hidden on phone), the checklist keeps one width from step to step, and the header + Session button is hidden while it shows.

- **Add Session offers Sane YOLO mode, on by default**: the Skip permission prompts box is now Sane YOLO mode, checked when a dialog opens on a machine where Sane YOLO can run, with a ? link to its setting. A session started with it skips permission prompts and always runs with the Sane YOLO hook, even when the machine `saneYolo` setting is off, across restarts, copies and the sessions it spawns. Where Sane YOLO cannot protect the session, because it is not installed, your settings did not load, or a Coder environment is picked, the box starts or turns unchecked and a note says checking it skips prompts unprotected. Run without guardrails moves under a Safety overrides toggle that shows its state beside it, the checkboxes now sit beside their labels, and a line under the mode buttons says what Workspace does.

- **Spawned sessions stay quiet**: a session another session started with `glimmervoid spawn` no longer sends browser or Telegram notifications when it waits, finishes or fails. Its parent session is told instead and is the one that reaches you.

- **Review shows what it compares against**: the review panel now names the branch and commit a session's committed changes are compared against, as `vs origin/main @ db44dc0` under the headline, with the full commit on hover. If the base branch cannot be found, the line reads `vs db44dc0, base branch not found` in amber, so a diff against a stale or missing base no longer looks like the session's own work.

- **Review from the keyboard**: in Reviews, J and K move through the queue, N and P move between findings, X posts or drops the current finding, and O opens the pull request in a session.
