A, R and C pick Approve, Request changes or Comment without posting: the button lights up, the note box takes focus, and Cmd+Enter (Ctrl+Enter off a Mac) posts it, while Esc drops the pick. Cmd+Enter in the note box posts the action the agent recommends. No single key posts anything, and the keys do nothing while you type in a text field. Settings lists them under Reviews.

- **Agents can show you what they made**: inside a session, `glimmervoid show <path>... [--caption <text>]` hands Glimmervoid an image (PNG, JPEG, WebP, GIF, SVG), a video (MP4, WebM) or a single-file HTML page, up to 200 MB each, with no artifact or preview page needed. It works on files in the session's scratchpad, and refuses other kinds of file or anything over the limit with a message naming it. The session card gains a Shown button, with a dot beside it and on the session's rail entry until you open it, which opens a viewer for every file the session showed: images and animations in place, videos with playback controls, and an HTML page as a card whose Open page button opens it in its own tab, sandboxed so its scripts run but cannot reach the dashboard. While you watch, each file also appears as a small chip at the right edge of the terminal line where the agent showed it, and clicking the chip opens that file. Each session keeps its newest 50 shown files, and they survive a dashboard reload.

- **Reviews reads like a decision desk**: a ready review now puts the agent's call first ("Agent recommends: approve"), keeps the reading column narrow, and shows a Will post to GitHub panel that names the review type, how many findings post and whether your note goes with it, updating as you change anything. Each finding has Post and Drop buttons instead of a checkbox. On a wide pane that panel and the pull request's checks, threads and new commits sit in a column beside the review. Queue rows now read "Agent: approve" with how many findings would post. After you post, the next review's action bar says what was posted and where, and the row you just acted on lights up briefly; the banner that used to appear above the next review is gone. A post that comes back with a warning stays on screen with the warning until you move on.

- **Code beside each Reviews finding**: an inline finding on a ready review now shows the few diff lines around the line it cites, with the cited line marked, so you can judge it without opening the pull request. Reviews drafted before this update show no excerpt until they are reviewed again.

- **Request changes from Reviews**: a ready review now offers Request changes next to Approve and Comment, posting a change request on GitHub under your name with the findings you ticked and your note. It needs at least one finding or a note. When the agent's verdict is request changes or blocked, Request changes is the first button; otherwise Approve is.

- **The server log survives a dashboard Restart**: Glimmervoid now copies its own output into `logs/server.log` under its home folder (`~/.glimmervoid` unless `GLIMMERVOID_HOME` says otherwise), so the log keeps going after a Restart from the dashboard, which used to discard it. The file rolls over at 10 MB and keeps the four previous files as `server.log.1` to `server.log.4`.

- **Every cleanup leaves a record**: before Glimmervoid deletes a worktree, a branch, a workspace folder, a leftover scratch or guardrails temp folder, or an aged log file, it writes a line to `logs/removals.jsonl` naming what goes, why, and which checks it passed. If that line cannot be written, the deletion is skipped and the server log says so.

### Fixed

- **The Changes panel no longer says "No session selected" while a session is open**: editing or restarting a session cleared the panel's selection, and clicking the session that was already open could not bring it back. The panel now keeps the session through the rebuild, and clicking an open session in the rail always selects it.
- **Closing a session whose work is already on origin no longer warns that it would be lost**: the check before removal compared the session's commits only with your local base branch, so work pushed straight to `origin/main` while your local `main` was behind (for example, checked out in another folder) looked unmerged. Commits on either your local base branch or `origin/<base>` now count as landed, and a restart no longer marks such a session as pending review. A commit on neither, uncommitted changes, or a check that fails still warns.
- **The clipboard prompt no longer covers the agent's input line**: when an agent copies text and the browser needs a click first, the Copy to clipboard prompt now appears at the top-right of the terminal instead of over the bottom rows where the agent's prompt and status line sit. The clipboard copied and failed notices show there too.

- **The dashboard reconnects on its own on phones**: coming back to the dashboard after more than 10 seconds away now opens a fresh connection straight away instead of waiting 5 seconds to find out the old one died, so on a slow mobile link the open terminal is live again in about a second instead of about 6. While the dashboard is on screen it now checks the connection every 15 seconds and when the phone switches networks, and replaces a connection that stopped answering, where it used to sit on a dead one until you left and came back. A connection attempt that hangs for 2 seconds is dropped and retried straight away instead of leaving the dashboard on Connecting, and a terminal that was waiting to retry reconnects the moment you come back to it instead of finishing a wait that could grow to 30 seconds while the phone slept. After a reconnect only the terminals on screen reconnect at once; the rest reconnect when you open them, so their activity pulse pauses until then. This now holds on Safari and Firefox too: a dropped connection attempt no longer throws away the dashboard's sign-in and refetches it, which added most of a second there, and the terminal on screen connects before the control connection, since Firefox opens only one connection to the dashboard at a time.

- **The dashboard works from the keyboard**: an open dialog now keeps Tab inside it, takes focus when it opens, blocks clicks and dashboard shortcuts behind it, closes only the top dialog on Escape, and hands focus back to whatever opened it. The power menu now works like the Session actions menu: the arrow keys open it and move through it, and Escape or moving focus away closes it. The Issues scope tabs move with the arrow keys, and every tab strip jumps with Home and End. The first Tab on the page offers Skip to main content.

- **Automatic cleanups no longer delete a live session's worktree**: branch cleanup, the startup sweep of leftover workspaces, and the owned-paths sweep now keep any worktree or branch that belongs to a session in your config or one that is running, and anything touched in the last 24 hours. Work from a session you removed is still cleaned up once it has been idle for a day. Removing a session, discarding its work, or finishing and merging still deletes at once. A Glimmervoid started on a different config file (a test run, for example) can no longer sweep the worktrees of the one you use.

### Removed

- **Post-turn checks are gone**: Glimmervoid no longer scans files for trailing whitespace, a missing final newline or a byte order mark after each turn, and the Post-turn checks setting is gone from Settings. Their report showed only in the session header tag that was removed, so the default report mode did nothing you could see. A `postTurnChecks` block left in `config.json` is ignored.
- **The experimental Zen view is gone**: the Zen tab, its meadow of session pets and the Settings > Sessions > Zen view toggle no longer exist. Cmd+J (Alt+J off a Mac) still jumps to the next session that needs you, in Focus. A `zenView` key left in `config.json` is ignored, and a dashboard last left on Zen opens on Focus.

### Changed

- **About has its own Settings page**: Status key and about is now two pages. Status key holds the session status and pull request legends, and About shows the version with GitHub, Changelog and Website links side by side instead of stacked. On phone, a section page now opens under a slim Settings back row in place of the search field and boxed Back button.
- **Session worktrees get their own node_modules**: a new session worktree no longer links in the main checkout's `node_modules`, so an install the agent runs there stays inside the worktree instead of rewriting the main checkout's dependencies (a pnpm install through the link left the main checkout refusing every later install). Resuming a session whose worktree still holds that link removes the link and leaves the main checkout's `node_modules` alone. The agent installs dependencies when it needs them. To keep sharing, add `node_modules` to `worktreeShare` in `config.json`, whose default is now `.env`, `.env.local` and `.claude`.
- **Adding a session to a folder that is not a git repository just works**: Add Session no longer asks you to tick "Run in this folder, no worktree" and no longer refuses such a folder. The session starts in the folder itself, since it cannot get a worktree of its own, and the + on a sidebar group starts it directly instead of opening the dialog. The project picker no longer labels folders "(not a git repo)". Git repositories still get their own worktree.
- **The dashboard font starts loading sooner**: the page now asks for the CommitMono font as it starts loading instead of after the stylesheet arrives.
- **Less to download before the board appears**: the code behind the secondary views (Usage, Reviews, My PRs, Visions, Trace, Hooks and Settings), the plan view and the Add session dialog now downloads when you first open them, so a first visit fetches about 40 KB less before the board shows. On a simulated 3G link the board still appears after about 8.3 seconds, because the fonts now use the bandwidth this frees. A settings link naming a section that does not exist now opens Settings instead of the last view you used.
- **Session card headers keep the task title readable**: the text tags next to the title are now small icons, so a long task title no longer gets cut off beside them. Only YOLO or Sane YOLO, guardrails, and token usage (when shown) remain; hover or focus an icon to see what it means. The needs input, cloud, worktree, agent, wakeup, compacting and post-turn tags are gone from the header; needs input and cloud still show in the session list.
- **Guardrails toggle from the shield icon**: click the guardrails shield in a session's header to release or restore guardrails, with the same confirmation as before. A released session shows a slashed shield. The Guardrails item is gone from the Session actions menu.
- **Experimental features run only on experimental builds**: Conversation view, Remote Coder sessions, Coherence, Issues and the phone swipe arrow pad now work only on an experimental build (a version like 0.45.1-next.212, from Settings > Updates > Channel > Experimental). On a stable release they read as off, and they are gone from Settings and from `glimmervoid settings`, which refuses to read or change them. Whatever you had set stays in `config.json` and comes back on an experimental build.
- **An Experimental update channel replaces Main**: Settings > Updates > Channel now offers Release and Experimental. Experimental tracks prerelease builds (versions like 0.45.1-next.212) published to npm under the `next` tag from changes on main before they reach a stable release, and offers a stable release instead when it is newer. A clone still updates from the dashboard, now to the experimental build's commit rather than the tip of its branch; an npm install gets the matching `npm install -g glimmervoid@<version>` command. A `config.json` set to the old `main` channel loads as Experimental. Moving back to Release while running a build ahead of the latest stable offers nothing until a newer stable ships, so no downgrade is offered.
- **Shift+Enter starts a new line in the terminal**: in a session terminal, Shift+Enter now starts a new line in the agent's prompt instead of submitting it, as it already did in the Conversation view. Enter alone still sends.
- **Reviews covers pull requests from forks**: a pull request opened from a fork now gets the same sandboxed agent review as one from a branch in the repository, so open source contributions reach the Reviews queue as drafts ready to post. The "From a fork" row that sent you to GitHub is gone, and a fork pull request it was holding gets reviewed on the next poll. Keep mergeable still never pushes to a fork's branch.
- **Reviews is on by default, Issues is now experimental**: the Reviews tab and team review now start on unless `teamReview.enabled` is set to `false`; team review still waits for an organization and a GitHub team before drafting anything. The Issues tab is hidden and its GitHub polling stopped until you turn on Settings > Issues (experimental) > Show the Issues tab (`issues.enabled` in `config.json`).
- **Approvals carry one combined note**: an approval posted from Reviews now opens with a single note reading "Automated review by Glimmervoid, not written by a human. Approved by hand after checking it." in place of the automated-review note plus a separate closing line. A comment posted from Reviews names Glimmervoid in its note too, and never claims a hand approval. Inline finding comments are unchanged.
- **Shortcuts and about is now two Settings pages**: Settings > Shortcuts holds the recordable jump shortcut and the shortcut reference, laid out as one column with each action on the left and its keys on the right, under titles sized like the rest of Settings. The status colors, pull request icons, version and links moved to a new Settings > Status key and about page, with the version and links at the top. The ? button still opens Shortcuts.

### Fixed

- A session needing you while the dashboard is in the background now sounds once: the desktop notification carries the alert sound and the dashboard chime stays quiet, instead of both playing. With the dashboard focused, or desktop notifications off or blocked, the chime plays as before.
- Headless Chromium and Playwright now start inside a guarded session on macOS instead of crashing on launch: the guardrails let a session register its own Mach services, which Chromium needs at startup. Lane guardrails stay as strict as before.
- The attention bar at the top of the Reviews queue, with the Cmd+J hint, now fits inside the queue column instead of running past its right edge, and the bar and the queue below it use the same spacing as the Focus rail.

## [0.45.0] - 2026-10-26

### Added

- **Quick session from the one you are in**: Cmd+Option+0 on Mac, Alt+Shift+0 elsewhere, starts a new session right away on the same project as the session you are typing in (or the selected one), with the same agent, permission setting, guardrails (a session whose guardrails you released starts guarded), base branch, workspace repositories or Coder environment, and puts you in its terminal. It starts a fresh conversation, never the source's. With no session open it opens Add session instead. A refusal shows in the header like any Add session error. Alt+Shift+0 works from inside a terminal; Alt+0 still reaches the shell.

- **A session closes itself when its agent quits**: typing `/quit` (or any clean exit of the agent) now removes the session instead of leaving a "Session complete" card. When removing the session would lose work, the Remove dialog opens instead and names that work, so nothing is discarded without asking. A failed exit, Kill, Restart, Finish and merge, and stopping the server never remove a session. Cmd+K on Mac, Alt+K elsewhere, removes the selected session the same way its X does.

### Changed

- **Remove skips the confirmation when nothing would be lost**: Cmd+K, the card's X, the actions menu, focus view and the phone board now remove a session at once when its worktree has no uncommitted changes and no commits missing from its base branch, its workspace repositories and Coder workspace hold no unsaved work, and its agent is not working. The server checks the worktree at that moment, so edits made during a turn count even before the card shows them. Otherwise one Remove dialog opens and names the reason it stopped you. Agent-spawned sessions follow the same rule, and confirming the dialog now discards a spawned session's worktree too. An agent that quits with work the card had not shown yet opens that dialog instead of staying on the board silently.

## [0.44.2] - 2026-10-26

### Changed

- **The server log now says when the dashboard breaks**: when a dashboard tab receives an update it cannot read, hits an uncaught error, or fails to load a view such as Reviews, Glimmervoid's own log gets a `[dashboard]` line naming what failed, for example the update type and the field that did not match. Each distinct problem is logged once per page load, at most 32 per page.

- **The board appears sooner on a slow connection**: the dashboard's stylesheet no longer carries the flying-animal pictures, which now download only when an animal flies, and the CommitMono font starts from a smaller file holding the characters the dashboard uses, fetching the rest only when a terminal shows a rarer one. On a simulated 3G link a first visit shows the board after about 8.3 seconds instead of 9.7, with 298 KB downloaded instead of 371. Italic text is gone: the italic font files are removed and everything renders upright.

- **The session board appears before the terminal code loads**: the terminal renderer now downloads after the board is on screen instead of before it. On a simulated 3G link a first visit shows the board after about 9.8 seconds instead of 12.2. Opening a session in the first moments after a first visit waits for that download.

- **Live updates use less data on a slow connection**: the dashboard now compresses what it streams over its live connections, both the session list updates and each terminal's output. The dashboard also appears without waiting for sessions that are off screen. On a simulated 3G link, opening a session drops from about 2.1 to 1.15 seconds and reloading the dashboard from about 4.1 to 3.4 seconds.

- **Flying animals move like animals**: each animal now has its own eased gait. Frogs and deer crouch, leap and squash on landing, trotters bounce on their feet, the penguin waddles, and flyers glide and tilt along their arc, as does the cat in the telemetry banner. The website cookie banner now always shows the owl instead of a random animal. Booping one squashes, springs and wobbles before it speeds off. Every trail is redrawn at the sprites' pixel size with four frames, a fainter slower layer drifting behind it, and a burst of its motif at the animal's tail, in the dashboard, the settings gallery and on the website.

- **Dashboard opens faster on a slow connection**: the build now compresses the dashboard ahead of time, so a phone over a slow link downloads about a quarter of the bytes it used to on first load, and a reload uses its cache instead of downloading the app again. On a simulated 3G link the first load dropped from about 40 seconds to about 15. Settings, Reviews, Usage, Issues, Hooks, Trace, Radar, Visions and Bench now load their code the first time you open them, so the app starts with less to download and parse; anything that arrived before then still shows when you open the tab.

- **Changes panel stays current while you watch it**: while the Changes panel is open and the tab is in front, edits a session makes show up within about 4 seconds, mid-turn, for every agent, not just when the turn ends. Glimmervoid checks only the one session on screen, and stops as soon as the panel is collapsed, the phone leaves the Changes screen, or the tab is hidden.

- **Changes screen on phone**: one title row with the session name instead of two stacked headers. A session with no changes yet shows a short note saying edits land here, ready to merge into the base branch, instead of an empty screen, on desktop too. The branch sync refresh button now always shows its sync state beside it on phone, since there is no hover tooltip to explain it.

### Fixed

- **Opening a session no longer downloads its screen twice**: a session card you were not looking at used to receive its whole screen, throw it away, and download it again when you opened it. Hidden cards now ask the server to hold their output, and opening one catches it up in place over the same connection. On a slow link opening a session is back to its earlier speed, and sessions off screen no longer use bandwidth while they run. Their activity pulse in the rail still beats.

- **More menu on phone sits above the all clear bar**: the bottom item of the More menu, such as Hooks, was hidden behind the attention bar on the Focus screen. The menu now opens on top of it.

- **Typing on a phone no longer pushes the terminal out of view**: pressing a key, such as a number on a Claude question screen with a side preview, could slide the terminal's content up and leave the card blank until you left and reopened the session. The terminal now stays put while you type, and the key still lands.

## [0.44.1] - 2026-10-25

### Added

- **Delete my data**: Settings > Privacy has a Delete my data button. After you confirm, Glimmervoid asks for everything its usage data recorded under this install to be erased and switches to a new install id; your telemetry setting stays as it was. The same page links to the privacy details on glimmervoid.dev, and Shortcuts and about links to the website.

### Changed

- **Usage data says plainly what it collects**: the first-run banner, now with a cat, and the terminal notice read "Glimmervoid collects usage data to help us improve it. No prompts or project information are collected.", with a link to what is collected. The setting is now called Send usage data. Each install now keeps a usage profile with its first version and platform and a salted one-way hash of your Claude and Codex account ids, never the ids themselves, emails or names. Opening the website from the dashboard ties that visit to your install only after you accept the site cookie.

- **Anonymous usage data marks a first start**: the app start event now says whether this is the first time Glimmervoid has started on the machine (no config file yet), so a new install can be told apart from a restart. Nothing else about the install is sent.

## [0.44.0] - 2026-10-25

### Added

- **Anonymous usage data covers the first-run checklist**: it now reports which checklist step a new install is on and when each step is done, plus clicks on the button that looks for agents again, on adding a repository root (typed or picked from the found list) and on + Session. No paths or folder names are sent.
- **See a session's changes on your phone**: the phone Terminal screen has a Changes button next to the session menu. It opens that session's review panel full screen, with its diff, Merge and resolve controls, and Back or a swipe returns you to the terminal.

### Changed

- **One Restart... item in the session menu**: Restart, Restart fresh and Resume conversation... are now a single Restart... item, on desktop and phone. It opens a dialog where you pick this conversation (the default), a fresh one, or an earlier conversation from any worktree of the repo, and Restart restarts into it right away. Picking an earlier conversation no longer waits for the session's next restart. A mid-turn restart still asks first, a session that has not started yet starts into your pick, and a session spawned by another agent offers only this and fresh.
- **One Reviews queue**: the Reviews tab no longer splits into Team and Mine. Teammates' pull requests and your own sit in one queue grouped by who acts next: Your turn, Agent working, Waiting on others, and a collapsed Set aside. The attention banner from Focus sits on top, and Cmd/Alt+J on the Reviews tab opens the next pull request waiting on you. An All, Reviewing and Mine filter narrows the list without hiding the banner, one Refresh updates both lanes, and the tab's dot lights for anything new on your turn, your own pull requests included. Pull requests from forks now open in the queue with a Review on GitHub link.
- **Team review reads top to bottom**: a ready review now shows its verdict with what it did not cover right under it, then every finding as a checkbox (inline ones and ones not on a changed line alike; low severity starts unticked), then one optional note and a decision bar with Approve, Comment, Re-run review and Not mine, all visible. The status line under the buttons says exactly what will post. Approve with nothing ticked and no note posts a bare approval, and after Approve or Comment the next item that needs you opens with a line naming what was posted. The editable review body and the More menu are gone; Queue review is now Re-run review.
- **Your own pull request page leads with what blocks the merge**: Merge readiness lists failing, conflicting and waiting rows before the ones already fine, with every failing check named under Checks. Threads waiting on you come next, then other open threads, reviews, and an Automation section holding Keep mergeable and Merge when ready, with Merge in the bar at the bottom.
- **Reviews on a phone is two screens**: tapping a pull request opens its page on its own screen, with Back to queue and the position in Your turn at the top and the decision bar held above the bottom nav. Back, or a swipe back, returns to the queue.
- **A team review names what to check by hand**: when a pull request touches UI, its review page shows a Check by hand list between the verdict and the findings: the layouts, copy or flows the reviewer could not judge from code, or the changed UI files when it named none. The list is information only and never holds back Approve.
- **One-line attention banner**: the banner at the top of the Focus rail and the phone Board is now a single line. The separate "Next session" row is gone: on desktop the Cmd/Alt+J keys sit at the right of the same line, lit in the color of the session they would open and dimmed when everything is clear, so you can tell at a glance whether pressing them does anything. On the phone the line shows status only, and a tap anywhere on it still opens the next session.
- **Clearer errors at the top of the window**: each error now starts with where it came from (Server, Worktree, Upload, Remove session and so on), says what to do next, shows in a full-width strip under the header that wraps instead of being cut off, and shows how long ago it happened. It clears by itself when its session is removed or the same action later succeeds. An error about one session now shows on that session's card instead of the header, and a request the server cannot read asks you to reload the page instead of showing validation details.

### Fixed

- **Anonymous usage data counts every session end**: closing a session, a settings change that restarts it, and stopping the server now send the session ended event, with which of the three it was. Before, only an agent that quit on its own sent it.
- **Phone terminal no longer comes back half blank**: returning to the dashboard after your phone put the tab in the background could leave the Terminal showing only a scatter of characters until new output arrived. The terminal now redraws every character as soon as the browser hands its graphics back.
- **Phone clears what you have seen**: opening a session on the phone Terminal screen now reliably clears it from Needs you, the Board dot and the favicon, even when the mobile browser reports the page as unfocused. Coming back to the phone with a session still open on the Terminal screen clears it too, including after the connection reconnects.
- **Ended background sessions no longer leave their temp folder behind**: a session spawned by another agent, a team review, a Radar fix or a Visions job kept its folder under `~/.glimmervoid/guardrails-temp` after it ended, holding its scratchpad and background task output, so the folder grew by gigabytes over a week. The folder is now deleted as soon as the session's processes have exited.
- **Temp folders of sessions whose Glimmervoid has exited are cleared right away**: every running Glimmervoid now holds an operating system file lock for each session folder it uses under `~/.glimmervoid/guardrails-temp`, and the startup and daily cleanup delete a folder as soon as no Glimmervoid holds its lock, instead of waiting a week. The system drops the lock the moment its holder exits, even after a crash or a forced kill, so a second Glimmervoid sharing the same home keeps its sessions' folders, including ones for the same project, for exactly as long as it runs. Folders from earlier versions, which carry no lock, still go after a week.
- **Dependencies**: adds `fs-native-extensions` 1.5.2 for those file locks; it ships prebuilt for Linux, macOS and Windows, so installing needs no compiler.

## [0.43.5] - 2026-10-24

### Added

- **See who is next in line**: every session waiting on you, failed or finished unseen now carries a small numbered tab in the corner of its row in the Focus rail and on the phone, in the same order as the Next session banner. Tab 1 is filled: it is the session Cmd/Alt+J (or a tap on the banner) opens next. Rows never move; hovering a row or using a screen reader reads its place, such as "next up" or "#3 in line".

### Fixed

- **Phone bottom nav usable on first run**: before your first session, the Next session banner sat on top of the phone's bottom nav and blocked taps on More. The banner now stays hidden until there is a session to jump to.

## [0.43.4] - 2026-10-23

### Added

- **Rearrange your tabs**: drag a tab in the desktop header to a new place, or use Move up and Move down under Settings > Sessions > Tab order. The order is saved with Glimmervoid, not the browser, so the phone's More menu follows the same order as the desktop header.

### Changed

- **The whole Next session banner jumps**: a click or tap anywhere on the banner, not only its Next session row, does what Cmd/Alt+J does. The session tiles in it no longer open their own session; they show who is next in line. On a phone the banner now sits at the bottom of the Focus screen, just above the bottom nav, within reach of your thumb.
- **Phone nav names match desktop**: the phone's Board button is now called Focus, like the desktop tab it stands for.

### Removed

- **Phone Review screen**: the phone bottom nav no longer has a Review button for the selected session's diff. Pull request reviews are still under More > Reviews, and the desktop review sidebar is unchanged.

### Fixed

- **Resuming on a phone no longer gets stuck connecting**: coming back to the dashboard after your phone was locked or switched away used to reconnect the open terminal every time output had arrived meanwhile, and on Android that reconnect could hang on the connecting animal until you reloaded the page. The server now pauses terminal output while the page is hidden and catches the same connection up when you return, so the terminal comes back live with what you missed and its scrollback intact. A hidden page also stops receiving terminal data, which saves mobile data.

## [0.43.3] - 2026-10-23

### Added

- **Set up a remote host from Settings**: Settings > Sessions has a Set up remote host button. It starts a Claude Code or Codex session, whichever you are signed in to, that walks you through running Glimmervoid on another Linux machine you reach by ssh: installing it, running it as a systemd user service, opening it from your laptop and phone through `tailscale serve` and `glimmervoid pair`, signing commits through your forwarded ssh agent, and the Ubuntu sandbox fix. The session runs outside the guardrails so it can use your ssh keys, still asks before each command, and its working folder is deleted when it ends.

### Changed

- **Pick your own jump key**: Settings > Shortcuts and about now records the key that, held with Cmd on a Mac or Alt elsewhere, jumps to the next session needing you, in place of J. Press Record, then the whole shortcut; keys other dashboard shortcuts use, the browser's own Q, W, T, N, L and R, and the copy, paste, cut, select all and undo keys C, V, X, A and Z are refused with the reason. Every on-screen hint follows the new key at once, and Reset brings back J. The choice is kept per browser.
- **Agents are pointed at glimmervoid spawn**: an operator session's agent is now told to delegate work that edits files or runs long with `glimmervoid spawn`, which gives the task its own dashboard card, instead of an in-process subagent you cannot see. Lanes and spawned sessions, which cannot spawn, are not told. The `spawn` line of `glimmervoid --help` says the same.
- **Click Next session to jump**: the Next session row at the bottom of the Focus rail banner is now a button, so clicking it does exactly what Cmd/Alt+J does, including the all-clear line when nothing needs you. The phone Board banner uses the same row in place of its separate Next button.
- **Fewer empty wakes from spawned sessions**: a session is no longer woken with "has news" just because a session it spawned ended after it had already read that session's report. The end note still waits in its next `glimmervoid read`, and now says how the session ended: exited by itself, closed by the operator, or closed because its parent ended. Closing a spawned session yourself with `glimmervoid close` posts no end note. An end that leaves a worktree kept for you, a session that ends before its parent read anything from it, or one that fails, still wakes the parent.
- **Spawned sessions are named for their task**: a session started with `glimmervoid spawn` and no `--name` is now named from the opening words of its brief (up to 60 characters), so its card and every note about it say what it was doing, in place of the parent's name with "agent" on the end. A brief that is a slash command still gets the old name.

## [0.43.2] - 2026-10-22

### Changed

- **GitHub actions start sooner and show their progress**: Reviews, Issues and PR session checkout actions now run their GitHub reads ahead of background polling. Action statuses show the Focus rail's pulsing dot while working, a check when done and a cross on failure; the dot stays still with reduced motion. Approve and resolve-thread actions log total and per-step timing to `server.log` and include `duration_ms` in the `team_review_settled` and `team_review_post_refused` telemetry events.

## [0.43.1] - 2026-10-22

### Changed

- **The open session stands out in the Focus rail**: its row used to get a faint accent wash that looked the same as the wash on waiting, failed and finished rows, so after Next session (Cmd/Alt+J) it was hard to tell where you landed. The open row now has a raised background, a full accent outline and a bold name, and a jump rings the row you land on in accent for a moment. The ring is skipped when your system asks for reduced motion.

### Fixed

- **Dashboard idles lighter while a session works**: the pulsing ring on a working session's icon, and the spinner on a starting one, made the browser lay out the whole page again on every frame, about 60 times a second. They look the same but no longer do, which cuts the dashboard's idle CPU use while any session is working.

## [0.43.0] - 2026-10-22

### Changed

- **Next session banner**: the counted chips are now one icon tile per session needing you, in jump order (failed, then waiting, then finished), with the head tile larger and the banner border in its color. Next session (Cmd/Alt+J) always opens the head tile, which flies into that session's rail row; the rest slide up. Clicking a tile opens its session the same way, hovering a tile or a rail row highlights its partner, and tiles past the banner width fold into +N. The session you are in leaves the line and rejoins at its rank if it still needs you. The phone Board banner gets the same tiles.
- **Removing a multi-repo workspace session cleans up its folder and asks before discarding work**: removal used to leave the `ws-` folder behind, and often its repo checkouts too. Now, when every member is clean and its commits are already on the base branch, removal deletes all of it. When a member still holds uncommitted changes or commits that are not on the base branch, removal stops and the dialog lists each such folder and why; choosing Discard work then removes everything.
- **Removing a session deletes its Claude Code scratch files**: an unguarded session used to leave its scratchpad under the system temp folder forever, which added up to gigabytes on a busy machine. Every local session now keeps Claude Code's temp files in `~/.glimmervoid/guardrails-temp/<session id>`, which survives restarts and edits and is deleted when you remove the session.
- **Server start cleans up checkouts left by removed sessions**: worktrees and workspace folders that Glimmervoid created for a session that no longer exists, or for a lane run cut short by a restart, are deleted when the server starts. A checkout that still holds uncommitted changes or commits not on its base branch is kept, named in the server log with the reason, and checked again at the next start. Folders Glimmervoid did not create, including every checkout made before this release, are never touched.
- **GitHub tabs recover from GitHub outages within minutes and say what is going on**: when GitHub answered with errors or timed out, Reviews, My PRs and Issues could stop refreshing for up to an hour, and Refresh either waited behind every background read or failed at once with the old error. Now those tabs keep their last rows, retry after 10 s, 30 s and 90 s, and then at least every 5 minutes. A GitHub rate limit still waits its full window. Refresh runs next, ahead of background reads, and shows it is working from the moment you press it. While githubstatus.com lists an open incident, the error line names it, and a "Last updated N min ago." line shows while an error stands or the rows are more than 5 minutes old. GitHub reads now give up after 15 s instead of 30 s (writes such as merges and reviews keep 30 s), and server log lines for a failed poll now say why it failed.
- **Dependencies**: `cc-safety-net` 2.6.16, so Sane YOLO now also refuses `rsync --delete` into a folder outside the working directory or a trusted temp folder, shell `-c` invocations whose real command string it cannot identify (such as `bash -c -x '<cmd>'`), and commands holding a carriage return or an unquoted Unicode blank. It also inspects more scripts that a heredoc wrote before they run, and blocks fewer safe `source` and script paths as unverifiable.

## [0.42.3] - 2026-10-21

### Fixed

- **The review panel no longer lists work that already landed as this session's**: when the local main checkout lagged behind origin, the Committed list, the line totals and Worth a look's "Also being changed in" lines counted commits already on origin as the session's own work. A session now compares against origin when the local branch is just an older copy of it, and still compares against the local branch when that branch is ahead or has gone its own way.

## [0.42.2] - 2026-10-21

### Changed

- **The review panel is one plain view**: the Map and Diff tabs are gone. The top says what changed in one sentence, such as "5 files changed, not committed yet", with the added and removed line counts. A short Worth a look list follows, at most five lines, each naming a file: another session changing it, a package link that reaches another repository, a file it usually changes with that is missing, or no test reaching it; Show all reveals the rest, and clicking a line opens that file below. Then the file list, where clicking a file shows its diff right under it, grouped into Committed and Not committed yet only when both exist. "Often fixed" and "N depend on it" now sit as small tags on the file row instead of in the list, and the base commit, committed and uncommitted counters and area chips are gone. A disabled Merge on work with nothing committed now says why beside it.
- **The "needs you" banner in the Focus rail has room to breathe and puts failed sessions first**: the status chips sit on their own row, with a "Next session" line and larger, readable Alt+J (Cmd+J on Mac) keys below them. Chips, the banner color and Cmd/Alt+J now go failed first, then waiting, then finished, in Focus and the phone Board. ALL CLEAR sits where the chips were, so the banner keeps its height when the last item clears, and the thick colored left edge is gone. Pressing Cmd/Alt+J (or Next on the phone) gives the banner a short key-press dip, chips pop when their count drops, slide out when they reach zero and scale in when they arrive, and a press with nothing waiting shows a short dry remark in place of ALL CLEAR for a moment. With reduced motion on, all of this is a plain fade.

### Removed

- **Review notes are gone**: the note button on diff lines, the Notes tab in the review panel and its Send notes bar are removed. To steer a session about its diff, type into its terminal.
- **The change map narrator is gone**: the optional model-written claims at the top of the review panel's map, and the Settings > Lanes > Change map section that turned them on, are removed. Glimmervoid no longer starts a narrator lane. An existing config.json that still has a `changeMap.narrator` block loads as before, and the block is ignored.
- **The STREAMER badge below the header is gone**: it hung over the middle of the page while streamer mode was on. On the desktop header, the streamer button still shows in the accent color while the mode is on.

### Fixed

- **Settings > Updates offers Restart when the newest release is already pulled but not running**: it used to say "The last check did not finish" with only a Check for updates button. It now names the checked-out release, lists it as Latest, and shows Restart. The status card also stops repeating itself: the headline gives the verdict (up to date, update available, or why the check failed), and the version and last-check time appear only once each, in the rows below.

## [0.42.1] - 2026-10-20

### Changed

- **The needs-you box at the top of the rail is easier to read**: instead of one purple "1 NEEDS YOU, 1 FAILED, 1 FINISHED" line that wrapped in a narrow rail, it now shows one chip per kind with the same icon and color as the session rows (needs input, failed, finished) and its count, and hovering a chip names it. The box is taller, its border takes the color of the most urgent kind present, and the `Cmd+J` (Alt+J off a Mac) keys no longer stack on top of each other. ALL CLEAR shows in a plain box when nothing needs you. The phone Board shows the same chips beside its Next button.

### Fixed

- **A remote session card says why its connection ended**: when a remote session's connection to its Coder workspace ended, the card showed failed with no reason. Now it names the cause: the workspace stopped (by its idle stop or its stop-after limit), was deleted, failed, or the connection was lost, and what Restart will do about it. Restart on a workspace Coder reports as failed now gets a fresh workspace too.
- **Restarting a failed remote session gets it a fresh workspace**: a remote session whose Coder workspace failed to come up, or was deleted outside Glimmervoid, used to fail again with the same reason on every restart, so the only way out was to close it and start a new one. Now Restart provisions a fresh workspace for that session and starts it there, keeping its branch name. The old workspace, if Coder still lists it, is stopped and later deleted like any other workspace this host no longer uses. If the environment is already at its live workspace limit, Restart says so instead.
- **Removing a remote session deletes its Coder workspace**: removing a remote session used to leave its workspace running and billing. Now Glimmervoid checks the workspace's checkout first. If nothing is uncommitted, stashed or unpushed, the session is stopped and the workspace is deleted with it. If there is unsaved work, or the check cannot run because the workspace is stopped or unreachable, the removal is refused with the branch and workspace named, and the dialog offers Discard remote work to delete it anyway. Removing a session while its workspace is still being created waits for that first.

## [0.42.0] - 2026-10-20

### Added

- **Remote Coder sessions are marked cloud**: a session running in a Coder workspace now shows a cloud icon after its name in the session list, on desktop and phone, and a CLOUD tag on its card. Hovering either names the workspace, so a remote session no longer looks like a second local one.

### Fixed

- **One "finished" notification per prompt again**: when a background task or monitor finished after a Claude Code session had already told you it was done, the session woke to read the result and notified you that it had finished a second time, and again for every later task. A long Stop hook made this more frequent by giving tasks more time to finish. About a third of finished notifications were these repeats. Now only a prompt you send starts a new round of notifications. The session card still shows each finish.
- **A remote Coder session no longer fails when its workspace builds fine**: when `coder create` lost its build log stream partway (`Coder create failed: watch build:`), Glimmervoid marked the session failed, tried to delete a workspace that went on to build, and left it running unmapped. Now it checks whether the workspace exists and waits for it to be ready as usual. A Coder error now also shows the cause Coder prints on the lines after it, and cleaning up a failed workspace no longer gives up after 30 seconds.
- **Coder workspaces no longer run unattended**: a Coder workspace this Glimmervoid created that no session holds any more is now stopped within about a minute, on every check rather than only at startup, and deleted once it has been stopped for `deleteAfterStoppedDays`. Glimmervoid records the workspaces it creates in `coder-owned-workspaces.json`, so another Glimmervoid signed in to the same Coder account never has its workspaces stopped or deleted. A failed or exited remote session's workspace now stops after `idleStopMinutes`, like an idle one, instead of running until its stop-after limit. One failed `coder list` while a new workspace comes up no longer fails the session and deletes the workspace.
- **A signed-out Coder CLI says so**: when `coder` on this host is signed out or its session expired, starting or adding a remote session now says the CLI is not signed in and to run `coder login`, instead of failing later with a build or ssh error. Remote sessions work again as soon as you sign in, with no restart.
- **Spawned sessions no longer leave worktrees behind**: a session started with `glimmervoid spawn` used to keep its worktree and branch after `glimmervoid close` or after its parent ended, until Glimmervoid restarted. Now a clean worktree is removed however the child ends. A child holding work that is not on the integration branch is never thrown away silently: `glimmervoid close` refuses and names its branch and path, so the parent can have it run `glimmervoid push` first, or pass `--discard` to drop the work. If the child exits by itself, or its parent ends, with that work still in it, the worktree is kept and the parent's card asks for your attention with its branch and path. Removing the parent session removes its children's worktrees too, as removing a session already does with its own. The parent also gets a message on every child end saying whether the worktree was removed or kept.
- **Session titles no longer echo your last message**: while a turn ran, a session card's title showed the prompt you just sent until the turn ended. Now the card keeps its last refined title until a new one is ready. A new session, or one you just cleared, also gets its refined title within seconds of its first prompt, instead of after that first turn ends.
- **Failed and finished sessions leave the attention banner once you see them**: a failed session, such as one whose Coder workspace could not be created, used to stay in the FAILED count and the Cmd/Alt+J queue until you restarted or closed it. Now viewing it clears it from the banner and the queue on every open dashboard, and it stays failed with its reason shown. A session that finishes while a background task is still running, and then starts working again for that task before you look, now keeps counting as FINISHED until you view it, instead of dropping out of the banner as soon as its Monitoring status came back.

## [0.41.1] - 2026-10-20

### Added

- **Codex sessions get Claude Code's status detail**: on codex 0.163.0 or newer, a Codex card now stays working while a subagent it spawned is still running, shows compacting while Codex compacts its context instead of flashing complete, and moves to waiting when Codex asks you a question with request_user_input. A `git push` or `gh pr` command a Codex session runs refreshes its linked pull requests, and Team Review and Radar see each tool call a Codex session makes, as they do for Claude Code. Older Codex versions keep working as before.
- **Codex plan limits in Usage**: the Plan limits section of the Usage tab now shows Codex's 5 hour and 7 day windows beside Claude Code's, read from the Codex session files on this machine, each with its own reset countdown and age. With only one agent reporting limits, the section looks as before.

### Fixed

- **Task titles ignore background task notifications**: when a Claude Code background task or subagent finishes, the `<task-notification>` it injects no longer becomes a session's task title or feeds the title and name refiner, so titles stay on what you actually asked for.
- **Reviews leave the queue once merged or approved**: a review of a pull request that GitHub reports merged or closed now leaves the Reviews tab within about a minute, from Ready and from Waiting on author alike. Before, it could sit there for hours whenever one of the team searches came back incomplete. Approving a review whose comments were all resolved now moves it out of Ready right away, instead of waiting for GitHub to report the approval back.
- **Remote Coder sessions work with templates that ask for parameters**: a template with a parameter that has a default, such as a dotfiles branch, no longer fails every new remote session with "Coder create failed". Glimmervoid now takes each parameter's default unless your environment's `parameters` sets it. When a Coder command does fail, the card now shows Coder's own error, and it no longer reports a cleanup failure for a workspace that was never created.

## [0.41.0] - 2026-10-20

### Added

- **A session owns the sessions it spawns**: a session that runs `glimmervoid spawn` now gets a channel to that child. `glimmervoid send <child> <text>` steers the child, `glimmervoid read [<child>]` reads what it sent back, and `glimmervoid close <child>` ends it once the task is done. Inside the child, `glimmervoid send <text>` reaches its parent and `glimmervoid read` reads the parent's messages. Only a parent and its own children can reach each other; no session can message, read or close one it did not spawn. When the parent exits or its card is removed, its children end too.
- **Spawned sessions sit under their owner**: on the Focus rail and the phone Board, a session another session spawned now sits directly after that session, indented and labelled "Owned by <parent>", and its card header carries the same label. The parent shows how many sessions it owns.
- **A parent hears when its spawned session stops**: whenever a spawned session finishes a turn, waits on a prompt, exits or fails, Glimmervoid leaves a note in the parent's channel with the child's state and its last message. If the parent is idle, Glimmervoid types one fixed line into its terminal telling it to run `glimmervoid read`, so it picks the work back up on its own. The same happens for a child its parent messaged. It never types while you typed into that terminal in the last 30 seconds, and after 10 wake lines with no input from you it stops and flags the session for your attention instead.
- **Security notice for unsafe versions**: when the version you run has been marked unsafe on npm, the dashboard shows a red security notice above everything else, with npm's reason and the update command for your install. It shows even when no newer release is out yet, and also in Settings > Updates. Dismissing it hides it until you reload, never for good. The server console prints one security line too. It works with telemetry off, and it rides the normal update check, so turning update checks off turns it off.
- **Telemetry counts dashboard tab opens and actions**: when telemetry is on, two new anonymous events are sent. `view_shown` names which dashboard tab opened, on desktop or phone, every time it opens. `operator_action` names the tab (Usage, Issues, Radar or Reviews) and the action from a fixed list, such as sort, refresh, resolve or approve. Neither ever carries a repository, pull request or issue number, a URL, a title, a branch or any text. Every event is listed in [docs/configuration.md](docs/configuration.md#telemetry), and turning telemetry off stops them too.
- **Telemetry counts team reviews and Radar outcomes**: when telemetry is on, five more anonymous events are sent. `team_review_drafted` says how many inline comments a ready review draft holds, and `team_review_settled` says whether it was approved, commented, approved without comments or discarded, with how many comments were drafted and posted. `team_review_post_refused` names why posting was refused from a fixed list. `radar_investigation_finished` carries the verdict of each Radar investigation or fix, with a reason from a fixed list when it ended in an error, and `radar_fix_handoff_failed` names which step of opening the fix pull request failed. None carries a repository, pull request or issue number, a URL, a title, a branch, comment text or an error message.
- **Coherence keeps its own hooks wired**: in a repository with a `coherence.config.json`, a session whose Coherence hooks are missing or out of date gets them installed at the end of a turn, with no action from you. Hooks are rechecked after every turn, so ones that went stale mid-session heal too. The installed settings file stays in the session's worktree for the agent to commit with its work. If an install fails, the session asks for your attention with one line saying why. Codex hook trust stays yours to grant in `/hooks`. After a turn that changes files an invariant covers, that invariant's totality oracle re-runs in the background, along with the spec check. Every Coherence command Glimmervoid runs for a guardrailed session, the oracle's tests included, runs inside that session's own guardrails, so nothing the agent edits can reach anything the session itself cannot. A break goes to the agent first, through Coherence's own end-of-turn report; if the next turn ends with the invariant still failing, or the spec check still reporting problems it did not have when the session started, the session asks for your attention once, naming the invariant and its test. A failure Coherence had already recorded when the session started never asks for you. A healthy session's card looks the same as before.
- **Remote Coder sessions (experimental)**: turn on Remote Coder sessions (experimental) in Settings and list your Coder templates under `coder.environments` in config.json, and Add Session shows an Environment select (Local by default) when `coder` is on PATH on Linux or macOS. A session given an environment runs in its own new `gv-<12 hex>` Coder workspace: Glimmervoid creates it, checks out your branch there, and runs Claude Code or Codex inside it while the terminal, hooks, title and status work as for a local session. Claude Code signs in with your host login, or the one Connect saved, handed over through the SSH environment, never the command line or config. Codex runs its app-server in the workspace, signs in through Codex's `chatgptAuthTokens` login with your host access token (or the one Connect saved), and asks the host for refreshes, so your refresh token never leaves this machine. A workspace idle for `idleStopMinutes` (30 by default) is stopped and resumes on your next prompt; closing the session deletes it. `maxLive` (2 by default) caps live workspaces per environment, and Add Session names the count when it refuses. A remote session gets no local worktree. Its card shows why a provision failed, and a failed provision deletes what it created. Leftover `gv-*` workspaces no session owns are deleted once stopped for `deleteAfterStoppedDays` (3 by default). Turning the setting off refuses new remote sessions and leaves running ones working until you close them.
- **Connect a Claude Code or Codex login for remote sessions**: when this machine has no Claude Code or Codex login, Add Session (with an environment picked) and Settings > Agent logins show a Connect button. It runs the agent's own sign-in here and shows only the link to open, plus Codex's one-time code or a box to paste Claude's code. The login is kept in an `agent-logins` folder beside config.json, readable only by you, and your existing logins are never touched. Guarded sessions cannot read or write that folder.
- **`glimmervoid limits` names the session's boundary**: it now says whether the session runs in the OS sandbox, in its own Coder workspace, or unconfined, and for a remote session names its environment, workspace and last-seen Coder state.

### Fixed

- **Merged pull requests leave Merge when ready**: a merged or closed pull request could keep showing as first in line for a long time, because it only left the queue after GitHub returned your full pull request list in one poll. It now leaves on the first poll that shows it merged or closed, or as soon as Glimmervoid merges it. The status line now reads "On, merges as soon as it is ready", since a pull request that is not ready never holds up the others: each repository merges one ready pull request per poll, and queue order only decides which goes first when several are ready at once.
- **Reviews dots point to new Ready drafts**: the Reviews dot no longer lights when a draft leaves Ready. Drafts that lit it carry a row dot until clicked, and the Team sub-tab shows the dot while you are on Mine.
- **Open session from Reviews uses your own checkout**: opening a pull request in a session from the Reviews tab used your project's checkout only while a session in it was open. With none open, it fell back to Glimmervoid's private review copy, which a guardrailed session cannot read, so the session failed at once with "can't cd". It now also looks through the folders under your repo roots (Settings) and starts in your own checkout of that repository, the same one from every browser and device. When that folder is a worktree, the session starts from its main checkout instead. When no checkout of that repository is found and the session would run with guardrails, Reviews says to clone it under a repo root instead of starting a session that cannot run.
- **Reviews says what to do with pull requests left for you to review by hand**: when no review is selected, the Reviews pane used to say only "Drafts all handled" and a count. It now lists each pull request from a fork as a link to GitHub, says the agent cannot review it because it cannot check out a fork's branch, and says how many agent reviews are still running. A queue holding only fork pull requests no longer leaves the pane blank.
- **Sessions get their refined task title and name again**: Claude Code sessions never told the title refiner about your prompts, so it never ran. A card kept a "glimmervoid (2)" style name, and its description was Claude Code's own terminal title. Now, after a turn ends, an auto-named session gets a short name and a task title from your recent prompts. Claude Code's own conversation title is read at turn end again too.
- **Adding a session to a folder that is not a git repository**: the + on a sidebar group for such a folder now opens Add Session with the folder filled in and the "Run in this folder, no worktree" choice shown, instead of printing an error in the header. You no longer have to remove the folder and add it again.
- **Header errors no longer push the header onto two rows**: a long error message stays on one line and is cut short, with the full text on hover.
- **Guarded sessions can no longer reach the Coder CLI login**: the Coder CLI config folders (`~/.config/coderv2`, `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/coderv2` and `~/Library/Application Support/coderv2`), which hold its session token, are now blocked from reads and writes in guarded sessions.
- **A Claude Code turn that ends on an API error no longer looks like it is still working**: the session now goes idle when Claude Code reports the error, instead of staying in Working.
- **Commands Glimmervoid feeds input to no longer fail when the program exits early**: such a command, the Coder version check among them, used to fail when the program exited without reading its input.

## [0.40.1] - 2026-10-19

### Fixed

- **Finished sessions are green again**: a session that finished a turn you have not looked at shows as Complete with a green check and a green row highlight, instead of looking the same as an Idle session. Once you look at it, it turns Idle.
- **The browser tab icon only marks what needs you now**: it shows a question mark when a session needs input and a red X when one failed, and otherwise the plain Glimmervoid mark. A finished session you have not opened no longer keeps a mark on the tab; the rail still lists it.
- **The needs-you readout stays visible when all is clear**: the box at the top of the rail keeps its full size, outline and bright ALL CLEAR text when nothing needs you, and shows the `Cmd+J` hint dimmed, so it never shrinks into the background.
- **Session names update in the sidebar**: when a session is renamed, by you or by naming itself after its task, the desktop sidebar shows the new name at once instead of keeping the old one until the session next changes status.
- **Merge celebrations play in the middle of the card**: the bowling, basketball, darts, headshot and royal flush animations stay centered in the celebration card instead of sinking to its bottom edge when the card lists the merged pull request's details.
- **No more unexplained icon in the header**: the status icon next to the trophy, which repeated the rail's needs-you count only on hover, is gone. The rail box still carries the count.

### Changed

- **Session pull requests open in Reviews**: click a session's pull request chip or press Enter or Space to select it in Mine, or Team when it is not in Mine. Pull requests outside the queues still open on GitHub. Cmd/Ctrl-click, Shift-click and middle-click keep opening GitHub.
- **Linked sessions belong to their pull request**: in Reviews > Mine, a pull request and its linked session pills share one hover and selection highlight, with a thin tree connector showing the link, including stacked pull requests.

## [0.40.0] - 2026-10-19

### Added

- **Sessions name themselves after their task**: a session still called by its folder name, such as `glimmervoid (2)`, is renamed once to a short name for what it is working on, from the same Haiku call that refines the task title. A name you chose yourself is never changed. Turning off `taskTitle.refiner.enabled` turns this off too.
- **Agents can ask for reviews and read your GitHub state**: inside a session, `glimmervoid review <pr>` queues a team review of any open pull request, including one outside your teams. It lands as a draft in the Reviews tab, and only you can post it. `glimmervoid reviews` lists what is reviewing, queued and drafted, `glimmervoid prs` lists your open pull requests with their checks and merge state, `glimmervoid link-pr <pr>` links a pull request to the session's card, and `glimmervoid settings list` or `get` reads your settings, with secrets shown only as configured or not. Agents still cannot post, approve or merge anything, and review lanes cannot use any of these commands.

### Fixed

- The startup screen's "server is not answering" hint is now a short centered note under "Reconnecting to server..." instead of one long run-on line that ran edge to edge on a phone.
- **Resuming a dormant session shows it is starting right away**: the card switches to Starting the moment you open it, while Glimmervoid syncs the integration branch and recreates the worktree, instead of sitting on Dormant with no sign anything is happening. A session whose integration branch is missing still drops back to Dormant with its notice.
- **Pasting a screenshot no longer covers the prompt**: pasting or dropping a file on a terminal no longer pops a notice over the bottom of the terminal. The file's path appearing at the prompt is the confirmation, and a failed upload says why in the header status line.

### Changed

- **`Cmd+J` reaches failed sessions**: the jump to the next session that needs you now goes to waiting sessions first, then failed ones, then finished ones, and the rail count's key hint and the phone's Next button count failed sessions too. The prompt badge on a card now reads Needs permission or Needs input in full.
- **Remaining GitHub reads share the rate limit**: opening an issue, mining benchmark cases and looking up your GitHub account now take their turn with the other reads and say at once when GitHub asks to wait, instead of failing or hanging.
- **One place says what needs you**: the top of the rail is now the single count of sessions that need you, larger and highlighted whenever anything is waiting, and it now counts failed sessions too, for example "1 NEEDS YOU, 1 FAILED, 2 FINISHED". The header's "1 session needs input" text is gone; a status glyph in its place carries the same count on hover. `Cmd+J` (Alt+J off a Mac) jumps to the next session that needs you from any tab, not only Focus, and the first-run checklist now mentions it. On a phone, a Next button beside the count does the same. The count is no longer clickable on desktop.
- **Issue pull request status matches My PRs**: an issue's linked pull request now shows the same status as My PRs and updates when that pull request changes, without waiting for the issue to change.
- **Workflows reads the same PR status as My PRs**: workflow rules now see pull requests from the same GitHub data as My PRs, Reviews and the session chips, so a rule fires on the same checks, reviews and labels those views show. Each watched repository still checks its 50 most recently updated open pull requests every five minutes.
- **Clearer session status icons**: each status now shows a familiar icon instead of a font symbol. Dormant is a dashed circle, Starting is a spinner, Working is a pulsing dot, Monitoring is an eye, Needs Input is a question mark, Idle is a pause sign, a session whose agent quit shows a stop square and a crash shows an X. Preparing now shows as Starting, and a finished turn you have not looked at shows as Idle, so the board has fewer statuses to tell apart. The browser tab icon uses the same icons: a question mark when a session needs input and a pause sign when a finished turn is waiting for you.
- **PR status updates right after you act**: when a session pushes or runs a `gh pr` command, or you merge, post a review or add a label from the dashboard, that pull request's status refreshes within seconds instead of on the next poll. A pull request linked to a session now shows its stage on the session chip even when someone else opened it.
- **Session PR chips match My PRs**: a session's PR chip and its My PRs row now always show the same stage, and both update together when a check run finishes.
- **My PRs keeps up with GitHub**: while a dashboard is open, My PRs picks up a finished check run, a new review or a push within about a minute instead of up to five, without pressing Refresh. With no dashboard open it checks every five minutes as before. Refresh now updates My PRs straight from GitHub.
- **Reviews reads the same PR status as My PRs**: team review now takes its review requests, heads, checks and review decisions from the same GitHub data as My PRs and the session chips, so the Reviews tab no longer lags behind them. A new review request starts its review within about five minutes while a dashboard is open, instead of waiting for the next fifteen-minute poll, and Glimmervoid spends fewer GitHub API points doing it.
- **Review actions answer at once when GitHub rate-limits**: posting, approving or discarding a team review now refuses straight away with how long GitHub asks to wait, instead of hanging until the limit clears. Team review's pull request reads now share the same GitHub budget and cache as the rest of the dashboard.
- **View investigation shows what the agent found, not its commands**: the investigation view now lists the agent's own progress notes as it works, such as what the issue turned out to be and what it is checking next, and folds each run of tool calls into one quiet line with a step count. The issue row shows the latest note while the investigation runs.

## [0.39.0] - 2026-10-19

### Added

- **See what a session loaded**: Session setup in a card's session menu lists the Claude Code session's model, plugins with their skills and agents, MCP servers, hooks with how often each ran, and its other skills and agents, read from the session's own transcript. Use it to check, for example, whether a plugin is active in that session.
- **Boop a flying animal**: tap or click an animal as it flies by and it does a happy loop-de-loop, then zooms off the screen three times faster.

- **Coherence session journals**: With Coherence installed, a session's Coherence journal with its latest entries opens on demand from the session, and the session row says when an installed Coherence's hooks are not active; rows in a project without Coherence stay quiet.
- **Coherence from each repository's own install**: Glimmervoid now runs the @posthog/coherence CLI (1.8 or later, below 2) that a repository installed itself, so a Coherence upgrade never waits on a Glimmervoid release. A missing or unsupported install shows its reason on the session row and never blocks a session from starting.
- **Swipe arrow pad on phone (experimental)**: a Settings toggle under Appearance swaps the four arrow keys for one pad you swipe on, sending one arrow per short drag.
- **Release a session from its guardrails**: a Guardrails checkbox in each session's restart menu releases or restores that session's guardrails from the dashboard, phone included. After you confirm, the session restarts with the new setting, which is saved to that session only. A GitHub issue session opened from an unguarded session now starts guarded.

### Changed

- **Desktop session header drops what the session list already shows**: the status mark, elapsed time and linked pull request chips left the desktop card header, since the session list beside it shows all three, so the elapsed time no longer jumps from beside the name to after the task title. Unlinking a pull request moved into Link pull request in the session menu, which now lists the session's linked pull requests. The phone keeps its own header unchanged.
- **Restarts skip sessions left idle for over a day**: a session that has not run a turn in the last 24 hours is no longer started again on every restart. It stays in the dashboard and resumes its conversation when you open it. A session that was working when Glimmervoid stopped is always resumed and told to carry on, however long it has been running.
- **Task leads the desktop session header**: like the phone, a desktop card header now opens with the task title in bright text, with the session name muted beside it; the centered card shows the title at the phone size. A session with no task title keeps its name in bold.
- **Phone and desktop session lists match**: the Board now lists sessions in the order they were added, like the desktop rail, instead of moving sessions that need you to the top, so a new session stays at the bottom. Both use the same project headers (with a session count) and the same Needs you banner, which on the phone now opens the next session that needs you when tapped.
- **Conversation view composer works like a chat app**: the message box is one line that grows as you type, with + to attach a photo or file on the left and Stop and Send on the right, sized for a thumb on the phone. Enter sends on desktop and starts a new line on the phone, where you send with the button. The Terminal | Conversation switch in the card header and the Terminal button are gone; to get the terminal back, turn off Conversation view in the session menu. A prompt the view cannot answer still offers Open terminal, and after you answer it in the terminal, or close the plan, the card returns to the conversation.
- **Photos show in the Conversation view**: in a Claude Code session, a photo you attached through Glimmervoid now shows under your message while Glimmervoid still keeps it, which covers the last 20 attached in that session. Tap one to open it full size. Send with an empty box sends an attached photo on its own, and + stays off while the agent is waiting on a prompt.
- **Session card menu button**: the session card's menu button is now a neutral Session actions button instead of the restart arrow, since the menu holds more than Restart (Conversation view, Guardrails, Coherence journal, Link pull request).
- **Quiet project headers**: on the phone Board and the desktop rail, a project header shows only its name, session count and +, which starts a session straight away.
- **Phone session header leads with the task**: the phone terminal header gives the task title its own line and lets it wrap to three lines instead of cutting it off, with the session name, a state dot and the timer underneath.
- **Session rows lead with the task**: on the phone Board and the desktop rail, a session row now shows its task title (or its name when it has none), and the colored status mark alone carries the state; only Plan ready keeps its word. Under the title sits one line of at most two signals, most urgent first, then +N: a parked merge or Coherence hooks that stopped, then pull requests by how much they need you. Tapping a pull request opens it; tapping a Coherence signal opens that session's journal, which is also in the session card menu as Coherence journal. The Hide status words in sidebar setting is gone, since rows now always work that way.
- **Lanes settings split up**: Each lane (Visions, PostHog, Team review, Workflows and the rest) now has its own Settings page, listed under a Lanes heading, instead of one long page. On a phone each lane is one tap from the list. Old #settings/lanes links open Visions.
- **Lighter dashboard updates**: the terminal and validation libraries now ship as their own files, so after a Glimmervoid update the browser reloads only the dashboard's own code and keeps those cached, and the build no longer prints a chunk size warning.

### Fixed

- **The shell gets its Alt keys back, and the rail explains itself.** With a terminal focused, Alt+1..9, Alt+0, Alt+U and Alt+\\ now reach the shell (readline digit arguments, upcase word, delete spaces) instead of driving the dashboard; Alt+J and Alt+arrows still move between sessions, and the digit shortcuts still work anywhere else. On a Mac, jumping to session 1 to 9 is now Command+Option+digit, so Command+digit keeps switching browser tabs. Rail rows lead with the session name, with the task title on the line below, and a Status key at the foot of the rail explains each status color.
- **The phone key strip is always there.** On a phone the Esc, Tab, arrows, Paste and Attach keys now show whenever a terminal is open, not only while the soft keyboard is up, and a new sticky Ctrl key applies Ctrl to the next key you type (Ctrl, then c sends Ctrl+C). Tapping the session name no longer starts a rename by accident; double-tap it instead. Paste and attach problems show on the line above the keys, and the last toast notices are gone from the dashboard.
- **Terminals keep their place and say what they are doing.** Coming back to the tab no longer resets every terminal: a healthy connection is checked and kept, and a terminal that has to reconnect returns to where you had scrolled. A terminal scrolled up shows a Jump to latest button that lights up when new output arrives, and loading older history no longer flashes the Connecting screen. Dragging a file over a terminal shows a drop zone, uploads report progress, success or failure beside the terminal (also for the phone attach key), keys typed while a terminal reconnects say they are held, a copy from the agent that the browser blocked offers a Copy to clipboard button instead of vanishing, and clipboard and session warnings appear inline or in the header status line instead of as toasts.
- **Buttons look and behave the same everywhere.** Every button shows the same orchid focus ring when reached by keyboard, a disabled button reads as disabled (dimmed, not-allowed cursor) instead of looking clickable, hover highlights no longer stick after a tap on a phone, small controls get a full-size tap area on touch screens, clickable status chips can be reached with Tab and opened with Enter or Space, the review sidebar's primary action uses the outlined accent style, and the health footer and branch sync line keep their numbers and status beside the button instead of in its label. Radar rows now open their report from an Open report button instead of acting as a button that holds other buttons.
- **GitHub, Radar, Bench and Visions say when the server is unreachable, beside the control.** A merge, review action or session open that was waiting when the connection dropped now says so at once instead of waiting out a one or two minute timer, timeouts name how long they waited, Bench buttons are disabled while offline instead of failing after the click, Visions marks its feed as from before the connection dropped, Suppress in Radar asks before it marks the issue in PostHog, and pending text reads the same everywhere (Saving, Refreshing).
- **Settings and plan review say why something failed, beside the control**: a failed update check or update now says why next to the update buttons instead of quietly stopping, and an unrelated error no longer cancels a pending update. Turning a workflow rule on or off shows Saved. A refused plan decision shows its reason in the plan view once instead of also raising popups, a decision sent while offline says the server is not connected, and other session errors appear on that session's card.
- **Hooks, Usage, Trace and the doc editor say why a request failed**: turning a hook on or off, deleting it, or saving it in the editor now shows progress and any error on that hook's row or in the editor, never in a popup, and Save is disabled while a save is in flight. Usage says when a refresh got no answer, and while the dashboard is offline it marks its figures as from before the connection dropped.
