Trace says when a trace did not load or the server is not connected, and the doc editor shows whether a load or save failed because the server was not connected or did not answer.
- **Telegram says when it cannot send**: Settings warns when session notifications are on but the bot token or chat id is blank, shows the last failed send with Telegram's own reason (for example a 401 for a bad token), explains where the token (@BotFather) and chat id come from, and has a Send test message button that reports its result beside itself
- **The dashboard says what to check when it cannot reach the server, and optional tabs are listed in Settings**: after 10 seconds without the server the loading screen or header says to check the terminal running Glimmervoid, a first-run checklist that cannot load says why instead of showing empty steps, the browser no longer asks for notification permission on page load (an Enable desktop notifications button in Settings and the checklist asks instead, and plain-HTTP or blocked pages say why), the PostHog placeholder points at its settings link, and a new Settings > Features page lists Reviews, Usage, Radar, Visions and Bench with whether each is on and a link to its toggle
- **Startup and setup messages say what to do**: a taken port names --port and no longer assumes another Glimmervoid holds it, a taken remote port points at remote.port, a missing config file says GLIMMERVOID_CONFIG named it, a failed Settings save shows the reason instead of pointing at the server log, `glimmervoid doctor` gives the install command for a missing Claude Code or Codex, the setup checklist says when to restart Glimmervoid after installing an agent, and a global npm install shows its PATH notice in the terminal again
- **Looking at a session counts as seen everywhere**: centering a session that needs you in Focus, or opening it on the phone Terminal screen, clears the favicon, closes its desktop notification and stops escalation on every channel, and it stays cleared after a reload; clicking inside a terminal no longer clears it on its own, Alt+J jumps to waiting sessions before finished ones, desktop notifications and Telegram name the session, and blocked desktop notifications are said once in the header
- **Session actions say what went wrong where you clicked**: renaming, restarting, removing and the other session card actions report a lost connection or a refused name on the card itself instead of a pop-up, a rename shows the new name at once and puts you back in the terminal, Add Session and Resume stay open until the server accepts and say why when it does not, a failed project scan gives its reason with a Scan again button, unlinking a pull request asks first, and a streamer mode change the server could not save says so in the header
- **One clear message when the server is out of reach**: requests that wait for a server answer (for example sending review notes or adding a session) now fail with "Not connected to the server. Reconnecting." when the connection is down or drops, and a reply the dashboard cannot read fails its request at once with a reason instead of leaving it to time out silently
- **The review sidebar keeps your notes and stays on the session you are looking at**: a half-typed review note keeps its text, cursor and focus while the agent changes the diff, and a note whose line changed stays listed under Notes, marked as no longer attached, instead of vanishing. The sidebar header names its session and always follows the centered one, so Alt+I merges what you are looking at and says why when there is nothing to merge. Merge and Resolve disable after a click so a double click cannot send twice, Resolve only says sent when it was, the sidebar resize handle works from the keyboard and resets on double-click like the rail's, and Discard worktree, plus Restart Server while sessions are running, confirm in red
- **Auto-resume continues sessions that were working**: after a restart, a session that was mid-turn now gets told to carry on when you sent it a message while it was busy or while a question was open, and when a turn it started on its own, such as after a background task finished, has run a shell command. Before, those sessions came back idle at their prompt with the work stopped.
- **A folder that is not a git repository needs your say-so**: Add Session marks such folders "(not a git repo)" in the project picker and no longer adds one silently. Since a session there cannot get its own worktree and the agent would change your folder directly, Add is refused with a hint to run git init and make a first commit until you tick "Run in this folder, no worktree". The refusal shows inside the dialog, and the path field no longer shows a Windows path on Linux and macOS.
- **A session that fails to start says why**: the card shows the reason it could not start (for example the guardrails refusal, or a session folder that no longer exists) in a red line above the terminal, and it stays there after a reload until you restart the session. A session that ends now keeps its terminal output on screen instead of wiping it, with the Restart hint below it.
- **Rail picks land in the terminal, and removing always asks**: picking a session on the Focus rail (click, Enter, or Alt+1 to 9) now puts the keyboard in its terminal, so the first keys you type reach the agent. Delete or Backspace on a rail entry, and the remove X on the rail and phone board, always open a red confirm first instead of removing a session with no unmerged work at once. Removing the centered session lands on its rail neighbor rather than the first session, and the rail no longer drops keyboard focus when a session changes state.
- **Same status marks on desktop and phone**: the Idle and Initializing marks used shapes the dashboard font does not draw, so each device substituted its own and the phone showed a different icon. Idle is now a ringed circle and Initializing a quartered circle, both drawn by the bundled font everywhere.
- **Matching icons**: the session header's menu, close, trace and debug buttons, and every remove or close X (sidebar rows, project groups, the phone board, pull request chips, the debug overlay), now draw one set of matching line icons, so the menu dots no longer blur into a line and the X looks the same everywhere.
- **Working sessions no longer look idle**: a running session that is thinking or in a long tool call kept its status mark dimmed, which on the Board and the desktop rail read as Idle or Complete now that rows carry no status word. The running mark now stays at full strength.
- **Status colors tell states apart in every theme**: Exited is now a neutral slate in Golgari, Midnight and Compleated, so it no longer shares a hue with Complete or reads as plain text. In PostHog light, Working is a clear green instead of an olive that looked like Idle.
- **Leftover scratch folders are cleaned at startup**: temp folders that an earlier Glimmervoid left in its scratch folder when it stopped mid-run (Coherence readings, checks, the session feed, Factory and lane folders) are now removed once each time the server starts, so a restart or crash no longer leaves them behind for good.
- **Lane sessions keep your recap setting**: lane sessions skip your user-level Claude Code settings, so Claude Code's away recap came back on in them even with `awaySummaryEnabled: false` in your settings. Every Glimmervoid session now carries your `awaySummaryEnabled` choice.
- **Sessions stay out of the main checkout**: a guarded session working in its own worktree could edit, journal or stage files in the repository's main checkout, which left it dirty and stopped every later landing. The guardrails now make the main checkout's tracked files and its index read-only to such a session; ignored folders such as node_modules stay writable, and the session still commits and pushes its own branch, including straight to the integration branch on origin.
- **Glimmervoid's checkouts survive outside prunes**: a git worktree prune run inside a guarded session on the same repository could not see the checkouts in the Glimmervoid folder and dropped them from git. Glimmervoid now locks every checkout it keeps in its own folder, so only Glimmervoid removes them, and starting Glimmervoid (including from a test run in another session) no longer prunes the repository's checkouts when no update is staged.
- **Swipe back in phone Settings**: swiping back from a Settings section now slides straight to the section list, instead of sliding the same section away and then blinking over to the list.
- **No terminal keys over the Conversation view**: on the phone, opening the keyboard in the Conversation view no longer shows the Esc, Tab, arrows and Paste strip meant for the terminal.
- **Messages sent mid-turn show in the Conversation view**: a message you send while the agent is still working now shows as your message in the Conversation view and in Trace, instead of never appearing. A photo in such a message shows as its [Image #N] label only.
- **Conversation view keeps you in the loop**: your message shows the moment you send it, dimmed until the agent receives it, and one Working line with a timer sits above the message box while the agent works. The work between your messages now folds into a single quiet Worked line instead of one line per burst of tool calls, with no step counts. Subagents show again: the Working line and each Worked line count the agents launched, and tapping one lists every agent with whether it is running, done or failed, including background agents. Opening the view starts at the latest message. A file you attach shows above the message box, with a thumbnail for a photo, and the composer no longer says Sent after every message.
- **Dashboard shortcuts work while typing in the Conversation view**: Alt (Cmd on a Mac) shortcuts such as jumping to the next session or switching views now work with the message box focused, as they do in the terminal. While the box holds text, the arrow shortcuts are left to move the cursor.
- **Background task notices are not your messages**: a background task finishing no longer shows as a message from you, full of escaped characters, in the Conversation view.
- **Terminal gap**: the strip below a terminal's last row now matches the terminal background instead of showing black, most visible on phone above the bottom nav.
- **Terminal stays live after a session setting change**: releasing or restoring a session's guardrails, or changing its agent, folder or permissions, no longer leaves its terminal blank until you restart it, on desktop or phone.
- **Phone terminal has Left and Right keys**: the key strip above the phone keyboard now sends left and right arrows, so you can step back to an earlier question in a Claude Code multi-question prompt and change your answer. To make room, the arrows are compact glyph keys and the Image and File keys are one Attach key, whose picker still offers your photos.

### Removed

- **Factory and knowledge graph**: the Factory tab, factory lanes, the `glimmervoid factory`, `dispatch`, `reopen` and `cancel` commands, the experimental `glimmervoid kg` knowledge graph, and their settings are removed.
- **Bundled Coherence**: the bundled Coherence 0.37 package and the Factory floor readings built on it are removed; Coherence now comes from each repository's own install (see Added).

## [0.38.2] - 2026-10-17

### Fixed

- **Sessions with background work keep working after a restart**: a session that had finished its turn but still had a background agent, a monitor or a scheduled wakeup in flight now counts as working, so after a restart auto-resume tells it to continue instead of leaving it idle at the prompt with its background work silently gone.
- **A restart keeps a guarded session's scratchpad**: a guarded session's temporary folder, which holds Claude Code's scratchpad and background task output, is now named after the session and survives restarts. Before, every restart started the session in a fresh folder and deleted the old one. Folders of removed sessions are cleared after a week.
- **Sessions can clear out their own scratch**: a guarded session's temporary folder, where Claude Code keeps its scratchpad, lives under the Glimmervoid folder rather than the system temp folder, so Sane YOLO refused `rm -rf` inside it as a delete outside the project. Recursive deletes inside Glimmervoid's guarded session temp folders are now allowed, the same as inside the system temp folder.
- **Phone board row highlights span the whole row**: the tint on a finished, waiting or failed session now runs under the remove button too, instead of stopping short of it.
- **Session status looks the same on phone and desktop**: the phone board and the desktop session list now share one set of state colors and highlights. Desktop now tints failed sessions; phone now tints a parked merge, flashes once when a session finishes, colors every status, and dims the icon of a running session that has gone quiet, pulsing it on new output.
- **Desktop session list uses the phone row layout**: each session is a full-width row with its status icon pinned on the left, name and task title on top, status, merge state and linked pull requests underneath, elapsed time on the right, and an always-visible remove button. The icon and status no longer jump around as the status word changes or when you hover to remove a session. Phone rows now show linked pull request chips too, and the desktop list shows the task title, elapsed time and "Plan ready".
- **Pull request chips in session rows read as status, not buttons**: a linked pull request shows as a small colored check icon and number in the same type as the status word, red when checks fail or there are conflicts, amber when changes are requested, green when ready to merge, instead of an outlined box with an oversized icon. With status words hidden, the chips sit on the name line, so every row stays one line tall.

## [0.38.1] - 2026-10-17

### Added

- **Any repository can put itself on the factory**: a session can now set its own repository up for factory builds and hand work over without the Factory tab. `glimmervoid factory setup` proves and saves the repository's factory checks and prints them, and `glimmervoid factory queue <file>` queues an intent written as JSON, such as the intent file the spec skill's cut writes, in the same shape as a Factory tab request; its name and project id are dropped and floor allowances are refused. The intent always goes to the session's own repository, and floor allowances stay yours to grant from the Factory tab. A repository joins the factory once its integration branch holds a `coherence.config.json` (holding `{}`), which the session can commit and land with `glimmervoid push`; setup prints that step when it is still missing, and queueing before it is refused with the same step. Both commands answer operator sessions and the sessions they spawn, never factory workers, orchestrators or lanes, and the repository must already be a project in your config.

### Fixed

- **Every session gets a refined title**: a session whose first prompt was short enough to show in full kept that prompt as its card title, because the title model was asked whether the prompt itself still described the session and nearly always said yes. The first refinement now always writes a short title of its own, and later refinements still keep a refined title that remains accurate.

### Changed

- **Livelier flights**: animals now fly in from either side, follow a gentle arc instead of a ruler-straight line, and once in a while take a big swoop. Their trails stay where they were left and fade there, instead of being dragged along behind the animal.
- **Redrawn animals that really move**: the whale, nyan cat, hamster, sheep and giraffe are redrawn and now walk, swim or paddle through six frames instead of toggling between a few poses, Max the hedgehog keeps his look and gains a proper walk, and the outline and glow are drawn into the art, so a crowd of animals no longer runs four stacked filters each.
- **Sharper flying animals**: the animals and their trails now always draw at a whole number of screen pixels per art pixel, so eyes, legs and other one-pixel details no longer shimmer or drop out as they fly. They are slightly larger on a standard display, the Size multiplier steps to the nearest crisp size, and the dashboard stylesheet is about 820 KB smaller.
- **Updates download on their own**: when a check finds an update a clone install can apply, Glimmervoid now downloads and builds it in the background at the lowest CPU priority, without being asked. Once it is ready, the update banner offers Restart, and if you do not restart, the update installs the next time Glimmervoid stops or restarts. Restart still works during a download, which simply starts over afterwards, and a failed download is retried at the next check. Turning off update checks also turns this off.
- **Finished sessions no longer read as needing you**: the attention readout above the phone Board and the desktop rail now counts a session waiting on you separately from one that finished while you were away, for example "1 NEEDS YOU, 2 FINISHED" or just "1 FINISHED". On the phone, a finished session you have not opened yet is now tinted, so it is easy to spot even with compact status labels on.
- **One update button at a time**: the Updates settings page now shows only the action the moment calls for: Check for updates, Update, or Restart once a download is ready. Update and restart and Update without restart are gone, along with the step list for a finished run. A failed run still shows its steps and output, and an npm or npx install shows the command to run.

## [0.38.0] - 2026-10-17

### Added

- **Deeper scrollback after a reload**: a terminal reopened from the server, after a page reload, on the phone, or when you open a card that was hidden, used to start with only its last 500 lines. Scrolling to the top now loads up to 5000 lines once, and the line you were reading stays where it was.

### Removed

- **Settings that never did anything**: the file-only settings `autoRecoverSeconds`, `inputGraceSeconds` and `promptDetectionMs` are gone from the settings reference, since nothing read them. A value already in your `config.json` is ignored, as it always was.
- **Session sleep and card reordering on the server**: the dashboard stopped offering both long ago, so the server no longer accepts them and the health panel drops its always-zero sleeping count.
- **Markdown Radar reports**: Radar has written investigation reports as HTML since August, so the report dialog no longer looks for the old Markdown copies.

### Changed

- **Automatic starts wait for free memory**: auto-resume, the factory, agent spawns and other automatic session starts now wait until Glimmervoid's memory limit has room for another session, instead of piling on until the service stalls in swap. Each start waits for 600 MB of headroom plus 600 MB for every start in the last 30 seconds, set by the file-only `sessionMemoryReserveMB` (0 turns the check off). On Linux the headroom is the tighter of the systemd memory limit and the host's available memory; if it cannot be read, starts go ahead and the log says why. An auto-resumed card shows Waiting for memory while it is held. Sessions you start from the dashboard never wait.
- **Agent spawns refused at once when memory is short**: `glimmervoid spawn` now answers immediately with a low-memory refusal, naming the free memory and what a new session needs, when headroom is under `sessionMemoryReserveMB` after counting recent starts, instead of waiting silently for memory.
- **Memory gate in the health panel**: the debug health panel now shows the memory headroom from the last start check, how long ago it was read, and how many automatic starts are waiting for memory.
- **Ended lane and factory sessions free what they held**: a background session (factory worker or orchestrator, review lane, spawned agent) is now fully torn down once it exits or fails to start, so its terminal ingest tap and its place in the session list are released. A failure alert it raised still reaches you. Before, these stayed behind for the life of the server.
- **Factory workers skip the full test suite**: workers now run only the test files they touched plus the non-test checks (typecheck, lint) before stopping, and can no longer run the whole suite. The full checks still run once in a clean checkout when the worker stops, so each ticket no longer runs the suite twice.
- **One factory check run at a time**: the factory's close-out checks (typecheck, lint, the test suite) now run one at a time across all projects, and each waits for 1.5 GB of memory headroom first, set by the file-only `checkMemoryReserveMB`. A waiting ticket is never failed or charged for the wait, and its project shows Waiting for memory to run checks. Before, several projects could each start a full test run at once, about 1.3 GB apiece.
- **Hidden terminals stop drawing**: a session card you are not looking at, one showing its plan or conversation instead of its terminal, and every card while the dashboard tab is in the background, no longer draws incoming terminal output. With many busy sessions this was about a third of the tab's work. The rail's working pulse still beats for those sessions, and when you open one that produced output while hidden it reloads its current screen with the last 500 lines of scrollback.
- **Lighter usage scan at turn end**: when a session finishes a turn, usage tracking now rescans only that session's own transcript folder instead of every Claude project, removing a burst of about 200 ms of CPU per turn on large histories. The full scan every 5 minutes is unchanged.
- **Quieter background polling**: session trace polling no longer reopens unchanged transcripts every 2 seconds, and the factory no longer runs git every 10 seconds to look for a coherence config while a project's integration branch has not moved.
- **Faster hook relays**: the hook relays for Codex and Grok sessions, the statusline refresh and the session start relay each start about 19 ms sooner, so every supervised turn reports back faster.

### Fixed

- **No more post-turn reports in your repositories**: after each turn Glimmervoid used to save a copy of the post-turn check report under `.glimmervoid/post-turn/` in the session's repository, where git listed it as a new file that an agent could commit. Nothing read that copy, since the dashboard already gets the report live, so it is no longer written. Glimmervoid now deletes the report files earlier versions left behind when it starts. If git already tracks one in a repository, the dashboard shows a notice with the command that stops git tracking it, also printed in the startup output, since untracking a file changes your repository.
- **Guarded session folders left behind after a crash**: when Glimmervoid stopped without a clean shutdown, each guarded session's temp folder, launch home and policy stayed under your Glimmervoid home for good, often tens of MB each. Glimmervoid now removes them on its next start, but only for launches whose owning Glimmervoid process has exited, so a second instance running alongside never touches the sessions of the first. Folders from before this version carry no owner record and are left alone.
- **Idle dashboard tab drawing every frame**: the hidden shutdown spinner animated for the whole life of the tab, so an idle dashboard drew a frame on every screen refresh. It now animates only while shown.
- **Flying animals costing CPU off screen**: a parked animal between flights kept its animations running out of sight. It is now hidden between flights.
- **Visible terminal losing its WebGL context**: terminal WebGL contexts are now released when a card is evicted or removed, ending the "Too many active WebGL contexts" losses that hit the terminal you were looking at while sessions came and went.
- **Crash on a flooding session**: a session writing to its terminal faster than the server-side screen can keep up no longer crashes Glimmervoid at about 50 MB of backlog. The screen sheds the backlog and rebuilds the attach view from recent output instead.
- **Window titles in large redraws**: the window-title status fallback and task titles now work for terminal redraws larger than 8 KB, where every title was silently dropped before.
- **Guarded sessions failing to start under a long temp folder**: on Linux, when Glimmervoid itself ran with a long `TMPDIR` (for example when you start it, or its browser tests, from inside a guarded session), every guarded session failed at launch with "Failed to create bridge sockets", because the guardrails network bridge socket path ran past the 107-byte limit for a unix socket. The guardrails launcher now puts that socket in `/tmp` when the inherited folder is too long, and the agent's own temp folder is unchanged.

## [0.37.0] - 2026-10-16

### Added

- **First-run checklist**: until your first session exists, the Focus view (and the Board on a phone) walks you through three steps: an agent CLI Glimmervoid can find, with the install command and a Rescan button; the folder that holds your git checkouts, with one-click suggestions for home folders such as `~/Projects` that already hold git repos, or a typed path such as `~/src`; and your first session. Only the current step is open, and the list goes away once a session exists.
- **Factory intents finish without you**: a ticket that cannot be done inside its write scopes, blocks three times, or whose worker ends three turns without a change is now parked instead of stopping the run. The orchestrator keeps dispatching the other tickets and never waits on an answer, and when only parked tickets are left the intent ends partial with one notification. The Factory tab shows each finished intent's digest: what merged, what is parked with its exact need, and any flaky tests the factory confirmed. Retry parked in the tab, or `glimmervoid factory retry <intentId>`, reopens only the parked tickets and keeps everything already merged.
- **A blocked test file can widen a ticket's scope**: when a ticket's own change breaks a test file outside its write scopes, the orchestrator can replace it with a ticket that also covers that file, as long as the file matches the project's `widenableScopes` (test folders and `.test` or `.spec` files by default). Any other missing scope leaves the ticket parked rather than worked around.
- **`glimmervoid factory status` and `glimmervoid factory retry`**: any session can print each intent's tickets with their state, attempts, block count, last reason and next action, plus the project's spend today against its budget. `retry` reopens an intent's parked tickets. A factory worker or reviewer session cannot run `retry`.
- **Per-project factory profiles**: `projects[].factoryProfile` sets a project's check commands, its `focusedTestCommand` and its `widenableScopes`, falling back to the global `factory` settings. The first intent queued for a project without a profile runs setup first: Glimmervoid detects the checks from the repository, proves them in a clean checkout, and asks a setup agent only when detection finds nothing that passes. Fields it cannot prove stay unset. The Factory tab shows the profile and has Re-run setup.
- **Guardrails tag on session cards**: a session running with guardrails now shows a GUARDRAILS tag in its card header, next to the worktree tag, so you can tell guarded sessions apart from unguarded ones at a glance.
- **Answer Claude Code prompts from the Conversation view**: a Claude Code permission prompt now shows on the Conversation face as a card with the full request, such as the whole command or the file and its edit, and offers Approve, Always allow and Decline. Always allow appears only when Claude Code suggests a rule, names the rule it adds, and keeps it for this session only. A Claude Code question shows its options as buttons, or as a short form when it asks several questions or allows more than one answer. The terminal prompt stays live, so answering in either place works and the card clears itself. A Claude Code session started before you turned on the Conversation view setting picks this up after a restart. Codex approvals now show commands up to 4000 characters instead of 300 before falling back to the terminal.
- **Out-of-date reviews keep their findings**: when new commits land after an automated review, its verdict, summary and inline comments stay readable in the Reviews detail instead of disappearing, with the commit it reviewed, the commit the pull request is at now, and a Compare changes link to the new commits on GitHub. While a new review runs, and after it finishes or fails, the review before it stays under Earlier review. Out-of-date comments still cannot be posted, since they were written against older code.

### Changed

- **Queue review starts right away**: queueing a review on an out-of-date, failed or posted review now starts it as soon as a review slot is free, instead of waiting up to 15 minutes for the next poll.
- **One name for guardrails**: messages, `glimmervoid doctor` output, Settings descriptions and the folders under `~/.glimmervoid` now all say guardrails, guarded or unguarded, where some still said sandbox or confined. New sessions use `guardrails-temp`, `guardrails-homes` and `guardrails-policies`; once every session started before this update has ended, you can delete the old `sandbox-temp`, `sandbox-homes` and `sandbox-policies` folders. Sandbox still names the sandbox runtime Glimmervoid is built on and the built-in sandboxes of Claude Code and Codex. The guardrails code moved from `server/sandbox/` to `server/guardrails/`, so update any `factory.protectedPaths` entry under `server/sandbox/` to the new paths: `server/guardrails/core/guardrails-roles-core.ts`, `server/guardrails/core/guardrails-policy-core.ts` and `server/guardrails/guardrails-launch.ts`.
- **Clearer error for a missing session folder**: adding a session, or a workspace repository, whose folder is gone now says "No folder at <path>. It may have been moved or renamed; pick it again or enter its current path." instead of a bare "Path does not exist".
- **Sessions that were working keep working after a restart**: when Glimmervoid restarts or crashes while a session is mid-turn, auto-resume now reopens that conversation and tells the agent to continue where it left off, instead of leaving it idle at the prompt. Sessions that were idle or waiting for your answer resume quietly as before. Turning off Auto-resume sessions on startup turns this off too.
- **Factory check failures are short and complete**: when a check fails, the worker's feedback now starts with the failing test titles and the lint and type errors inside its write scopes, and names a file in its temp folder holding the full output. Ledger and decision-log reasons keep up to 3999 characters instead of 1000. Workers run only the test files they touched with the project's focused test command, then the full checks once before finishing.
- **A flaky test no longer costs a factory ticket a block**: a check that fails at the ticket's head is rerun once in the same checkout, only its failing test files when the project has a focused test command. A pass counts, and the test is listed as a confirmed flake in the intent's digest.
- **Conversation view shows tool work as one quiet line**: each stretch of tool work is now a single line such as "Worked 1m 20s, 14 steps" instead of a list you could expand. The full tool detail stays in the Trace tab.
- **Clearer `glimmervoid --help`**: commands are grouped into everyday commands, editor integration, commands for use inside a session, and experimental ones, and `visions uninstall` is now listed.
- **Clearer startup output**: the server now prints a short banner with the version, the dashboard address to open and how to stop it, and on the very first start from a terminal it opens the dashboard in your browser once. Pass `--no-open` to skip that. The first-run telemetry notice is down to two lines, and the line saying remote mode is off is gone.
- **Repository roots accept `~`**: a repository root typed as `~/src` in Settings now saves as the full path under your home folder instead of being refused as a missing directory.
- **+ Session works before you add any repository roots**: instead of a dead-end "No projects found", the dialog offers the same add-folder block as the checklist and fills the project list in place once you add one. Advanced options is now labelled "Run in a folder outside your repository roots".
- **Pull request sessions keep your permission choice**: Open in session on a team review pull request now skips permission prompts, with the Sane YOLO hook, whenever the matching repository's session does, or whenever `skipPermissionsByDefault` is on when no session lists that repository. It used to always start with permission prompts. Prompts are skipped only when the session runs with guardrails, which means it runs Claude Code and the checkout is not under `guardrails.unguardedRepos`; otherwise it still starts with permission prompts. The session never inherits an unguarded flag.
- **Reviews and Issues look like the rest of the dashboard**: a session linked to one of your pull requests in Reviews, Mine now shows as the same row as in the Focus list, with its name and live status, instead of a large outlined button. Buttons on the Reviews and Issues screens use the dashboard's one button style, and secondary controls (Refresh, Filters, Back to issues, View on GitHub) are a quieter outline. The selected pull request row is a soft highlight instead of a colored stripe on its left edge.
- **Dependencies**: `cc-safety-net` 2.6.12, so when Sane YOLO refuses a command it cannot parse, such as `echo ${`, the denial now names the reason (`analysis.strict-unparseable`) instead of reporting that the check failed unexpectedly. Such commands are still refused.

### Fixed

- **My pull requests no longer shows passing pull requests as failing**: a check run that was cancelled and then passed on a rerun used to mark the whole pull request "Checks failing" and start Keep mergeable repairs that had nothing to fix. Each check now counts only its latest run, and pull requests with more than 50 checks are read in full instead of judged from the first 50.
- **A reopened factory ticket no longer hangs its intent**: when the orchestrator reopened a ticket, its new worker could pick up the dead worker's old worktree on an outdated base, and close-out then waited forever without a word. A reopened ticket's worker now always starts from a fresh worktree on the current base, the factory refuses to remove a worktree it did not create, and a worker close-out that cannot find its worktree raises a factory exception instead of stalling.
- **Telegram stops piling up while you work in another session**: an unacknowledged session used to reach your phone after the phone escalation delay (5 minutes by default) even while you were typing in a different session. Now the phone ping waits while you type in any session. If you kept typing for the full delay after a session finished, you had it on the dashboard and moved on, so it never pings. Sessions you left less than the delay after they finished reach the phone once you have been idle for the delay, and pings that come due within a second of each other arrive together in one Telegram message, split only where they would pass Telegram's length limit. Mouse-reported scrolling, focus changes and terminal replies do not count as typing, but scrolling that the terminal turns into arrow keys, as in a full-screen app without mouse support, does.
- **Codex and Grok keep their hooks for projects inside your home folder**: Glimmervoid used to read your own `~/.codex/config.toml` and `~/.claude/settings.json` as if they belonged to the project whenever the project sat somewhere under your home folder. A Codex project with `codexBypassHookTrust` on then lost the bypass as soon as your Codex config held any hook entry, including the trust records Codex writes itself, and a Grok session fell back to title-only status whenever your Claude Code settings had hooks. Your own config folders are no longer treated as project settings, so these sessions behave the same wherever the project lives. Hook settings inside the project still turn the bypass and Grok's hooks off as before.
- **Terminal replies no longer answer a waiting prompt**: when a session is waiting on you and the terminal answers a query from the agent on its own (a cursor position, device attributes or color report), that reply no longer counts as your input. Before, it could end the waiting state or clear the session's notification without you doing anything.

## [0.36.0] - 2026-10-15

### Added

- **Conversation view (experimental)**: turn on Conversation view in Settings > Sessions, then pick Conversation view in the restart menu of any Claude Code or Codex card to try it on that session alone; every other session keeps its terminal, and the choice is remembered per session. A card with it on opens on the Conversation face in Focus, with a Terminal | Conversation switch in its header. Conversation shows the session as messages: your prompts, the agent's replies rendered as Markdown, and each stretch of tool work collapsed into one line you can open for commands and output. A composer sends a message or Stop (Esc) to the agent. When a Codex session asks for approval, a card above the composer offers Approve and Decline; a Claude Code permission prompt or question points you to the terminal to read and answer it. A Codex session connects the next time it starts, so restart it after turning the view on: it then runs through a local Codex app-server on 127.0.0.1 behind a per-session token, its command and file-change approvals can be answered from the card, and its turns appear in the Trace tab too. A sandboxed Codex session, one set to bypass Codex hook trust, or one where the app-server fails to start, keeps the plain terminal. An approval the card cannot show in full (a long command, or a permission scope it cannot render) offers only Decline there, so you read and approve it in the terminal. Prompts and menus the view cannot answer, such as folder trust, point you to the terminal, which stays one click away.
- **Streamer mode**: a mode for screen sharing and live streams. While it is on, tokens and keys (GitHub, Anthropic, OpenAI, PostHog, AWS, Slack, JWTs, bearer values, private key blocks, passwords in URLs and command-line flags, and `NAME=value` lines whose name says secret, token, key or password) are covered by an opaque block in session terminals, live and after a reload or resize, and masked in diffs and session doc previews; your home directory shows as `~`; Radar issue titles and reports, team-review PR titles, logins and avatars are hidden; error toasts show a generic line; and no browser notification fires (Telegram is unchanged). The doc editor opens in Preview, and Edit still shows the raw text. A screen button in the header, Alt+\ (Command+\ on a Mac), and the Streamer mode setting in Settings > Sessions turn it on or off for every open dashboard. While it is on, a STREAMER badge hangs below the header and the Settings tab shows only a line saying settings are hidden, with a Turn off streamer mode button.
- **Session cards show their pull requests**: a session's card header and its Focus roster pill now show the PRs it works on as `#number` chips. A Claude Code session links a PR the moment it runs `gh pr create` or edits one; commenting on someone else's PR links nothing. Any agent's session also links an open PR of yours whose head is the session's checked-out commit, or a descendant of it after GitHub added merge commits, on the next Reviews poll. A session whose commit is already on its base branch links nothing this way. Linking reads only your local git and the PR list the Reviews tab already fetches, so it adds no GitHub requests. Chips show open PRs first, then merged, then closed, newest first within each, and past two the rest collapse into `+N`. With My PRs set up, each chip carries its My PRs stage icon. Hover a chip for the repository and title, and click it to open the PR. Link pull request in a card's menu links one by URL or `owner/repo#number`, and the x on a chip unlinks it for good, so it is not linked again automatically. Links survive a server restart and stay until the session is removed. In Reviews, Mine, each of your PRs that a live session works on gets a button named for that session, which opens it in Focus. When a linked PR's checks fail, changes are requested, threads go unresolved, it conflicts or it merges, the session's chips get a badge until you focus that session.
- **Open a team review pull request in a session**: each pull request in Reviews, Team now has an Open in session button. It creates a session whose worktree starts on the pull request's branch at its latest commit, links the pull request to the session, and pastes a prompt with the pull request's details, description and Glimmervoid's own review. Then it takes you to that session. The session merges back into, and auto-rebases onto, the pull request's branch rather than your main branch. If a session you created already lists the repository, the new session uses that checkout with the same agent. The session always starts with normal permission prompts and guardrails, because it runs the pull request author's code. Otherwise it uses Glimmervoid's own copy of the repository. Clicking it again for a pull request that already has a linked session goes to that session. Pull requests from forks are not supported.
- **`glimmervoid settings` edits `config.json` from the terminal**: `glimmervoid settings list`, `get`, `set`, `unset`, `search` and `schema` read and change the same settings the dashboard shows, including the config-file-only ones, each with `--json` for scripts and agents. `set` and `unset` take a key path such as `posthog.trafficSpikeEnabled` or a setting id, validate the whole file before writing it, and write nothing when the value is wrong, so a running Glimmervoid reloads the file as it does after a hand edit and updates open dashboards, and a stopped one reads it at its next start. The listener port and the keys the Settings view does not know (the remote listener, projects, hooks) are refused with a note to edit `config.json` by hand. `get` and `list` show a Telegram bot token or PostHog API key as configured or not configured and never print the value. `set` and `unset` never take or remove one, because a value typed on the command line lands in shell history: change it in Settings or through `GLIMMERVOID_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` or `GLIMMERVOID_POSTHOG_API_KEY`. `schema` prints every setting with its id, path, type, default, stored and display unit, when it takes effect and its section, and needs neither a server nor a config file. A session with guardrails cannot read the Glimmervoid home, so the command says so there instead of failing on the file.

### Changed

- **The website moved to https://glimmervoid.dev**: it replaces https://johncwaters.github.io/glimmervoid/, and the npm package page now links there.
- **Phone Settings opens with a section list**: search stays at the top; tap a section to open it, then use Back or browser Back to return to the list. Fields and actions fill the available width, with larger touch targets and input text that avoids zooming on focus.

- **Settings search shows one result list**: desktop and phone share a flat list with section breadcrumbs and highlighted matches. Search also matches config keys and option labels, ranks an editable setting ahead of a config.json-only key that matches as well, and `glimmervoid settings search` returns the same results in the same order as the dashboard. A config.json-only result shows its default and when changes apply, and adds its current value and Copy as JSON (for editing config.json or using glimmervoid settings set) when the dashboard receives that value. A key the dashboard does not receive, or receives only as a summary such as `customAgents`, shows no current value rather than a wrong one.
- **Link to a settings search**: open a hash such as `#settings?q=replay` to open Settings with the search filled and run. Typing updates the link, and clearing the search removes its query; existing setting links keep working.

- **Settings is rebuilt around nine sections**: Settings now lists Appearance, Notifications (alert sounds, desktop notifications and the Telegram bot), Sessions, Guardrails and unattended, Updates, Usage, Lanes (one page with a heading per lane: Visions, PostHog, Team review, Workflows, Factory, Benchmarks, Knowledge graph and Change map), Privacy, and Shortcuts and about. Each setting carries two chips, one saying whether it is kept in this browser or on this machine and one saying whether it applies now, to new sessions or after a restart, so descriptions no longer spell that out. A choice of up to three options is a row of buttons, the five Updates status rows are one status block, and the read-only per-project sections are gone. A key that lives in config.json only never appears in a section; search still finds it and shows its key and default.
- **Settings saves each change as you make it**: the Save button is gone. A toggle or a choice saves the moment you change it, and a text, number or list field saves on Enter or when you leave it, once its inline check passes. Turning on a toggle that lets agents act unattended, adding an unguarded repository, or resetting a stored Telegram bot token or PostHog API key asks you to confirm first. A line under each control says Saved or shows why the server refused the value; a refused value goes back to the stored one and the reason stays on screen. Every setting has a Reset button that restores its default. A change saved from another open dashboard shows within a second, except in a field you are typing in.
- **Number settings show their unit, and durations read in seconds, minutes, hours or days**: each number field shows its unit beside it, and titles no longer carry the unit in parentheses. A config.json-only duration found by search shows its default in a readable unit next to the stored value, for example `Default 30 seconds, stored as 30000 ms`. config.json keeps its keys and stored units, so an existing file loads unchanged.
- **Flying animal tuning sits behind Advanced in Appearance**: the Flying animals section is gone. Its toggle is in Appearance, and its six tuning controls (time between flights, flight duration, size and the phone layout switch) sit behind an Advanced disclosure there, the only one left in Settings.
- **Old Settings links still open after the renames**: General and Repositories are now Sessions, Unattended actions is now Guardrails and unattended, Ingest is part of Visions, Flying animals is part of Appearance, and the alert sound and desktop notification settings moved from Appearance to Notifications. A saved `#settings/` link to an old section or setting id still opens that setting in its new place, and links to the old Updates status rows open the Updates status block.
- **PostHog projects is a list**: Settings > Lanes > PostHog takes one project id per line. An empty list monitors every project the key can read, and the single word none monitors no project. config.json stores the same values as before.
- **`glimmervoid settings` shows a duration in its dashboard unit too**: `get`, `list`, `set` and `unset` print a value whose dashboard unit differs from its stored one with the dashboard reading after it, for example `visions.dispatch.quietMs = 30000 (30 seconds)`, and `schema` has Stored unit and Display unit columns. `set` still takes the stored unit, so `set visions.dispatch.quietMs 45000` means 45000 ms. `list`, `search` and `schema` group settings under the new section names.
- **Team review: Discard is now Not mine, on every review row**: the Reviews tab's Discard action is renamed Not mine, and the Discarded list is now Not mine. Use it for pull requests that are not yours to review. You can now use it on a queued or in-review pull request, and on a failed or out-of-date review, not just on a ready draft. Not mine on a pull request that is in review stops that review, so it uses no more tokens. A pull request marked Not mine is not reviewed again, even after new commits, until you click Queue review. Nothing is sent to GitHub.
- **The Factory tab says what the orchestrator is doing**: the orchestrator panel now reads Planning while the orchestrator works, Waiting on workers once it has handed tickets to running workers, and Idle when nothing is running. Before, it showed the orchestrator session's raw status, so it read COMPLETE in the middle of a run while its workers were still busy.
- **Harness runs show in the Factory tab by default**: `factory.mirrorHarnessRuns` now defaults to on, so a factory harness run appears in the Factory tab as a read-only project without a settings change. A config that only sets `factory.enabled` gets the mirror too. The harness turns the mirror off on the instance it boots, so a harness run never mirrors itself. An install first set up on v0.32.0 through v0.35.1 stored the old `false` and keeps the mirror off until the toggle is flipped in Settings > Lanes > Factory.
- **30 settings now live in config.json only**: these settings are no longer in the Settings sections, and you change them by editing config.json. An existing config.json loads unchanged, and every value it stores stays in force. Settings search still finds each one by its key and shows that config.json key, so you know what to edit. The moved keys, by area: General `debugMode`; Terminal, which is no longer a section, `replayBufferKB` and `cursorBlink`; Usage `usage.vendors.codex`, `usage.vendors.grok`, `usage.fetchPricing`, `usage.scanIntervalMinutes` and `usage.costMode`; Change map `changeMap.narrator.timeoutSeconds`; Visions `visions.dispatch.quietMs`, `visions.dispatch.cooldownMs`, `visions.dispatch.activityMaxPerHour`, `visions.dispatch.dispatchTimeoutSeconds` and `visions.intent.threadTtlMs`; Ingest `ingest.sources.fs.enabled`, `ingest.sources.git.enabled`, `ingest.sources.editor.enabled`, `ingest.sources.terminal.enabled`, `ingest.sources.agentLogs.enabled` and `ingest.sources.shellHistory.enabled`; PostHog `posthog.investigationTimeoutSeconds`, `posthog.fixTimeoutSeconds`, `posthog.userEscalationThreshold`, `posthog.trafficSpikeMultiplier`, `posthog.trafficSpikeMinUsers`, `posthog.trafficSpikeCooldownMinutes` and `posthog.trafficSpikeBaselineDays`; Team review `teamReview.org` and `teamReview.team`; Workflows `workflows.maxActionsPerPoll`. You now turn on each machine context ingest source (file, git, editor, terminal, agent logs and shell history) by editing config.json: the Enable machine context ingest toggle in Settings > Lanes > Visions turns on no source by itself. Turning Visions on still adds the file, git and editor sources when config.json does not set them.

### Removed

- **The Calm layout is gone**: the experimental Calm tab, its priority rings and single-action panels, the phone Board's Calm home and bottom sheet, the Focus corner card pointing at the next waiting session, and the Alt+H and Alt+T shortcuts are removed, along with the Calm layout setting. Focus always shows its session rail. A `calmLayout` key left in config.json is ignored.

### Fixed


- **Status icons match the Settings key everywhere**: one waiting session now reads `1 session needs input` in the header, and the header line uses the session state icons and colors from the Status colors key in Settings. Waiting shows the diamond rather than the triangle that marks a failed session, failed shows that triangle, finished-but-unseen shows the hollow diamond, ready shows the idle ring, all-exited shows the done square and all-dormant the hollow circle.
- **rtk rewrites no longer hide from Claude Code**: with the rtk setting on, a Claude Code session is now told when its Bash command ran through rtk, for example `git diff` as `rtk git diff`, so it knows the output is condensed and can rerun the command as `rtk proxy <command>` for the raw output. Before, the condensed output looked like the real command's, which led an agent to blame the wrong cause. Claude Code still asks for permission as before.
- **Factory checks leave room for unix sockets**: a factory check's temp folder path is 21 characters shorter, so a test that opens a unix socket under it stays within the operating system's socket path limit on a standard install instead of failing to bind.
- **A Claude Code session in a new folder shows its trace**: Claude Code creates a folder's transcript directory only after the session starts, so a session in a folder it had never seen, including every new worktree, recorded nothing in the Trace tab. Glimmervoid now picks the transcript up at a later hook of the same session, so the first turn shows once it ends at the latest.
- **A `config.json` edit applies in full while Glimmervoid runs**: removing a setting from `config.json` now takes it back to its default at once; before, the running server kept the old value until a restart. Open dashboards also show an edited setting without a page reload.
- **A factory ticket is no longer blocked by a check that already fails on the base branch**: when a check fails, the factory now reruns it on the commit the ticket started from. If the check fails there the same way, the ticket is not charged for it: it goes on to review and merges. Before, a lint error or broken test already on main cost every ticket its three attempts and a block. The merge note in the decision log names each check that was not charged. A ticket that adds its own failure to a check that was already failing is still sent back to fix it. Test failures are matched by test name and file, and other checks by each error's file and message.
- **A factory block warning clears once the factory recovers**: the project's error banner for a blocked ticket now clears when a later ticket in that project merges. Before, it stayed up until the intent ended. A ticket cancelled after three blocks still keeps its warning.
- **The Factory tab shows every harness run in flight**: two harness batches running at once from different worktrees now each get their own project chip, named by the batch id (for example "Harness 2ca52034: Factory seed"). Before, only the batch that started last showed. A batch that finished within the last day also hid an older batch that was still running.
- **The Factory tab lays out without dead space**: the panels now size to their content. Before, the Intent, Orchestrator and Up next row reserved a fixed tall block that left a large empty gap. The floor had an empty fifth column and worker slots pushed to the bottom. The intent title and criteria scrolled inside cramped boxes. The 21 protected floor checkboxes now sit behind a "Protected floor opt-in" disclosure. It opens on its own when any of them is checked and stays open across updates.
- **The header no longer overlaps itself in a narrow window**: when the tabs and header buttons do not fit on one line, the buttons move to a second row and a long tab list wraps, instead of the tabs running under the buttons. The session status text, for example "1 session dormant", now shortens with an ellipsis when space is tight and shows in full on hover. Before, it was drawn over the Settings tab.
- **A stalled factory run names the ticket that was refused**: when the orchestrator ends its turn with nothing running after a dispatch was refused, the stall warning now names that ticket and why it was refused, for example "wrk-735d1c01d3e14e27 was refused: ticket write scopes exceed the intent write scopes". Before, it said only that nothing was running.
- **Merged PRs show when they merged**: in the Reviews tab, a merged pull request's row now reads "merged 2h ago" instead of when it was opened.
- **Factory and lane sessions no longer stop on Claude Code's external imports prompt**: Claude Code 2.1.299 asks "Allow external CLAUDE.md file imports?" in every new folder when your user-level CLAUDE.md imports other files, and an unattended factory orchestrator sat on that prompt until the run stalled. When Glimmervoid trusts a folder for Claude Code it now also approves its external CLAUDE.md imports, as answering Yes would. A folder trusted earlier gets the approval on its next spawn, while a folder where you answered No keeps that answer. This also applies to a third-party repository opened as a session, so its CLAUDE.md imports load without asking.
- **Merge celebrations name the pull request**: the celebration card now shows the merged PR's title under its repository and number, so it says what landed instead of just a number. When several PRs merge at once, it lists the first three by number and title, then how many more.
- **A failed Merge when ready shows on the card and retries**: when GitHub refuses a queued merge, My PRs now shows a red Merge when ready row with GitHub's error, and the queue status says the last merge failed, so a stuck PR no longer looks queued and on its way. Before, the failure only went to the server log, and that head was never tried again until a new commit or a server restart. Now a failed merge retries an hour later once the PR is ready again, at a new head, or when Merge when ready is turned off and on again.
- **Merging a live session restores only its own uncommitted work**: the git stash list is shared by every worktree of a repo, so when another session or a manual `git stash` added an entry during a merge, Glimmervoid restored that entry into the merging worktree and left its own work behind on the stash list. It now saves under a unique tag, restores that exact entry, and drops only it. Other stash entries stay untouched.
- **A failed auto-rebase retries after an hour**: a failed auto-rebase no longer blocks the same head forever, even across restarts. It retries hourly, so the error clears once the cause is fixed (for example a token that gained repository access). The Auto-rebase row now shows as a warning, not a blocker, when GitHub does not require the update before merging.

## [0.35.1] - 2026-10-14

### Fixed

- **A commit right after the laptop wakes signs instead of failing**: a forwarded ssh agent drops while the laptop sleeps and comes back a few seconds after it wakes. A session commit made in that gap used to fail with "no reachable ssh agent holds the signing key". Glimmervoid now waits up to 30 seconds for a forwarded agent that holds the key before giving up.

## [0.35.0] - 2026-10-14

### Added

- **`glimmervoid limits` for sessions**: a session can run `glimmervoid limits` to see which of `push`, `spawn`, `attention`, `board` and `edit` answer for it, each refusal with its reason. `glimmervoid --help` run inside a session prints the same report under the command list, or one line saying the limits are unknown when Glimmervoid does not answer within two seconds. `push` answers only for a session with a worktree of its own, so a session in a folder that is not a git checkout, or in a multi-repository workspace, is told it has nothing of its own to land. An operator session with guardrails, or a session it spawned, is also told what its guardrails block and the route that still works: credential folders, Glimmervoid's own folder and any path you blocked are off limits, so it flags you with `glimmervoid attention` instead of retrying. A factory orchestrator is still limited to `dispatch`, `reopen` and `cancel`.
- **Claude Code and Codex sessions are told that Glimmervoid supervises them**: every Claude Code and Codex session Glimmervoid starts now gets one line added to its instructions, saying Glimmervoid supervises the session and that `glimmervoid --help` lists the commands it gives. For a session with guardrails the same line adds that a read, write or connection they refuse will not succeed on retry, so it stops retrying what the guardrails block. A session whose permission rules stop it running `glimmervoid --help`, such as a factory worker, the factory orchestrator or a review judge, gets no line at all, the retry sentence included, because the line would point it at a command it cannot run. This is the only always-on text Glimmervoid adds to a session. Your own instructions win: a Claude Code launch that already appends its own system prompt gets no line, and a Codex session gets none when your Codex `config.toml` sets `developer_instructions`, because a per-launch value would replace yours. Grok and custom agents get no line.
- **Restart Server on the phone**: the power button now sits at the right end of the phone Board's top bar, so you can restart the server, or shut it down where that is allowed, without switching to a desktop browser. Its menu items are sized for a finger.

### Changed

- **The parked-merge prompt ends with `glimmervoid push`**: when a session's merge parks, the prompt Glimmervoid pastes into the agent now ends by telling it to run `glimmervoid push` once its branch is rebased and clean. When the push answers no, the agent follows what the push reply tells it to do, and says the work is ready only when that reply leaves it nothing to do. It no longer asks the agent to click Merge in the Review panel, which an agent cannot do.
- **The agent commands work in every session with no setting**: every session Glimmervoid starts is now handed its own agent address, so `glimmervoid push`, `glimmervoid attention`, `glimmervoid board`, `glimmervoid edit` and `glimmervoid spawn` no longer wait for a setting to be switched on. The server still answers no where a command must not run, and says why: only an operator session may spawn a session, only an operator session or a session it spawned may push, a lane session (review, PostHog, visions, change map, task title, benchmark) may read the board but may not raise attention or surface a file with `glimmervoid edit`, since it has no card to deliver a note or a Docs button to you, and a factory orchestrator stays limited to dispatch, reopen and cancel, including after it has been told to stop.
- **Dependencies**: `cc-safety-net` 2.6.10, so Sane YOLO now also blocks recursive deletes of dangerous targets such as your home folder when they are nested in `cmd /c` or `powershell -Command`, and commands that wipe or repartition a disk through `diskutil`, `wipefs`, `parted`, `sgdisk`, `mke2fs` or `newfs`, or by redirecting onto a block device. It does not cover every disk tool: `format`, `diskpart`, `fdisk`, `sfdisk`, `nvme format` and `blkdiscard` still run. Also `express` 5.3, `zod` 4.6.7, and in-range updates to their transitive packages.

### Removed

- **The Agent API setting**: `agentApi.enabled` and its Settings row are gone, because the agent commands are now always available to a session. A `config.json` that still carries the key loads as before and the key is ignored.

## [0.34.1] - 2026-10-13

### Added

- **Paste or drop screenshots into a session from any browser**: pressing Ctrl+V or Cmd+V with an image on the clipboard, or dropping files onto a session's terminal, now uploads them to Glimmervoid and types the saved path into the session, the way the phone's Image and File keys already did. This works when the dashboard is opened on a different machine from the server, such as through a Coder port-forward URL, where the agent cannot read your local clipboard. A paste that carries text still pastes the text.
- **Add or remove repositories in a workspace**: a workspace session's restart menu now has Edit repositories, which lists its current repositories alongside the ones found under your repo roots. Saving restarts the session in the same folder and resumes its conversation: a new repository gets its own worktree on the workspace branch, a removed one has its worktree deleted while its branch keeps any committed work, and the workspace `AGENTS.md` map is rewritten to match unless you edited it. Removing a repository with uncommitted changes is refused until you commit or discard them.

### Changed

- **Update checks read the latest release from npm**: a release-channel check now asks the npm registry for the published version on every install, including a git clone, instead of reading GitHub tags, so the version it offers is always one you can install. The main channel still compares a clone with its upstream branch.

### Fixed

- **Check for updates shows that it is working**: clicking Check for updates under Settings, Updates now disables the button and shows "Checking for updates." beside it until the result arrives, and says when the dashboard is not connected, instead of appearing to do nothing.
- **Guardrails only block what they list**: an ordinary session still ran under the sandbox runtime's allow lists for everything but writes, so tools broke in ways sessions blamed on "the sandbox". File watchers failed with `EMFILE` (`fs.watch`, `@parcel/watcher`, Vite), node-pty failed with `posix_spawnp failed`, the macOS keychain looked empty (so `coder login`, `git-credential-osxkeychain` and other keychain logins failed), `open` and browser-based logins could not launch the browser, and Unix sockets were refused on Linux. Sessions with guardrails can now reach any macOS system service, any Unix socket and any terminal device, send Apple Events, and read and write the keychain. The credential folders and Glimmervoid's own state stay blocked for the session's own processes. The standard level guards against accidents rather than containing a hostile session, because an app it launches through Apple Events runs outside the guardrails. Lanes that read untrusted input (team review, PostHog fixes, the factory, benchmarks) keep their strict limits.

## [0.34.0] - 2026-10-13

### Changed

- **Sessions write anywhere except credentials and Glimmervoid's own state**: an ordinary Claude Code session used to write only its own checkout and the folders you listed, so a session in a linked worktree could not fast-forward main in the primary checkout, and `/commit` promotion failed with `Operation not permitted`. A session can now write anywhere your account can, except credential folders (`~/.ssh`, `~/.config/gh`, `~/.aws`, `~/.azure`, `~/.config/gcloud`, `~/.kube`, `~/.docker`, `~/.gnupg`, `~/.netrc`, `~/.npmrc`, `~/.git-credentials`, `~/Library/Keychains`) and Glimmervoid's home (`~/.glimmervoid`, bar the session's own temp and hook folders), plus `config.json` and Glimmervoid's files beside it when `GLIMMERVOID_CONFIG` or `--config` moves it elsewhere. A blocked entry that is a symlink is blocked at its target too. Your global agent config, git hooks and config, and shell startup files are now writable from a session. Lanes that read untrusted input (team review, PostHog fixes, the factory, benchmarks) keep their narrow write lists.
- **The session sandbox is now called guardrails, and its config keys moved**: it blocks a few things rather than locking a session down, so the settings, the add-session dialog, logs and refusal messages say guardrails. In `config.json`, rename `sandbox` to `guardrails`, `sandbox.unconfinedRepos` to `guardrails.unguardedRepos`, and each project's `unconfined` to `unguarded`; Glimmervoid does not migrate them, and an old key is ignored: until you move the block, your unreadable paths stop being hidden and a repository or project you had opted out runs with guardrails. While an old key remains, Glimmervoid logs a `[guardrails]` warning at start naming it. `sandbox.writablePaths` is gone, since sessions now write anywhere; the new `guardrails.blockedPaths` lists extra folders and files a session cannot write. `sandbox.unreadablePaths` keeps working as `guardrails.unreadablePaths`.

## [0.33.0] - 2026-10-13

### Added

- **Signed commits from sessions on a remote box**: when your git config signs commits with an ssh key held by an agent (`gpg.format=ssh`, `commit.gpgsign=true`, `user.signingkey` set to the public key, as PostHog's devbox setup does), sessions now sign their commits and tags even though the sandbox cannot reach the agent. Git inside the session hands the commit to Glimmervoid, which signs it outside the sandbox with whichever reachable agent holds that key: the server's own `SSH_AUTH_SOCK`, or an agent forwarded by an open `ssh -A` or `coder ssh` connection. The key never enters the sandbox, and only git commit and tag objects can be signed. Keep a forwarded connection open while sessions commit; without one, the commit fails with a message saying so instead of pushing unsigned. Keep-mergeable PR repairs re-sign through the same forwarded agent.

### Removed

- **The factory watch window is gone**: a merged factory ticket used to wait out a watch window (30 minutes by default) while Glimmervoid looked in PostHog for new errors in the files it touched, before the verifier could close its intent. That only ever caught anything when merged code shipped straight to production, and with no PostHog project mapped it was a dead wait. A ticket now counts as verified the moment it merges after its fence, checks and independent review pass, so the verifier closes the intent right away. The `factory.watchWindowMinutes` and `factory.watchProjects` settings are gone (an existing config that still sets them loads fine), the Factory tab has no Watch station, and the factory no longer files breach defects. The harness's `--watch-window` option is gone too. Let any factory intent that is in flight finish before you upgrade: a ticket that merged under the old version is never counted as verified by the new one, so its intent would stay open until you cancel it.

### Fixed

- **`gh` and other Go tools work inside a sandboxed session on macOS**: they failed every HTTPS call with `x509: OSStatus -26276`, because the sandbox blocked the macOS service Go uses to verify certificates. A session's network is already open, so the sandbox now allows that service too. `gcloud`, `terraform` and other Go-built tools are fixed the same way.
- **An idle session no longer retries a conflicting automatic rebase in a loop**: when a session's branch conflicted with its base branch, the automatic rebase could start and abort about twice a second for as long as the session sat idle, swapping the worktree's files each time and breaking any long test or build running in it. A conflicted rebase now waits until the base branch or the session's branch moves, or until a conflict resolution is actually recorded.
- **Factory retry feedback names the failing test on Node 24**: Node 24 prints `node --test` results with the spec reporter, so a worker whose test check failed got the tail of the output as retry feedback, and a test failing outside the ticket's write scopes was never recognized, so the ticket was never blocked as needing write scope. Factory check runs now ask Node for the TAP reporter, so the feedback quotes the failing test again. A check that names its own `--test-reporter` makes Node refuse that; Glimmervoid then reruns it once as you wrote it, and that run's result counts. A Node too old to take that option from `NODE_OPTIONS` gets the same single rerun.

## [0.32.1] - 2026-10-13

### Added

- **Edit an agent's drafts from the dashboard**: with `agentApi.enabled` on, a session can run `glimmervoid edit <path>` to hand you a text file from its checkout, and a Docs button appears on its card. Docs opens the file full screen, on desktop or phone, with an Edit view and a markdown Preview; Save writes it back so the agent reads your text on its next look. If the agent changed the file after you opened it, Save is refused and Reload shows its version, so neither side overwrites the other. Only an existing UTF-8 text file of 256 KiB or less inside the session's checkout can be surfaced, the dashboard can open only files a session surfaced, and the list is dropped when the session ends.

### Fixed

- **Sandboxed sessions take keyboard and mouse input again on macOS**: a session started inside the sandbox could not switch its terminal to raw mode, so moving the mouse over the terminal typed strings like `^[[<35;12;20M` into the prompt and keys only arrived after Enter. Sessions now reach their own terminal from inside the sandbox, and no other: every other terminal on the machine stays out of reach, and factory checks, which run without a terminal, still get none.
- **rtk keeps working inside sandboxed sessions**: with `rtk` on, a sandboxed Claude Code session, factory worker or factory orchestrator can now write rtk's own data folder (`~/.local/share/rtk`, or `rtk` under `XDG_DATA_HOME`), so token savings are recorded again and rtk's "No hook installed" notice no longer repeats on every command. Glimmervoid creates the folder if it is missing and grants it only while its rtk hook is active, so nothing changes with `rtk` off. While `rtk` is on, a sandboxed session can read as well as write everything rtk keeps in that folder, including rtk's record of commands run in every project and its saved command outputs, so leave `rtk` off for work where that matters. If you run your own rtk install with `rtk` off in Glimmervoid, add its data folder under Settings, Session sandbox, writable paths. Verified on Linux; macOS is unverified, where the folder is `~/Library/Application Support/rtk` and also holds rtk's own config.
- **Every review comment carries the same label**: inline review comments were labeled `**[reviewer] SEVERITY**`, only a bare `**SEVERITY**`, or not at all, depending on how the reviewer wrote them. Glimmervoid now labels each inline comment itself with the reviewer and severity of the finding it belongs to, replacing any label the reviewer wrote, and asks the reviewer to label the findings in the review body the same way.

## [0.32.0] - 2026-10-13

### Added

- **`glimmervoid reopen` and `glimmervoid cancel` commands for factory orchestrators**: both take a work id and, like `glimmervoid dispatch`, only a live factory orchestrator may use them. Any other session is refused. `reopen` puts a blocked child of the active intent back to open so it can be dispatched again; `cancel` closes an open or blocked child that has no live worker. Glimmervoid checks the ticket and writes the change to the factory record itself, and a cancelled ticket no longer holds its intent back from verification. The orchestrator's prompt tells it to use them for a blocked child, and to cancel or replace a child that depends on a cancelled one, since that child can never become ready; it still cannot change ticket state through coherence directly.
- **Factory block cap**: a ticket blocked for the third time is cancelled by Glimmervoid itself, the orchestrator is told to re-plan the intent without it, and a project exception records the cancellation without pausing the factory. When three tickets of one intent have been cancelled this way, Glimmervoid stops the intent's remaining workers, cancels its remaining tickets and then the intent, notifies you, and the queue moves on to the next intent; already merged tickets stay on the integration branch with their watches.
- **`glimmervoid push` for sessions**: with `agentApi.enabled` on, a session can run `glimmervoid push` to have Glimmervoid land its commits on the integration branch and push that branch to origin, the same merge the dashboard's merge and continue action runs. The push is fast-forward only and never forced, so the session needs no GitHub credentials of its own. When nothing lands, the reply says why: no commits yet, a conflict with the integration branch and the files involved, or a local branch that has diverged from origin. Only an operator session or a session it spawned can push; factory and lane sessions are refused.
- **Watch a factory harness run from the Factory tab**: with `factory.enabled` and `factory.mirrorHarnessRuns` on, the Factory tab shows the latest harness run as a read-only project while it runs and for a day after it ends. It offers no pause, queue or terminal controls, so the dashboard cannot steer the run.
- **The factory queue form offers protected floor opt-in**: tick any always-protected entry (such as `**/package.json` or `**/biome.json`) when queueing an intent to grant it, and every ticket under that intent inherits the grant.

### Changed

- **A factory ticket that needs files outside its write scopes blocks early and says so**: when a test fails in a file the ticket may not edit, and the test check reports in TAP (the default output of Node 22's test runner, or `--test-reporter=tap`), the worker's retry names that file, and if the same files fail again on the next attempt the ticket blocks with "Needs write scope" instead of using up its remaining attempts. The orchestrator is told to cancel such a ticket and create a replacement whose write scopes cover those files, rather than reopening it
- **Settings search matches partial words**: the search box in Settings now finds a setting as you type, where it needed each word typed in full before. A word matches from its start (`notif` finds notification settings), and from three letters on it also matches inside a longer word (`tree` finds worktree settings). Whole-word matches rank first, then start-of-word matches, then matches inside a word, and a match in a setting's title still outranks one in its keywords, section or description.

- **Claude Code sessions run inside the sandbox runtime by default**: an ordinary Claude Code session, and a session it spawns through the agent API, now starts inside the sandbox runtime on Linux and macOS, where it ran unconfined before. The session can write and commit in its own checkout (its worktree, each member worktree of a workspace, or the folder it was started in when that is not a git checkout), and can write the folders its worktree shares with the main checkout, such as `node_modules`, and the folders listed under `sandbox.writablePaths` (`~/.npm` and `~/.cache` by default, so package installs keep working). It cannot write the repository's git hooks or git config, the main checkout, the rest of the home folder, or its agent's settings, hooks and skills. Network stays open. SSH keys, cloud credential folders, the npm token file, Glimmervoid's own folder and anything listed under `sandbox.unreadablePaths` are unreadable. The GitHub CLI login stays usable, so `gh` and `git push` over HTTPS keep working: Glimmervoid hands the session the GitHub CLI token, because the system keyring is out of reach inside the sandbox. Claude Code and Codex both run inside the session, and files pasted or uploaded to the session stay readable. To run a session outside the sandbox, tick Run outside the sandbox when adding it, or list its repository under Settings, Session sandbox, Unconfined repositories; a session spawned through the agent API is unconfined only when its parent is, and a sandboxed session cannot spawn a child on an agent that runs outside the sandbox.
