Known limits inside the sandbox: a submodule checkout, a checkout with a separate git dir and a checkout whose `.git` is a symlink can edit files but cannot commit; git over SSH, Docker, the system keyring and anything else that needs a unix socket or an SSH key do not work; `/tmp` is not writable (the session's `TMPDIR` is); files the sandbox runtime always protects (shell rc files, `.gitconfig`, `.gitmodules`, `.mcp.json`, `.vscode`, `.idea`) cannot be edited where they exist; and Claude Code cannot save changes to `~/.claude.json`, so Glimmervoid records folder trust for the session's repository itself before the session starts. On Linux a session refuses to start while bubblewrap, socat or ripgrep is missing. Codex, Grok and custom agent sessions stay outside the sandbox, as does every session on Windows. Verified on Linux with a live interactive session; macOS is unverified.

- **Factory workers and orchestrators run inside the sandbox runtime**: a factory worker and a factory orchestrator now start inside the same sandbox runtime as the other factory lanes, so the whole agent process is confined and not only its shell commands. A worker can write only its own worktree and an orchestrator only the ledger checkout; neither can write the shared git folder, the checkout's `.git` pointer or its `.claude` folder, and neither can read credential folders or other agents' homes. Their network is now open, so a worker can fetch packages and look things up, where it could reach only its one session port before; permission rules, the credential-free environment and the required Sane YOLO hook are unchanged. The sandbox runtime leaves empty read-only placeholder files (such as `.mcp.json` or `.bashrc`) at the top of a checkout while a lane runs: close-out and merge ignore them and Glimmervoid removes them when the lane exits. When the checkout has a `.claude` folder, Glimmervoid also adds empty `commands` and `agents` folders inside it for the run, which the sandbox runtime needs in place to start, and leaves them in place as empty folders, which git ignores. A terminal resize now reaches an agent running inside the sandbox runtime. Verified on Linux with live probes; macOS is unverified. On Windows, where the sandbox runtime does not run, the factory refuses to start these lanes, as it did before.

- **PostHog and visions sessions run inside the sandbox runtime**: a PostHog investigation, a PostHog fix and a visions review now start inside the sandbox runtime, where they ran unconfined before. A PostHog investigation can write only its report and its result, so it can no longer change the repository it reads. A PostHog fix can write and commit only in its own worktree; the repository's git hooks and git config are locked, and Glimmervoid still pushes and opens the pull request. A visions review can write only its own work folder. None of them can read credential folders, other agents' homes or Glimmervoid's own folder, and GitHub tokens are removed from their environment. Their network stays open so an investigation can still reach PostHog and research freely. On Linux and macOS these sessions refuse to start when the sandbox cannot start; on Windows they run as before, without it. Linux is verified live; macOS is unverified.

- **Team reviews run inside the sandbox runtime**: a team review now starts inside the sandbox runtime, so the whole review process is confined, including its file tools, its hooks and the Codex runs it starts, where only its shell commands were before. The review can write only its own work folder, reads its checkout and the cached clone, and cannot read credential folders, other agents' homes, Glimmervoid's own folder, or the `.env` files and `.claude` folder at the top of a linked local clone. Codex now gets a home folder of its own for each review, linked to your Codex config and login and removed when the review ends, so a review no longer writes your shared Codex folder. The network is unchanged: the agent APIs, the GitHub API and the review's one session port. Unix sockets are no longer reachable from a review. Every shell command runs without asking, because the sandbox runtime is the boundary. A review refuses to start on Windows and when the sandbox cannot start. Linux is verified live with Claude Code starting Codex inside the review; macOS is unverified.

- **Keep mergeable and workflow sessions run inside the sandbox runtime**: a keep mergeable repair and a workflow session now start inside the sandbox runtime, so the whole agent process is confined and not only its shell commands. The session can write only its own work folder, where it commits the repair locally; it cannot write the checkout's git hooks or git config, the cached clone, or anything else, and cannot read credential folders, other agents' homes or Glimmervoid's own folder. A keep mergeable repair now has open network, so it can install packages to run the checks it is fixing, where it could reach only its agent's API before; it still has no GitHub credentials and Glimmervoid still pushes the verified commit itself. A workflow session keeps a closed network: its agent's API and its one session port. Every shell command now runs without asking, because the sandbox runtime is the boundary. Both refuse to start on Windows and when the sandbox cannot start. Linux is verified live with Claude Code; macOS is unverified.

- **The factory reviewer and verifier and the Codex change map lane run inside the sandbox runtime**: these lanes now start inside the same sandbox runtime as factory checks, on Linux and macOS, so their file tools and hooks are confined along with their shell commands. They can reach only their agent's API and their one session port, cannot read credential folders, other agents' homes or Glimmervoid's own folder, and can write only their own work folder. The reviewer and verifier keep their permission rules and the required guard hook, and still read their prompt file and the verifier's integration checkout. A lane refuses to start, with the reason, when the sandbox cannot start (Linux needs `bwrap`, `socat` and `rg`). On Windows the reviewer and verifier still refuse to start and the Codex lane keeps Codex's own read-only sandbox. Linux is verified live with Claude Code and Codex; macOS is unverified.
- **A sandboxed lane keeps its agent state to itself**: a Claude Code lane inside the sandbox runtime can now write only its own transcript folder, its own session folder and its login file in the Claude home, where it could write every project's transcript folder. A Codex lane gets a home folder of its own for the run, linked to your Codex config and login, and removed when the lane exits; a login Codex refreshes during the run is kept. Linux is verified live; macOS is unverified.
- **The agent command endpoint answers any session that holds its own token**: a session that presents its own token from this machine is now answered whatever `agentApi.enabled` says. Switching the setting off still stops new sessions from being handed an address, but it no longer cuts off a session that already holds one. A factory orchestrator stays limited to dispatch, reopen and cancel, including after it has been told to stop.

### Removed

- **Anti-slop code-quality note on Claude Code sessions**: Glimmervoid no longer appends the anti-slop code-quality note to the system prompt of Claude Code sessions. The `antiSlopPrompt` setting and its Settings row are gone with it; a config file that still sets the key loads as before and the key does nothing. To keep that guidance, put it in your own agent instructions file.
- **REVIEW tag on session pills**: the Focus rail and the phone Board no longer tag a session REVIEW, or tint its pill border, while its worktree holds changes that are not merged yet. The tag tracked unmerged work, not something to acknowledge, so opening the session never cleared it and it read as a stuck alert. PARKED and MERGING still show, and the review panel still lists the unmerged changes.

### Fixed

- **Phone pings no longer report a session that has already moved on**: a Claude Code session whose turn had ended while its background agents kept running could ask for input, carry on working by itself, and still send "needs your input" to Telegram five minutes later. Glimmervoid mistook the resumed work for a compaction starting and kept the notification open. It now waits ten seconds for the compaction to actually begin and otherwise clears the notification, so a ping matches what the session is doing. A notification interrupted by a real idle compaction still escalates as before.
- **Review comments preview their text again when the reviewer heads them with a bare severity**: a review comment whose first line is just a bold severity such as `**MEDIUM**` showed that line as its whole preview in the Reviews tab. The heading is now read as the comment's severity, so the preview shows the comment's first sentence, and posted threads with a bare `**LOW**` heading count as nits.
- **A failed review says why it failed**: a review whose run could not complete showed only "the review run did not complete (VERDICT: FAILED)". It now shows the first gap the reviewer reported, such as a sandbox error that stopped every shell command.
- **A rejected keep mergeable push says what GitHub refused**: when pushing a keep mergeable repair failed, the reason showed only the `git push` command line. It now shows what the remote said, such as a repository rule that requires signed commits.
- **Alt+Up, Alt+Down, Alt+PgUp and Alt+PgDn work again in a remote desktop session**: stepping through the session rail and the view tabs did nothing when the browser ran inside an xrdp session on Linux, because that X server reports the arrow and Page keys under the wrong physical key code and the shortcuts had matched on that code since 0.27.1. They now match those keys by the key the browser reports. Letter and digit shortcuts were unaffected.
- **The YOLO tag no longer looks bigger than the other session tags**: the YOLO and SANE YOLO tag in a session header now uses the same softened border and fill as the worktree, wakeup and prompt tags. It was always the same height, but its solid border made the box read heavier and larger than its neighbors.
- **Team re-reviews no longer treat the base branch's changes as the author's new work**: when a pull request merged its base branch in (GitHub's Update branch, or a stacked PR picking up its parent) or was rebased onto it, the re-review now points the reviewer at only the commits the author added since the earlier review, including any conflict resolutions in merge commits. Reviews done before this update fall back to reviewing the whole pull request on their next re-review. This needs git 2.48 or newer: on an older git a re-review still runs and covers the whole pull request, the server says so once in its log at startup, and `glimmervoid doctor` has a Git section with the installed version and the same hint.
- **Orchestrator ledger landings no longer wedge while a factory record is unlanded**: a record the factory itself declared is accepted by its exact line in an orchestrator landing too, so new work keeps landing instead of being refused for claiming the factory session.
- **Factory workers can no longer change dependency or tool config files your saved protected-path list missed**: every `package.json`, package manager lockfile (npm, yarn, pnpm and bun), package manager config such as `.npmrc`, `.yarnrc.yml`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml` and `.pnpmfile.cjs`, `tsconfig*.json`, Biome config, and Vite, Vitest, Jest, Mocha and Playwright config, at any depth, is now always protected; an intent may change them only when you list those exact entries in its `floorAllowances` when queueing it, and every ticket under that intent inherits the grant.
- **A factory project whose coherence ledger cannot be fully read now shows coherence's own refusal reasons**: when coherence refuses because it could not read its consequence or verification source, the factory floor shows why instead of an unexpected-output error.
- **The factory risk ceiling now covers the intent too**: a ticket is admitted only when both it and its intent are within the configured maximum risk, so a low-risk ticket under a critical intent no longer starts a worker, and queueing an intent above the ceiling is refused before anything is written to the ledger.
- **A blocked factory ticket always releases its worker**: when close-out blocks a ticket, the worker, its slot and its write scope are now released and the exception raised before the ledger lands, so a failed push is retried on the next poll instead of holding the worker until restart, and a reopened ticket starts again from its first attempt.
- **A verifier push failure no longer pauses the factory**: the independent verifier now records its trusted verification and intent close as soon as the ledger commit succeeds locally, so a push that fails afterwards no longer leaves a completed intent untrusted and pauses the project on the next tick as closed without its verifier. When its landing fails before that commit, for example because origin is unreachable, Glimmervoid remembers the verification and close across restarts and trusts them once a later ledger landing commits them.
- **A brief git failure no longer tears down a factory project**: when the factory cannot read a project's integration branch for one poll, it now keeps showing the project with the error instead of treating it as removed and stopping its orchestrator and every working agent.
- **A failed ledger push or watch write after a factory merge no longer strands the ticket**: the ticket is now closed and the worker released either way. A failed watch write raises a project exception naming the merged work that is unwatched, and a close that could not land survives the factory discarding a forbidden orchestrator ledger write and lands with the next ledger landing, so the intent can still be verified and closed.
- **A failed ledger landing survives a restart**: Glimmervoid remembers the exact content of its own unlanded ledger writes across a restart and retries landing them, instead of refusing every later landing as a forged factory write. A copy or altered version of one of those writes is still refused.
- **A failed factory check names the failing tests**: when a check such as `npm test` fails, the retry feedback a worker gets, and the reason recorded when the ticket blocks, now carry each failing test with its error and the pass and fail counts, instead of only the last lines of output, which for a long suite were all passing tests.
- **Factory workers wait for their own commands**: a factory worker now runs every shell command in the foreground, with up to 15 minutes per command, so a long `npm test` finishes and its result is seen before the worker ends its turn, instead of being moved to the background and left running.

## [0.31.0] - 2026-10-12

### Added

- **Intents can carry their tickets**: a `factory-queue-intent` request accepts an optional `tickets` list (id, objective, criteria, dependsOn, writeScopes). The factory writes those tickets into the ledger itself when the intent becomes the active one, with their dependencies and the intent's risk and boundary, and the orchestrator dispatches exactly those tickets. Such an intent can be queued behind an open one, and two of its tickets may write the same path only when one depends on the other. An intent queued without tickets is still decomposed by the orchestrator.

- **The factory verifier sees what changed**: the verifier that closes an intent now receives the diff of everything that landed on the integration branch since work on that intent began, so it can judge criteria about what changed and what stayed the same.

- **Factory decision log**: the factory now keeps a local decision log at `<glimmervoid home>/factory/<projectId>/decisions/<YYYY-MM-DD>.jsonl`, retained for 14 days.

- **Anonymous factory outcomes**: telemetry now includes one `factory_decision` event per factory decision, carrying only the kind of decision, how it came out, the class of failure, its duration and attempt number. It never carries an objective, a reason, check output, a path or a repository name, and it follows the same switches as all other telemetry (`telemetry.enabled`, `GLIMMERVOID_TELEMETRY=0`, `DO_NOT_TRACK=1`). The first-run notice, the Privacy setting and `docs/configuration.md` name it.

- **Factory projects are trusted for Claude Code automatically**: before the factory starts an orchestrator, it marks the project's repository root as trusted in your Claude Code config (`.claude.json`, or the legacy `.config.json` where Claude Code still uses one, honouring `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`), so the orchestrator no longer stops on the folder trust dialog. It sets only that one project's `hasTrustDialogAccepted` key, under the same lock Claude Code uses, and records the grant in the decision log. With telemetry on, the grant is also counted as an anonymous factory outcome that carries no path or folder name. When the config is missing, unreadable or stays locked, nothing is written and the factory raises an exception naming the folder. A project whose git directory is not a plain `.git` folder (a bare repository with worktrees, a separate git dir, a submodule) is left untouched and still needs the dialog accepted once by hand.

- **Merge celebrations wait for you**: a merged pull request no longer pops a celebration on its own. It lands in a trophy button just left of + Session, which turns green with a count of how many are waiting (and sits neutral when nothing is), and clicking the trophy plays them one after another, oldest first, so none go off while you are away. The tray keeps the latest 20. Celebrations now have sound: a whoosh as the banner drops in, a thump on the scene impact, then a short brassy level-up jingle. Each click of the trophy starts from the first of five jingles and steps through them, each one a step higher, so back-to-back celebrations never repeat one, and the confetti starts smaller and grows with each one. The Alert sounds switch mutes them.
- **Issues know their sessions and pull requests**: each issue shows the session you opened for it, with a Go to session button and no duplicate sessions, plus the pull requests linked to it with their state.
- **Shared GitHub teams**: Settings gains a GitHub teams list (org/team) that team review searches across; an existing teamReview org and team keeps working unchanged.
- **Knowledge graph (experimental)**: turn on Settings > Lanes > Knowledge graph to use `glimmervoid kg`, a typed, offline graph of your projects, tasks, notes, references and research questions, kept in one file under the Glimmervoid home. Fields and links are checked as you write them, so a task cannot block itself in a loop and a link only joins the kinds it is meant for. `glimmervoid kg next` lists unblocked tasks by priority, and `glimmervoid kg track` points a task or note at a ticket, decision, blocked report or conjecture in a repo's Coherence ledger, after which `glimmervoid kg delta` shows where your status and the ledger disagree, completed work with no verification, and retracted decisions. It only reads ledgers and never changes a repository, its git index included. Off by default.
- **Connecting indicator**: while the dashboard reconnects after you come back to it, each terminal shows a Connecting label with one of your flying animals running on its trail, the startup loading screen shows one too, and the header dot pulses until the logo arrow flashes green, so a terminal that is catching up no longer looks frozen. Anything you type meanwhile is still delivered once the connection is back.
- **Factory tab**: turn on the Factory setting to get a Factory tab that shows the coherence ledger of each repo whose integration branch holds a coherence.config.json as a live floor: the active intent and its criteria, the queue, working agents, review, watch and shipped work. Glimmervoid ships the `@danilocampos/coherence` ledger CLI (0.37.1) for it. Experimental and not yet reliable end to end: queue an intent from the tab and a master orchestrator session per repo breaks it into tickets and dispatches workers. Glimmervoid admits only tickets inside the intent write scopes and under the risk ceiling, live-worker cap and daily budget, then merges a worker change only after the write-scope fence, the repo checks (run in a clean checkout of the worker commit with an allowlisted environment) and an independent reviewer pass; a change too large to review fails the attempt. Post-merge watch windows check mapped PostHog projects for new error-tracking issues (an unmapped project is recorded as unwatched, and a failed query carries the window forward), and an independent verifier closes each intent, recording a rejection as a ledger defect shown on the floor. Only intents you queue from the tab are ever worked: the orchestrator may add tickets only under its active intent, and any other ledger write it leaves pending is discarded and raised as a factory exception. The clean checkout for the checks is staged with git hooks off and the same allowlisted environment, and a staging failure is redacted before it reaches the worker. Pausing a repo also holds merges and new dispatches, and a daily budget refuses dispatch while usage tracking is off. Settings: `factory.maxRisk`, `factory.maxLiveWorkers`, `factory.checks`, `factory.protectedPaths`, `factory.reviewerModel`, `factory.watchWindowMinutes`, `factory.watchProjects`, `factory.dailyBudgetUsd`, `factory.verifierModel`. Factory sessions never stop on permission prompts, and a stalled or never-started factory session raises a factory exception. Factory sessions run in Claude Code's sandbox, which blocks writes outside the session's own checkout, the temp directory and the git data a worker needs to commit (never the repository's hooks, config, packed refs, replace refs or integration branch) and blocks all outside network, but allows the local Glimmervoid port, so code a worker runs can still reach Glimmervoid itself. A ledger landing refuses, with an integration branch moved outside the factory exception and without pushing, while your local integration branch holds commits its origin branch lacks that the factory did not put there; when a factory merge or ledger landing reached your local integration branch but its push failed, the next landing pushes it first instead of stalling. They also always run with the Sane YOLO hook, and the factory refuses to start them, with a Sane YOLO is unavailable exception, when cc-safety-net is missing. The repo must already be trusted in Claude Code. When a dispatch is refused for a passing reason, such as the live-worker cap, the idle orchestrator is told once the last refused ticket can be dispatched again; Glimmervoid never dispatches it by itself. A daily budget refuses dispatch until usage history has finished its first scan, after which later partial scans keep spend known, and it refuses without that notice while usage history cannot be read. Changing `factory.checks`, `factory.protectedPaths` or `factory.maxRisk` restarts the factory sessions so none keeps the old permissions; other factory settings apply without a restart.
  Factory Git operations suppress repository commands and keep server secrets out, ledger landings reject concurrent forged factory records, and failed breach landings keep their watch until the defect lands.

### Changed

- **Linux sandbox check also needs ripgrep**: the sandbox check at startup, `glimmervoid doctor` and the install notice now look for `rg` (ripgrep) beside `bwrap` and `socat`, because the sandbox runtime refuses to start without it. With any of the three missing, team review, Keep mergeable repairs, workflow sessions and benchmark runs refuse to start and say which binary is missing; install all three with `sudo apt install bubblewrap socat ripgrep`.
- **Tickets, not work orders**: Factory and knowledge graph text now says ticket wherever it said work order or order: the Factory tab, settings descriptions, `glimmervoid kg` help and errors, dispatch refusal reasons and the prompts factory sessions receive. A ticket is still a Coherence work record, so `coherence work` commands and `wrk-` ids are unchanged.
- **Roomier phone header**: on the phone layout the top bar drops the ?, mute and power buttons, leaving the + Session button and the merge celebration tray. Mute moves to a new Alert sounds switch under Settings > Appearance and alerts (it also flips the desktop note button), Restart lives under Settings > Updates, and shortcuts and version under Settings > Shortcuts and about.
- **Issues tab redesign**: Issues now uses the Reviews layout: scope tabs for all, yours, your teams and your projects, search and filters, live session status on every row, and a detail pane that loads the description and opens or jumps to the issue's session.
- **Pull request status icons**: pull request statuses in Reviews (Team and Mine) now use GitHub's own Octicons, one per meaning (merged, draft, conflicts, behind, checks failing or running, changes requested, threads, waiting on a reviewer, your turn, queued, from a fork, out of date and more), instead of five shapes shared across every status. Color still shows urgency. The help page (the ? button) lists every icon under Pull request icons. Octicons are used under the MIT License, shipped as octicons-LICENSE.txt.
- **Issues load on their own**: the Issues tab now polls GitHub in the background for your configured projects' open issues plus issues assigned to you or mentioning your GitHub teams, keeps the last results across restarts, and shows them as soon as the dashboard opens.
- **Reviews Refresh at the bottom**: the Refresh button and its status now sit in a footer at the bottom of the Reviews queue, on both Team and Mine, and a long refresh status wraps instead of being cut off.
- **Review panel on Focus only**: the review panel now shows only on the Focus tab, so Issues, Usage, Radar, Visions, Hooks, Trace, Bench, Factory and Settings get the full width.
- **Quieter review panel**: the review panel says each thing once. In Diff, the Committed section names the branch it merges into only when the headline does not already, and the header drops its combined file and line totals because each section shows its own (Map and Notes keep them while the branch has changed files). An empty worktree shows only the headline in both Diff and Map, and a still-loading one only the headline in Diff, instead of a second line saying the same thing. Each Map repo card drops its file count, which its committed and uncommitted counts already add up to. A disabled Merge no longer adds a line repeating that the session ended or that the base branch needs a manual resync, and a base branch with no upstream, or an unknown sync state, no longer gets a second line restating its label.
- **Merge celebration**: when a pull request merges, the celebration now drops in as a banner near the top of the screen instead of covering the middle, plays a random sports-highlight moment in the banner itself (a bowling STRIKE!, a basketball SWISH!, a darts BULLSEYE!, an FPS HEADSHOT!, or a poker ROYAL FLUSH!) before the title slams in where it played, with confetti, and stays for about seven seconds so you can read which pull request merged.
- **CommitMono everywhere**: the dashboard and its terminals now use the bundled CommitMono font, so text looks the same on every machine instead of depending on which monospace fonts happen to be installed. CommitMono is used under the SIL Open Font License 1.1, shipped beside the font files.
- **Monospace everywhere**: the dashboard no longer falls back to a sans font for text without its own font rule, such as session task descriptions, header buttons, the update and telemetry banners, and the Focus empty state. In the focused card the task description and its timer now match the name's 12px size.
- **Quieter header**: the green connection dot next to the Glimmervoid logo is gone while the dashboard is connected. The dot and its label still appear while connecting, after a disconnect, or once the server shuts down. When the connection comes back after a drop, the arrow before the logo pulses green twice.

### Security

- **Factory sessions cannot write git data**: factory workers, the factory orchestrator and read-only lanes now get no write access to the repository's git folder at all, where a worker could previously write most of it apart from a short list of protected files. Workers no longer commit: when a worker stops, Glimmervoid commits everything it left in its worktree under a `factory: <objective>` subject and then runs the fence, checks and review on that commit, so a stray file a worker leaves behind is judged like any other change. The review always reads changes as text, so a binary file counts toward the review size limit. Glimmervoid refuses to commit a nested git repository, a shared host file such as `.env` that the repository does not track, or an empty placeholder file the sandbox leaves while a command is still running, and tells the worker what to fix instead.
- **Factory collects a worker's files inside the check sandbox**: when a worker stops, the step that reads its worktree into a commit now runs in the same sandbox as the close-out checks, so it can read only what a check can read, plus that worker's worktree wherever worktrees are kept, and write only the commit data. A close-out therefore needs the sandbox to start even when a project configures no checks. A project whose repository sits inside Glimmervoid's own folder, an agent's home folder or a credential folder cannot close out.
- **Glimmervoid keeps its own working folders out of the shared temp folder**: the folders Glimmervoid makes for a close-out check checkout, a reviewer or verifier run, a ledger landing and each session's settings file now live under `scratch` in Glimmervoid's own folder, which agent sessions cannot write, where they used to sit in the system temp folder that every lane can write. A check folder left behind by a crash stays there until it is deleted by hand; session settings folders older than a day are still swept at startup.
- **Sandboxed agent sessions cannot read Glimmervoid's own folder from the shell**: a factory worker, the factory orchestrator and a read-only review lane could run a shell command that read Glimmervoid's config, its secrets file or another session's settings. The shell sandbox for those sessions now hides the whole folder and keeps only its `bin` and `factory/bin` tool folders, plus the session's own working folder when it sits inside, readable. A project whose check commands read a file from Glimmervoid's folder will see that file as missing. A config file kept outside that folder with `--config` or `GLIMMERVOID_CONFIG`, and the files stored beside it, are not hidden by this change.
- **Sessions talk to their own port**: agent sessions now send hooks and agent API calls to a separate loopback listener that serves only those two routes, on a port Glimmervoid picks at startup, instead of the dashboard port. On Linux, where the sandbox enforces it, a sandboxed lane is allowed to reach that one port only, so code it runs can no longer reach the dashboard's control channel; macOS is unverified.
- **Factory checks run sandboxed**: the checks a factory close-out runs on a worker's commit now run inside Anthropic's sandbox runtime (new dependency `@anthropic-ai/sandbox-runtime`) with no network, writes limited to the check checkout and its temp folder, and credential folders, agent homes and Glimmervoid's own state unreadable. A close-out fails instead of running checks when the sandbox cannot start. Linux needs `bwrap`, `socat` and `rg` (ripgrep) on PATH; macOS uses the built-in sandbox and is untested, and a check there that listens on a loopback port fails; Windows cannot run factory checks yet.
- **Judge lanes without a shell run inside the sandbox runtime**: the benchmark judge, the team review thread judge, the change map narrative and the task title lane, when they run on Claude Code, now start inside the same sandbox runtime as factory checks, on Linux and macOS. Such a lane can reach only Anthropic's API and its one session port, cannot read credential folders, other agents' homes or Glimmervoid's own folder, and its file tools can write only its own work folder, Claude Code's own session records and its login file. A lane refuses to start, with the reason, when the sandbox cannot start (Linux needs `bwrap`, `socat` and `rg`), so on a Linux machine missing one of them these lanes stop working until it is installed. The factory reviewer and verifier and every Codex lane keep their current confinement, and on Windows these lanes run as before. Linux is verified live; macOS is unverified.
- **Factory checks share one sandbox policy builder**: the policy a factory close-out check runs under now comes from the same builder every sandboxed session will use. Checks keep no network and writes limited to the check checkout and its temp folder, and can additionally no longer read cloud credential folders (`.aws`, `.azure`, `.config/gcloud`, `.kube`, `.docker`, `.gnupg`), `.netrc`, the npm token file (`.npmrc`), or the Claude config file and the copies of it that sit beside it in the home folder when the check starts. Policy files moved from `factory-check-policies` to `sandbox-policies` in Glimmervoid's own folder.
- **Factory protected paths understand wildcards**: an entry in `factory.protectedPaths` may now use `*` inside one path segment, such as `server/factory-*`, which used to match nothing. An entry the fence cannot match blocks every change rather than silently protecting none: one using `?`, `[]`, `{}`, `!` or a `**` other than a leading `**/`, a `**/` not followed by a single file name with no slash (such as `**/dist/`), or one that resolves to the repo root or outside it. The close-out feedback names each such entry.

### Fixed

- **Reviews no longer carry the last action onto the next pull request**: after Approve or Comment moves you on, the next pull request opens without the previous one's green "Approved on GitHub" line above it. The acted pull request's queue row still shows the outcome. A warning about the action still shows, and the view stays on the acted pull request when it does.

- **Factory reports an orchestrator that stopped with nothing running**: when the orchestrator ends its turn while its project has no live worker, no ticket under watch, no verification in progress and no dispatch waiting on budget, worker cap or a scope overlap, nothing would ever wake it and the factory sat idle without saying so. After two minutes in that state the Factory tab now shows "factory orchestrator ended its turn with nothing running" and the project sends its usual stall notification. A paused project is not reported.
- **A factory intent no longer blocks its own tickets**: the ledger reports two runnable tickets that claim the same path as a write conflict, and an intent's write scopes were recorded there as a claim of its own, so an intent scoped to a single file was blocked together with the first ticket created for that file and the factory then sat idle. Glimmervoid now keeps an intent's write scopes in its own lane state as the bound its tickets must stay inside, and records no claim for the intent in the ledger. An intent write scope is now a relative path, where a folder covers everything under it, or `**` for the whole repository; a scope with any other wildcard, including a trailing `/**`, is refused when the intent is queued. An intent queued before this change keeps its claim in the ledger, so one that was blocked this way stays blocked until it is cancelled and queued again.
- **Factory no longer stops on the orchestrator's own calibration sample**: the coherence stop hook appends a calibration sample to the ledger whenever an orchestrator session that created tickets ends its turn, and the factory refused to land it and raised an exception. The sample that hook appends now lands with the orchestrator's other ledger writes, while a rewritten, deleted or forged one is still refused, as is one that carries a clean or defect verdict or sits in a file named for a different session.
- **Factory dispatch on Linux**: a factory orchestrator on Linux could not start its workers, because its sandbox has a network of its own and `glimmervoid dispatch` got "fetch failed" trying to reach Glimmervoid directly. The session commands (`dispatch`, `spawn`, `attention`, `board`) now go through the sandbox's own local proxy when the direct connection is refused inside the sandbox. Outside the sandbox, and with any proxy that is not the sandbox's own, nothing changes.
- **Usage catch-up on a fresh home**: transcript scans keep their short continuation timer when another scan is running, so catch-up finishes without starting overlapping scans.
- **Factory worker ledger discard**: committed worker ledger files are discarded even when the folder's capitalization differs from `.coherence`, so they do not block close-out or reach the integration branch.
- **Sleeping until chip**: the "sleeping until" chip on a session card now clears on its own once the scheduled wakeup has passed, follows the latest wakeup instead of an earlier one it replaced, and clears when the session stops its loop.
- **Git errors in the review panel**: when git cannot read a session's worktree, the review panel now says Could not read changes and shows git's error instead of an empty diff that looked like no work, the Map names the error for each repo it could not read, and a base branch sync check that fails says so with its error instead of staying on Checking branch sync. Once git can read the worktree again, the panel refreshes on the next change in the worktree or when you select the session again, and every failure is written to the server log.
- **No late ping for a turn you watched finish**: a session that finishes a turn and stays open while you are in it no longer sends a browser notification when you leave and a Telegram message `phoneEscalationMs` later. The dashboard now dismisses the finished turn on the spot, the same as clicking the terminal, when it lands in the session open on the phone Terminal screen, or on desktop in the terminal you are typing in with the window focused. A finished turn in any other session still waits for you and still reaches your phone, and a session whose process exits when its work ends is unchanged and notifies as before.
- **One Telegram ping per Needs input**: a session left on Needs input no longer messages your phone again every 5 minutes. Telegram now gets one message per notification, sent right away when no dashboard is open or after `phoneEscalationMs` when one is, and the next message comes only when the session asks again. Browser notifications still repeat every 5 minutes until you answer. A Telegram message that fails to send is now retried every minute until it is delivered, for up to 6 hours, instead of waiting for the next restart.

- **Dormant session identity**: worktree badges and workspace review controls stay intact when a session becomes dormant.
- **Bench on phones**: the Bench screen now uses phone padding, full width and readable text instead of desktop panel styling.
- **Factory on phones**: the Factory screen now uses phone panel padding instead of desktop spacing.
- Turning telemetry off prevents queued events, shutdown batches and flag checks from being sent after an install-state wait.
- Automatic update checks start or stop when their setting changes, without requiring a server restart.
- Repository roots must be existing directories; an invalid root rejects the settings save with a clear error.
- A notification debounce of zero now disables repeated-category suppression at startup and after settings reloads.
- Calculate cost mode now uses calculated transcript costs on session chips instead of CLI-reported costs.
- Restarted sessions now apply the current RTK compression setting to their hooks.
- **Settings: replay-buffer**: Align replay buffer validation at 64 to 16384 whole KiB; loading legacy subminimum or fractional values normalizes them.
- **Settings: usage-daily-budget**: Describe daily budgets as estimated-spend alert thresholds that do not stop spending.
- **Settings: usage-monthly-budget**: Describe monthly budgets as estimated-spend alert thresholds that do not stop spending.
- **Settings: telegram-session-notifications**: Explain immediate off-dashboard delivery and delayed unacknowledged escalation with a dashboard open.
- **Settings: telegram-bot-token**: Show environment ownership and disable token editing when GLIMMERVOID_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN supplies it.
- **Settings: usage-fetch-pricing**: Describe startup pricing loads and the one-day cache rather than promising continuous daily refresh.
- **Settings: usage-retain-days**: Disclose the current-month transcript retention extension when a monthly budget is configured.
- **Settings: file-record-signals**: Explain full-capture signal recording and the session recreation requirement.
- **Settings: file-trace-enabled**: State that trace capture changes require a server restart.
- **Settings: file-detect-background-agents**: State that background detection changes require session recreation.
- **Settings: file-detect-scheduled-wakeups**: State that scheduled-wakeup configuration requires a server restart and newly created sessions.
- **Settings: file-worktree-auto-rebase**: State that worktree auto-rebase changes require session recreation.
- **Settings: file-worktree-rerere**: State that conflict-resolution reuse changes require a server restart.
- **Settings: file-post-turn-checks**: Disclose project and rule overrides that can select fixes under global Report mode.
- **Settings: file-anti-slop-prompt**: Describe the built-in Claude Code quality note and the session recreation requirement.
- **Settings: file-integration-branch**: Explain that changing the integration branch does not retarget existing sessions.
- **Settings: file-worktree-root**: Explain session recreation and that changing the root does not relocate existing worktrees.
- **Settings: file-worktree-share**: Explain that a new share list applies to future worktree preparation by newly created sessions.
- **Settings: file-port**: State that listener port changes require a server restart.
- **Settings: file-branch-gc-prefixes**: Explain that cleanup prefixes filter remote branches and local worktrees.
- **Settings: usage-scan-interval**: Describe periodic transcript polling and scans triggered by session activity.
- **Settings: calm-layout**: Describe attention prioritization while acknowledging ready and working indicators.
- **Settings: update-installed**: Distinguish the running version from commit and branch information in the current disk checkout.
- **Settings: update-summary**: Give computed update rows separate virtual paths so persisted paths retain one editable control.
- **Settings: session-usage-chips**: Distinguish CLI-reported costs from calculated API list-price estimates.
- **Settings: compact-status**: Explain that hiding phone status words preserves the Plan ready badge.
- **Settings: repository-roots**: Keep case-distinct repository-root spellings instead of discarding entries by unconditional case folding.
- **Settings: sane-yolo**: Disclose executable availability and trusted-hook prerequisites for permission-skip protection.
- **Settings: post-turn-checks-mode**: Describe Report and Fix as defaults subject to project and individual rule overrides.
- **Settings: visions-model**: Document the actual opus alias fallback for a blank model override.
- **Settings: visions-projects**: Display unavailable selected projects so every stored selection can be removed.
- **Settings: visions-auto-fix**: Describe automatic edits to eligible open Markdown buffers, including inactive buffers.
- **Settings: visions-activity-max-per-hour**: Show the effective activity cap and reserved edit slot; require whole-number caps and migrate legacy fractions on load.
- **Settings: posthog-api-key**: Show environment ownership and disable editing when GLIMMERVOID_POSTHOG_API_KEY supplies the credential.
- **Settings: posthog-projects**: Use shared validation and serialization for positive safe integer ids, reject empty comma entries, and support none as an empty list.
- **Settings: posthog-min-users**: Explain investigation exemptions and require whole affected counts, rounding legacy fractional thresholds up on load.
- **Settings: posthog-max-investigations**: Require whole concurrency limits, rounding legacy fractions up on load to preserve effective capacity.
- **Settings: posthog-traffic-multiplier**: Allow fractional traffic spike multipliers supported by the consumer.
- **Settings: visions-enabled**: Describe setup for supported detected editors.
- **Settings: visions-quiet-delay**: Explain immediate save and blank-line review triggers; require whole milliseconds and normalize legacy fractions on load.
- **Settings: visions-cooldown**: Explain per-document cooldown and orientation exemptions; require whole milliseconds and normalize legacy fractions on load.
- **Settings: visions-dispatch-timeout**: Require whole timeout seconds and normalize legacy fractional values on load.
- **Settings: visions-intent-thread-ttl**: Require whole lifetime milliseconds and normalize legacy fractional values on load.
- **Settings: ingest-enabled**: Explain that enabling Visions preserves an explicit ingest opt-out.
- **Settings: posthog-interval**: Allow fractional polling minutes.
- **Settings: posthog-investigation-timeout**: Allow fractional investigation timeout seconds.
- **Settings: posthog-escalation**: Require whole affected-count escalation thresholds and round legacy fractions up on load.
- **Settings: posthog-fix-timeout**: Allow fractional fix timeout seconds within the existing bounds.
- **Settings: posthog-traffic-min-users**: Describe the initial-spike threshold and alert exemptions; require whole affected counts and round legacy fractions up on load.
- **Settings: posthog-traffic-cooldown**: Allow fractional cooldown minutes and explain immediate escalation, recovery and duplicate suppression even at zero.
- **Settings: posthog-traffic-baseline**: Require whole baseline days and truncate legacy fractional windows on load.
- **Settings: factory-enabled**: Describe the current Coherence report polling and status view.
- **Settings: knowledge-graph-enabled**: Clarify that Coherence ledgers stay unchanged while --db controls where graph writes occur.
- **Settings: workflows-max-concurrent-sessions**: Disclose the twenty-entry deferred spawn queue and logged overflow drops.
- **Settings: rtk-compression**: Show unsupported-platform installation refusal and conditional retries after the ten-minute failure cooldown.
- **Workflows stop switch**: turning workflows off aborts running workflow sessions, clears queued spawns, and stops remaining actions in the current poll. Actions that already ran are recorded, so they are not repeated and the stopped ones are not run later.
- **PostHog investigation slots**: issues deferred while investigation slots are full remain eligible when a slot becomes available.
- **PostHog investigation minimum**: an issue first seen below the configured minimum is investigated once its affected count reaches it. Issues recorded before this release are left as they were.
- **Visions model comments**: enabling Visions leaves model dispatch off unless explicitly enabled.
- **Visions project selection**: selecting a later project sharing a repository path now accepts that project's buffers.
- **Visions project reloads**: adding, removing, or relocating a project Visions watches refreshes the live buffer scope. Changes to other projects leave open editor connections alone.
- **Visions hourly budget**: reloading Visions or ingest settings preserves the rolling dispatch budget.
- **PostHog host default**: monitoring uses the displayed US cloud host when no host is saved.
- **Team review rebase suppression**: failed auto-rebases remain suppressed across restarts until the pull request head changes, and the failure note stays on the pull request for as long.
- **Git never waits on a password prompt**: every git command Glimmervoid runs in the background, such as repo refreshes, update checks, pull request lookups and change history, now fails right away when a remote asks for credentials, instead of some of them sitting on a prompt nobody can see until they time out.
- **One loading screen at startup**: opening the dashboard no longer shows the startup loading screen and then a second Connecting screen on each terminal. The loading screen now stays up until your sessions and their terminals are connected, for at most 2.5 seconds, so the dashboard appears ready in one step.
- **Command output no longer cut off in a pipe**: `glimmervoid` subcommands such as `pair`, `visions` and `agent` could stop after 64 KiB when their output went into a pipe, while still reporting success. They now finish writing before exiting.
- **Global options before a command**: `glimmervoid --config <path> <command>` and `--port` written before a command now run that command with those options, instead of starting the server. Anything written after a command's name belongs to that command, so `--help` or `--version` among its arguments no longer prints Glimmervoid's own help or version. `doctor`, `pair` and `visions` still accept the global options after their name.
- **Needs input holds while you answer**: a Claude Code card stays on Needs input while you move through or scroll a question or permission prompt, and changes once the question is answered. Escape at any prompt, or Enter or an option number key at a permission or plan prompt, moves the card on right away, so rejecting a prompt no longer leaves it stuck on Needs input. Moving through the prompt still quiets its notification, so it does not escalate to your phone while you are at it.
- **Hide status words now works on a phone**: with Hide status words in sidebar on, the phone Board rows drop the status word and keep the colored status mark, the same as the desktop sidebar. The setting is saved per browser, so turn it on in the phone's browser too.
- **Phone More menu matches the desktop tabs**: every tab you have turned on now has a screen under More on a phone. Factory and Bench were missing there, and PR reviews is now called Reviews, the same as its desktop tab.
- **Reconnecting status fits small screens**: on a phone or a window under 900px wide, the header shows only the blinking status dot while the connection recovers, instead of a "Reconnecting" label that overlapped the + Session button. Screen readers still announce the status.
- Session titles survive a Glimmervoid restart.
- **Phone terminal reconnect**: the phone keeps its fitted grid claim across reconnects, so a temporarily unavailable size measurement on resume no longer leaves the terminal clipped to a desktop-sized grid. Touch scrolling also keeps working in applications such as Claude Code after reconnecting.
- **Idle compaction settles correctly**: Claude Code cards no longer stay working after compacting while idle, and the dashboard shows when a session is compacting. A notification that was waiting on you still escalates to your phone after an idle compaction.
- **Keep mergeable repairs commit again**: a repair session could not write inside its checkout's `.git`, so every merge failed and the Reviews tab showed "the session committed nothing" even for a conflicted pull request. The session can now commit (`.git/hooks` and `.git/config` stay locked), and when you sign your commits, Glimmervoid signs the repair commits itself before pushing, because the sandbox cannot reach your SSH agent.
- **Keep mergeable heals on its own**: a repair that failed or changed nothing is retried 6 hours later, then after 12 and 24 hours and every 48 hours after that, even when nothing new is pushed. Before, it waited for the base branch to move as GitHub reports it, which only updates on a push to the pull request, so a stuck repair never came back.
- **Keep mergeable hands off large pull requests**: a repair that committed a merge could still fail with "could not read the session commit" when the pull request had earlier commits whose file contents Glimmervoid never downloaded. Repairs also no longer stall on a merge that brings in a changed workflow file from the base branch. A repair still never pushes its own change under `.github`.
- **Keep mergeable reads the repair branch**: a repair that committed its merge on the keep-mergeable branch but left the checkout elsewhere was reported as "the session committed nothing". Glimmervoid now always pushes what is on that branch.
- **Recordings survive a restart**: after you restarted a session or started a new task on it, its signal recording in `~/.glimmervoid/recordings` stopped until Glimmervoid itself restarted, so a missed status such as Needs input could not be traced. The respawned session now keeps recording, starting a fresh recording file for each run so an old run can still be pruned.
- **Claude Haiku 5.5 is priced**: the Usage tab no longer reports "No price for 1 model: claude-haiku-5-5" and counts its cost as zero. Haiku 5.5 is priced at its two rate cards, $0.10 / $0.50 per million tokens up to a 100K-token prompt and $0.50 / $2.50 above, and the bundled price list also gains Opus 5.5, Sonnet 5.5, Fable 5.1 and Mythos 5.1 so offline installs price them correctly instead of borrowing an older model's rate.

## [0.30.0] - 2026-10-08

### Added

- **Ready to merge comes first in My PRs**: pull requests you can merge now sit at the top of the Mine list, and the dashboard throws confetti when one of yours merges, whether you pressed Merge or GitHub merged it later. Reduced-motion setups get the message without the confetti.
- **Coder workspaces stay up while sessions run**: set `coder.appSlug` to an existing `coder_app` slug to keep a Coder workspace from autostopping while Glimmervoid sessions are running. Glimmervoid needs `CODER_AGENT_URL` plus `CODER_AGENT_TOKEN` (or `CODER_AGENT_TOKEN_FILE`) in its environment, which Coder provides when Glimmervoid starts from a workspace terminal or startup script.
- **Answered threads show the conversation**: each answered thread in a review now shows your comment and the author's reply, whether the automatic check thinks it was addressed and why, and a normal-size Resolve button, so you can decide without opening GitHub.
- **Re-reviews stop nitpicking**: when Glimmervoid reviews a pull request again after new commits, it only raises high or critical issues, including earlier findings that are still open. Nits and all medium or low findings are dropped, and a re-review never comes back as approve with nits.
- **Reviews move on after you act**: approving, commenting, discarding or requeueing the open pull request opens the next one that needs you and shows what just happened above it. When nothing is left the pane says you are all caught up, and a reply that carries a warning keeps you on that pull request so you can check GitHub.
- Sane YOLO is on by default for sessions that skip permission prompts in Claude Code, Codex and Grok. It blocks catastrophic commands without asking for approval; guarded sessions show SANE YOLO on their amber permission badge. Turn it off with the Sane YOLO toggle or `saneYolo: false`. Codex requires trusted hooks; rerun `glimmervoid agent setup grok` to enable it for Grok.
- **Task titles follow the conversation**: a session card's title now tracks what the session is working on instead of keeping the name from its first prompt. The card shows your latest substantive prompt as soon as you send it, then Haiku keeps or rewrites the title when the turn ends. Codex, Grok and custom agents keep their live terminal titles when Haiku keeps the title, a title you set yourself always wins, and pasted content no longer shows up in titles. Turn refinement off or pick another model under `taskTitle.refiner` in `config.json`.
- **Switch view tabs from anywhere**: Cmd+Left / Cmd+Right on macOS (Alt+Page Up / Alt+Page Down elsewhere) steps through the Reviews, Issues, Usage and other view tabs without first focusing the tab bar.

### Changed

- **npx updates from npm**: the dashboard update banner for an npx launch now checks npm for new versions and offers `npx glimmervoid@<version>` (`npx --allow-scripts=node-pty glimmervoid@<version>` on Linux).
- **Install from npm**: Glimmervoid is now on the npm registry, so the website and README install it with `npx glimmervoid@latest` (Linux adds `--allow-scripts=node-pty` so node-pty can compile) instead of the long GitHub release tarball command.
- **Website picks your install command**: the website's main install command now detects your operating system and shows the matching command, with macOS, Windows and Linux buttons to switch, and on Linux the build tools it needs. Without JavaScript it still shows the Windows and macOS command with a link to the Linux one.
- **Click the command to copy it**: on the website, clicking the main install command copies it. Selecting part of it to copy by hand still works.
- **Simpler website hero**: the hero keeps only the headline, the install command and the OS switch; the tagline under the headline and the platforms, license and runs-locally line are gone.

### Security

- **Remote access answers only its owner**: when the remote port is reached solely through HTTP-mode `tailscale serve`, set `remote.ownerLogin` to your Tailscale login and the remote listener refuses every request and WebSocket whose `Tailscale-User-Login` header is missing or different, including the pairing page. Funnel and tagged-device traffic carry no login and are refused too. Behind any other proxy a client can send that header itself, so the check proves nothing there. A paired device is still required; leave `ownerLogin` unset to keep the previous behavior. The remote listener logs at startup whether the owner check is on.
- **Glimmervoid secrets stay out of sessions**: `GLIMMERVOID_POSTHOG_API_KEY`, `GLIMMERVOID_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` and `GLIMMERVOID_CLAUDE_OAUTH_TOKEN` are no longer passed to the agent sessions Glimmervoid starts, so an agent can no longer read them from its environment.

### Fixed

- The phone layout's top bar now shows the Glimmervoid name.

## [0.29.1] - 2026-10-07

### Added

- **Glimmervoid overhead in Usage**: the Usage tab's Glimmervoid lanes section is now Glimmervoid overhead. It shows how much of your Claude use in the 5 hour and 7 day plan windows went to sessions Glimmervoid spawned for its own work (PR reviews, keep mergeable, workflows, change map, task titles, visions, benchmarks), with an estimate of how much of each plan limit that is and which lanes it went to. The per-lane table for the selected range stays below it.
- **Your review comments**: a pull request's details and its row hover show how many of your comment threads are resolved. When all are resolved, the row reads Comments resolved, in the same color as Waits on you, and the pull request needs you again unless your approval still stands: a review you posted moves back to Ready.
- **Answered threads**: team review now resolves answered nit threads automatically (once: a nit you reopen on GitHub stays open for you) and judges other answered threads. Each answered thread has a Resolve control whether or not it has been judged, since the judgement is advice, a nit that could not be resolved automatically gets the same Resolve control, and a thread that cannot be judged says why. A temporary GitHub comparison failure is retried later rather than marking threads unjudgeable; a comparison GitHub reports as unrelated history marks the thread unjudgeable until its head or last reply changes.
- **Fork reviews**: pull requests from forks now show under Review by hand with a link to GitHub. Glimmervoid still never runs an automated review on fork code.
- **Compaction reminders**: Claude sessions now receive a one-line reminder of their current task and latest plan after compaction.
- **Calm layout (experimental)**: turn on Settings > General > Calm layout to get a Calm tab where sessions sit on three priority rings instead of a list: sessions waiting on you closest to the center, then failed ones, then every session that has finished its turn and is waiting for your next step, even after you have looked at it; its panel shows the session's latest message in full, the files it changed, and a box for your next instruction. Every other session sits as a named light outside the rings: a hollow light for a session that has no task yet, and a faint one for a working session or one still finishing background tasks. Lights are spread evenly around the field so their names never overlap, and a panel closes with Close, Escape, or a click outside it. The rings shrink to fit the window height. Clicking a light opens one panel with a single action (Approve for a plain Bash command, Approve plan, Resume, or Open review) and a Terminal button that is always there. Approve only appears when the whole command is shown. While the layout is on, Focus drops its session rail, a corner card points at the next session waiting on you, Alt+H returns to Calm, Alt+T opens the current panel's terminal, and replying in a terminal opened from Calm moves you to the next waiting session. Off by default. On a phone the Board becomes the Calm home: the rings and a list of sessions waiting on you sit above your sessions, and tapping one opens the same single-action panel as a bottom sheet. A question from Claude (AskUserQuestion) can be answered from its panel by tapping an option or typing an answer, and a finished session's panel takes your next instruction.

### Changed

- **Quieter review rows**: team review rows in the left list no longer show the automated verdict (such as Nits), the inline comment count, reply counts or since approval. Each row keeps its state word, author and reviewers; the details are in the row's hover and the pull request view.
- **Waiting on author**: the Recently posted section in team review is now Waiting on author, since a posted review usually waits on the author's reply or new commits.
- **Approvals keep your words on top**: an approval posted from Reviews now reads your own text first, then the automated-review note, then any automated findings, and ends with "Approved by hand after checking the automated review.", so nothing above the note is machine-written. A one-click approval posts the note and that line.
- **Approval works the same in every repository**: team review now treats every pull request as if its repository required approval. In a repository without required reviews, your approval still counts after new commits unless someone requests changes, so the pull request no longer comes back for re-review.
- **Review timeouts**: team reviews count only time the machine is awake, so a laptop sleeping overnight no longer times reviews out and uses up their attempts, including a review resumed after Glimmervoid restarts. Review progress drops its countdown once the wall-clock deadline has passed while the review still runs.
- **Failed reviews you already handled**: a failed review moves to Already reviewed when your approval still stands or you reviewed the current commit.
- **Already reviewed section**: the team review section formerly called No review needed is now Already reviewed. It holds pull requests whose current commit you already reviewed, that a teammate approved or requested changes on, or where your earlier approval still stands.
- **Compact discarded reviews**: Discarded rows in the team review list take one line each (pull request and title); select one to see its details and Queue review.
- **One type scale across the dashboard**: every tab now follows the DESIGN.md scale: 11px and 12px captions, 10px and 11px bold uppercase labels with the same tracking, 14px and 16px bold titles and stat numbers, regular or bold weights only. Usage, Hooks, Issues, Benchmarks, Visions, Settings and Reviews no longer mix 8px, 9px, 13px, 18px and semibold text, and the review verdict reads in sentence case.
- **All caught up**: the top of the team review list says All caught up when nothing is ready for you, failed or waiting on a hand review, and counts reviews still running.
- **Review rows say what their glyph means**: each team review row's second line now opens with a word in the glyph's colour (You approved, You commented, Waits on you, Others reviewed, Checks failing, Review failed, From a fork, or a reason such as Queued, Draft), so the state reads without a hover; the separate exception, attention and waiting-for-a-slot labels it replaces are gone.
- **Review list hovers stay put**: the team review list no longer rebuilds every row on each status push when nothing in it changed, so hovering a glyph or row now shows its tooltip instead of the row being replaced before the tooltip appears.
- **Merged reviews leave the list**: a posted review drops out of Waiting on author as soon as its pull request is merged or closed, instead of lingering for seven days.
- **Glyph-led review rows**: each team review row leads with one state glyph (hover the glyph or the row to see what it means): a check when you approved or the pull request is already reviewed, a dash when you commented, a diamond when it waits on you or is out of date, a ring when it is not ready yet or waiting on the author after you requested changes, a cross when a review failed. The Team and Ready chips are gone; a small @ marks a direct request, and only exceptions (Checks failing, Author to fix, Draft, Checks running) get words. What you did on GitHub is read from your latest review or the review you posted, whichever is newer.
- **Quieter review list**: team review rows now use two quiet lines, and pull request refs omit the repo when the list contains only one repo.

### Fixed

- **Resuming the dashboard on a phone**: coming back to Chrome after the phone slept or switched apps no longer leaves terminals frozen until a refresh. After 10 seconds or more in the background the dashboard reconnects at once, after a shorter switch it checks the connection first, and anything you type while it checks is delivered once the connection is confirmed instead of being lost.
- **Doubled alert sound**: desktop notifications are now silent, so a session alert plays only your chosen alert sound instead of that sound plus the system notification sound.
- **Custom alert sounds play like built-in ones**: a custom sound file now plays through the same audio path as Chime, Soft ping and Beep, so it follows the same browser rules and never plays together with the Chime. The Chime plays instead only when the custom file cannot be loaded.
- **Keep mergeable**: failed attempts now show their reason beside the control and on the Mine tab row, retry once when the base branch gains new commits, and retry right away when you turn Keep mergeable on again.
- **Refresh status stays put**: the Refreshing text in the Reviews tabs now sits beside the Refresh button instead of pushing the list down while a refresh runs.
- **Large repository reviews**: team PR reviews of very large repositories no longer fail with "could not stage a checkout: timed out".
- **Benchmark runs near login expiry**: when a refresh does not extend your Claude Code login, a benchmark run now waits for the login token to expire and Claude Code to renew it, instead of failing every cell in roughly the last hour of each token's lifetime. Cancel still stops the wait. A cell fails only if the renewed token is still too short, and its error still suggests `GLIMMERVOID_CLAUDE_OAUTH_TOKEN`.

## [0.29.0] - 2026-10-04

### Added

- **Workflows**: write rules under `workflows.rules` in `config.json` that act on pull request events in the repositories you name. Each rule has one trigger (`opened`, `checks-failed`, `review-requested`, `approved`, `commented` or `merged`), optional filters (`mine` for your own pull requests, `teamReviewRequested` for a review requested from your Team review team, `authors`, `labels` and `baseBranches`), and one or more actions: `notify` sends you a notification, `label` adds a label, `comment` posts a comment, and `spawn` starts a sandboxed agent session on a local copy of the pull request from your prompt template (`{{repo}}`, `{{number}}`, `{{title}}`, `{{url}}`, `{{author}}`, `{{base}}`, `{{head}}`, `{{trigger}}`) with the same limits as Keep mergeable, except that nothing it does is pushed. A `spawn` action never runs on a pull request from a fork unless its rule also filters on `authors` or `mine`, at most 2 sessions run at once and up to 20 more wait for a free one; editing rules leaves running and waiting sessions alone. Rules start disabled and do nothing until you set `enabled: true`. Glimmervoid checks each repository every 5 minutes and remembers what it saw in `workflows-state.json`, so the first check of a repository only takes stock and fires nothing, and an event fires once even across restarts. At most 20 actions run per check. Settings > Workflows turns workflows or a single rule on and off and changes the session and action limits. A `workflows` block that does not validate runs no rule at all and the server log says why.
- **Keep mergeable**: toggle Keep mergeable in Reviews > Mine to have a sandboxed agent fix merge conflicts or failing checks on a local copy of that PR. The agent has no network access to GitHub, can write files only inside its own working folder, skips the repository's git hooks, and pushes nothing itself. Glimmervoid checks its commit and pushes the verified repair commit to the PR branch, so the PR itself becomes mergeable, but only when that branch still points at the commit the repair started from, so it never overwrites other work; if the branch moved while the repair ran, the push is rejected, logged and not retried. Pull requests from forks are never repaired or pushed, and a PR that closed or merged meanwhile is not pushed. Its shell cannot read your credential and tool config folders (`~/.ssh`, `~/.aws`, `~/.config`, `~/.claude`, `~/.codex`, `~/.glimmervoid` and similar). A repair that edits anything under `.github/` or adds a credential-like file (`.env`, `*.pem`, `*.key`, `id_rsa`, `.npmrc`, `.netrc`, `credentials*`) is not pushed, and the server log names each commit and branch it pushes. A repair stops at the Keep mergeable deadline (30 minutes unless you change it in Settings), or when you turn the flag off or the PR leaves the list. The flag survives restarts, each head commit is attempted once (a repair cut short by turning the flag off or by a restart is tried again), a repair commit Glimmervoid pushed is never repaired again, so a check still failing after a repair waits for your next push, saved flags are removed when the PR leaves the list, and work folders a crash left behind are cleaned up when My PRs starts. Behind branches still use the existing global auto-rebase setting.
- **Merge when ready**: toggle Merge when ready on one of your pull requests in Reviews > Mine to add it to Glimmervoid's own merge queue; the status beside the toggle shows its place in line among the queued pull requests of the same repository. On each poll Glimmervoid merges at most one pull request per repository, the first queued one that is ready to merge, with your default merge method and only at the head it just polled. A queued pull request that GitHub reports as behind its base is updated even when global auto-rebase is off, and a pull request updated during a poll waits for the next poll before it is merged. The queue never merges a commit Keep mergeable pushed: that pull request shows Repair pushed, waiting for you, and is merged by the queue again only after you push a new commit, or you can merge it yourself with Merge. A failed merge is logged and the pull request stays queued, but that head is not tried again until a new commit lands. Merged or closed pull requests leave the queue, and the queue survives restarts. Drafts, pull requests with conflicts or failing checks, and those already in GitHub's merge queue are skipped without holding up other repositories.
- **Settings switches**: Team review gains Keep mergeable and Merge when ready on/off switches (default on) and a Keep mergeable deadline (5 to 120 minutes, default 30); turning a feature off hides its toggle and pauses it while keeping each PR's saved choice. Workflows gains a master switch (default on), limits for concurrent agent sessions (1 to 5, default 2) and actions per poll (1 to 50, default 20), and an on/off switch per rule; editing a rule's other fields still happens in config.json.
- **Review request priorities**: Reviews > Team includes pull requests requested directly from you, labels Direct and Team requests, and puts blocking reviews first with chips for author changes, failing checks, running checks and drafts.
- **Stacked pull requests**: Reviews > Mine groups dependent pull requests beneath their parent, including across readiness sections, so you can follow a stack in order.
- **Reviews retry and refresh**: when GitHub cannot be reached, Reviews > Mine and Team now retry after 10 seconds, 30 seconds and 90 seconds before falling back to the regular schedule, and the notice counts down to the next try. A Refresh button beside the tabs checks GitHub right away, except while GitHub's rate limit asks to wait. Team now shows when it cannot reach GitHub instead of failing silently.
- **Benchmarks (opt-in)**: compare agent setups on frozen cases. Turn on Settings > Benchmarks to show the Bench tab, then put a suite (a `suite.json` naming its arms, subject prompt and judge) under `benchmarks/<suite>/` in the Glimmervoid home. Mine lists merged pull requests with review comments as candidates; move the ones you approve into the suite's `cases` folder, then choose Run. Each case runs once per arm in a fresh detached checkout of the reviewed commit, an LLM judge matches the findings against the review comments, and the tab shows paired recall per case, overall and per human and bot reviewers. One run at a time, and Cancel stops it. Arms authenticate with your Claude Code login, read from the keychain or `~/.claude/.credentials.json`, or with `GLIMMERVOID_CLAUDE_OAUTH_TOKEN`; the token is passed only to benchmark sessions and never written to a run record.
- **Approve without comments**: a ready team review now has an Approve button beside Comment and Approve and comment. It posts only the approval, stating it was made by hand, with no review body and no inline comments.
- **What a PR is for, at a glance**: a team review now opens with About this PR, stating the pull request's goal and what it changes, above the verdict. The goal also shows when you hover the PR in the list. Reviews made before this update show it after Queue review.
- **Usage on session cards is now opt-in**: turn on Show usage on session cards under Settings > Appearance to see each card's token count and estimated API list-price cost. It is off by default.
- **Status color reference**: Settings > Shortcuts now shows a Status colors legend above the version line, listing every session status with its glyph, color and a one-line meaning, Monitoring included. The colors follow the active theme.
- **Sandbox check on Linux**: Claude Code's sandbox needs `bwrap` (package bubblewrap) and `socat` on Linux. Glimmervoid now checks for both once at start and logs one warning naming the affected features when either is missing. Team review, Keep mergeable repairs, workflow `spawn` sessions and benchmark runs then refuse to start, saying which binary is missing and how to install it (for example `sudo apt install bubblewrap socat`), in the team review status, the Keep mergeable log line, the workflows log line or the benchmark run error, instead of failing inside Claude Code. Team review starts no reviews while the check fails, so no pull request uses up its review attempts or gets an error draft, and interrupted reviews wait to resume. `glimmervoid doctor` gains a Sandbox section with found or MISSING per binary plus the install hint, and `npm install` prints the same hint on Linux when either is missing. Restart Glimmervoid after installing them.
- **Merge from My PRs**: a pull request of yours that is ready to merge now has a Merge button under Merge readiness on the Reviews > Mine tab. It asks you to confirm, then merges on GitHub with your default merge method for that repository, only while the pull request is still at the head you saw. Glimmervoid does not delete the branch, though GitHub still does when the repository deletes head branches on merge. Where the base branch requires a merge queue or auto-merge, the status beside the button says the pull request was queued or set to auto-merge rather than claiming a merge. Drafts, pull requests in a merge queue, with conflicts or with failing checks are never merged; the reason shows beside the disabled button.

- **Compact sidebar status**: hide status words under Appearance and alerts to keep only colored status glyphs in the sidebar. The setting shows example sidebar sessions that update as you flip it. Monitoring now has a distinct color across themes, sidebar, phone screens and session cards.

- **Session tasks**: each card shows what the session is working on beside its name, taken from the agent or the first prompt, with no extra model calls. Click that space to write your own task; clear it to go back to the automatic one. Rename still changes the session name itself.

- **Anonymous telemetry**: Glimmervoid now sends anonymous usage and error data so it can be improved: the version, platform, when the server starts, a daily count of live sessions, when sessions start and end with their agent kind, how they exited and running time, server and dashboard errors as their type, error code and scrubbed stack frames (a crash is sent on the next start), and, while the usage lane runs, each session's token counts, models (a model name not in the pricing table is sent as unknown) and estimated cost, all under a random install id with no person profile. It never sends error messages, paths, repository or branch names, prompts, model output or terminal output. A notice prints once in the terminal on first start, and the dashboard shows a one-time notice until you dismiss it. Turn it off under Settings > Privacy, or set `GLIMMERVOID_TELEMETRY=0` or `DO_NOT_TRACK=1`; it is always off when `CI=true`. Every event also says whether it came from a development install (a git clone, a source checkout or a non-default `GLIMMERVOID_HOME`), so maintainers can filter their own use out, and a published version carries its PostHog release id so errors link to the release they came from and a fixed error that comes back reopens. At start, once a day and when telemetry is turned on, Glimmervoid checks the `telemetry-enabled` flag so the maintainers can stop all sending if ever needed, by setting that flag's rollout to 0% (deactivating or deleting the flag does not stop installs); a stopped install stays stopped across restarts until a check returns the flag enabled, and a failed check or a missing flag changes nothing. The full event list is in docs/configuration.md under Telemetry.
